## Part III: From SPAP to Physical Structure

### The Core Logical Sequence

**Summary of Theorem 15 (Horizon Constant K₀ = 3)** [§5.2.2]
The theorem separates three statements:

1. Any realization of the SPAP sub-dynamics satisfying (O1)–(O3) and (FC) must distinguish the eight joint response contexts $(\phi,p_{stored},c_{phase})\in\{0,1\}^3$, hence requires at least $K_0=3$ bits of visited-context capacity.
2. A three-bit architecture with state $(\phi,p_{stored},c_{phase})$ and a two-phase injective transition realizes the stated SPAP reflex rule and attains that conditional context bound.
3. Within the same three-bit register size, the persistence predictor $\hat X_{t+1}:=X_t$ achieves asymptotic accuracy strictly greater than $1/2$ on $\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{basic}}(\gamma)$.

Hence three bits is the least visited-context log-capacity for the stated SPAP encoding in the (O1)–(O3), (FC) realization class. The persistence construction establishes that this register size also admits one basic super-chance mode; it does not establish a three-bit lower bound for every above-chance predictor.

**Operational Conditions for SPAP Encodability (O1–O3 and FC)** [§5.2.2]
The following conditions define the restricted realization class used for the horizon-register theorem. Section 5 motivates them operationally but does not derive (O1) for every qualifying predictive loop:

| Condition | Requirement | Physical Meaning |
|:----------|:------------|:-----------------|
| **(O1)** Injective Stepping | One-cycle transition over visited configurations is injective | Logical reversibility on sub-dynamics; maximal computational efficiency |
| **(O2)** Two-Phase Control | Loop has two distinct phases distinguished by control bit $c_{phase}$ | Prevents race conditions between generate/store and reflex/update |
| **(O3)** Non-Destructive Retention | State $\phi$ and prediction $p_{stored}$ coexist across phase boundary | Preserves information essential for the logical paradox |

Conditions (O1)–(O3) alone do not force eight contexts. The additional hypothesis (FC) requires the joint readout map onto $\{0,1\}^3$ to be surjective. Under (O1)–(O3) and (FC), the Commit Snapshot therefore requires at least eight visited operational configurations, hence $K_0\ge3$.

**Summary of Remark 5.2.2a (Conditional robustness of $K_0\ge3$):** The lower bound is preserved under state relabeling and reordering of micro-operations only while (O1)–(O3) and (FC) remain satisfied. The load-bearing counting hypothesis is surjectivity of the joint response map onto the eight elements of $\{0,1\}^3$; injectivity alone does not supply that surjectivity.

**Summary of Definition (Environment Class $\mathcal{E}_{basic}(\gamma)$)** [§5.2.2]
The environment class for minimal predictive success certification:
$$
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{basic}}(\gamma) = \Big\{\text{stationary ergodic binary processes} : \liminf_{T\to\infty} \frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}\mathbb{E}[s_t] \ge \tfrac{1}{2}+\gamma\Big\}
$$
where $s_t=\Pr[X_{t+1}=X_t\mid\mathcal H_t]$ is the one-step persistence statistic. For fixed $0<\gamma\le1/2$, an i.i.d. Bernoulli($p$) process belongs exactly when $p^2+(1-p)^2\ge1/2+\gamma$, and the usual uniform-stationary ergodic symmetric two-state Markov chain with persistence $q$ belongs exactly when $1/2+\gamma\le q<1$. At $q=1$ that uniform-stationary chain is not ergodic; degenerate absorbing stationary realizations, if admitted, require a separate branch.

**Summary of Theorem 23 (Minimal MPU Hilbert Space Dimension)** [§7.2.1]
On Theorem 15's realization class satisfying (O1)–(O3) and (FC), the least visited-context count is $N_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\min}=2^{K_0}=8$. If those contexts are represented by mutually perfectly distinguishable quantum states, their supports are pairwise orthogonal and nonzero, so choosing one vector from each support gives $d_0\ge N_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\min}$, hence:
$$
d_0 \geq N_{\mathrm{vis}}^{\min}=8.
$$
Equality $d_0=8$ follows only on Theorem Z.2's branch carrying an admissible eight-state comparator in the same optimization class, response-preserving quotientability of every extra supported direction, strict operational-cost increase with maintained support, and minimality.

**Summary of Corollary 23c.1 (Predictive Fisher Metric and Cramér-Rao Gate)** [§7.1]
On a retained finite-dimensional MPU response-state chart, the SLD quantum Fisher tensor defines
$$
h=\frac14F^Q.
$$
It is positive semidefinite before quotienting response-null directions, positive definite on the locally identifiable quotient, and restricts to the Fubini-Study metric on the pure-state branch of Theorem 23c.

**Summary of Theorem 31 (Conditional Registered-Reset Ledger)** [App J, §7.4]
For a registered pre-reset record $P$, retained record $R$, law $q$, and isothermal reset implementation,
$$
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}
=H_q(P\mid R)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}
\ge H_q(P\mid R),
\qquad
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}\ge0.
$$
A positive physical floor requires $H_q(P\mid R)\ge h_{\min}>0$. Equality with $H_q(P\mid R)$ requires zero dissipative overhead; equality at $\ln2$ additionally requires a conditionally uniform binary record. Lemma J.1 establishes the required noninjectivity only on its declared reachable-domain and prescribed-ready-output architecture.

**Imported result (Lemma J.1): Conditional Logical Merging in the Declared Binary-Ancilla Architecture**

If the reachable pre-reset set has more than two elements and the accessible output fixes the ancilla to one ready value, the accessible map is noninjective. SPAP alone does not establish either hypothesis.

**Imported result (Theorem J.1): Conditional Cost of the Declared Binary-Ancilla Reset Architecture**

For pre-reset record $P$, retained record $R$, and their registered law $q$, the declared isothermal reset branch satisfies
$$
\frac{\langle Q_{\mathrm{bath}}\rangle}{k_BT}
=H_q(P\mid R)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}},
\qquad
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}\ge0.
$$

**Summary of Theorem J.4a (Finite Registered-Reset Ledger)**
For a finite set $\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ref}}(D)$ of registered resets satisfying the cyclic, degenerate-register hypotheses,
$$
\mathcal X_{\mathrm{reset}}(D)
=
\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ref}}(D)}H_{q_e}(P_e\mid R_e)
+
\sum_{e\in\mathcal E_{\mathrm{ref}}(D)}\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}(e).
$$
The separate structural label ledger is $N_{\mathrm{ref}}\ln2$. It equals a physical reset term only when the relevant conditional-uniformity and saturation hypotheses are verified. For sequential resets, $R_e$ contains every earlier record retained unchanged.

Corollary J.4a.3 restricts the finite reset ledger to physically assigned guard-reset events. If $m$ such events satisfy
$$
H_{q_e}(P_e\mid R_e)\ge h_{\min}>0,
$$
then
$$
\mathcal X_{\mathrm{guard}}(D)\ge mh_{\min}.
$$
Semantic or predetermined status alone assigns no reset term; any physical reset satisfying the corollary is counted, while reversible or unreset retention contributes no event.

### Conditional Dependency Cascade to the Horizon Branch

The relevant conclusions are available only on a conjunction of independent certificates:
$$
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\text{reachable binary fixed-ready reset}
\xrightarrow{\text{Prop. E.2a}}
C(\mathcal E_N)\le\ln d_0-\ln2,\\
\mathcal E_N=(1-p)\Psi+pT_\sigma,\quad p>0
\xrightarrow{\text{Lemma E.1}}
f_{\mathrm{RID}}<1
\xrightarrow{\text{Theorem E.2}}
C_{\max}<\ln d_0,\\
\text{accepted boundary-density, same-family saturation, and area-law branch}
\xrightarrow{\text{E.3 and E.6}}
S_{\mathrm{rel}}
=
\frac{k_Bc^3\mathcal A}{4G_{\mathrm{op}}\hbar}
+o(\mathcal A).
\end{gathered}
}
$$

| Step | Result | Premise | Reference |
|------|--------|---------|-----------|
| 1 | $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ | Reachable binary fixed-ready support | Theorem J.1 |
| 2 | $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}\ge H_q(P\mid R)$ | Registered physical reset and conditional Landauer hypotheses | Theorem 31 |
| 3 | $C(\mathcal E_N)\le\ln d_0-\ln2$ | Completed binary reset support | Proposition E.2a |
| 4 | $f_{\mathrm{RID}}<1$ and $C_{\max}<\ln d_0$ | Nonzero full-state refresh component | Lemma E.1; Theorem E.2 |
| 5 | Operational area coefficient | Boundary density, same-family capacity saturation, entropy saturation, and additive ledger | Theorems E.3 and E.6 |
| 6 | Measured Newton constant | External calibration $G_{\mathrm{op}}=G$ | Calibration entry |

SPAP's diagonal obstruction, the reset architecture, refresh contractivity, boundary scaling, operational area coefficient, and measured-$G$ calibration are distinct nodes in this dependency graph.

### Branch Separation: Uncertainty, Reset Cost, and Signaling

The three constraints below have different antecedents and are not proved to share a single SPAP mechanism:

| Constraint | Required branch | Conclusion |
|:-----------|:----------------|:-----------|
| Robertson uncertainty | Complementary sharp ledgers, a Hilbert representation, noncommutation, and the Robertson domain hypotheses | $\Delta A\,\Delta B\ge\frac12\lvert\langle[\hat A,\hat B]\rangle\rvert$ |
| Conditional reset cost | Registered physical reset, retained side-information ledger, and conditional Landauer hypotheses | $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}\ge H_q(P\mid R)$ |
| Finite-window signaling tradeoff | The specified binary context experiment, spacelike/AQFT branch, and finite-window regularity gate | $\Delta(\Phi)\Pi(\Phi)<1$ |

**Uncertainty path**
$$
\text{registered complementarity}
\to
[\hat A,\hat B]\ne0
\xrightarrow{\text{Robertson}}
\Delta A\,\Delta B\ge\frac12|\langle[\hat A,\hat B]\rangle|.
$$

**Reset path**
$$
\text{registered reachable reset with retained side information}
\xrightarrow{\text{conditional Landauer}}
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}\ge H_q(P\mid R).
$$

**Signaling path**
$$
\text{finite-window AQFT experiment}
\xrightarrow{\text{Theorems 39a and 14.2.6.1}}
\Delta(\Phi)\Pi(\Phi)<1.
$$

SPAP motivates the first comparison through a separately registered Logical-Indeterminacy/complementarity bridge, but it neither supplies the reset architecture nor the finite-window signaling premises. Refresh contractivity is an additional sequential-channel property and is not the source of Hilbert noncommutation.

**Summary of Theorem 14.2.6.1 (Branch-Disjunctive Signaling Uncertainty Principle, §14.2.6.1).** For a binary late-context experiment carrying either the sealed marginal-invariance certificate or the separately registered positive-overlap certificate, the normalized worst-case decoding determinism $\Delta(\Phi)$ and spacelike-reach indicator $\Pi(\Phi)$ satisfy
$$
\Delta(\Phi)\cdot\Pi(\Phi)<1.
$$
On the sealed causal branch the pre-lightcone transcript laws are equal and $\Delta=0$. On the regular overlap alternative, $\Delta\le1-\Omega_n<1$; a response-active marginal shift on this alternative falsifies the sealed branch rather than coexisting with it. Thus the shared conclusion is only exclusion of the zero-error spacelike corner. Corollary 14.2.6.1e keeps dimensionful rate-latency claims outside the theorem stack unless a separate cost ledger is supplied.

**Structural and Physical Ledger Distinction (Section J.5)**
The binary log-cardinality $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ is a structural reference and is not itself a physical reset ledger; SPAP alone supplies no universal positive heat floor.
- **Landauer bound**: A registered reset obeys $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}=H_q(P\mid R)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}$, with $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}\ge0$.
- **Sagawa--Ueda feedback ledgers**: Information-acquisition and feedback terms apply to their specified measurement-and-control protocols.

No universal physical $\varepsilon_0$ term may be added to those ledgers without a branch theorem identifying a distinct physical event and proving that its cost has not already been counted in $H_q(P\mid R)$, dissipation, acquisition, or feedback.

**Source-Exhaustive Power and Stress-Energy Ledger (Section 7.4.7)**
The expectation $\langle\hat H\rangle$ is an energy, not a baseline power. A physical implementation must declare a common time parameter and a non-overlapping first-law ledger, for example
$$
P_{\mathrm{in}}
=\frac{dE_{\mathrm{stored}}}{dt}+P_{\mathrm{work}}+P_{\mathrm{heat}}+P_{\mathrm{flux}},
$$
with every term assigned once. A registered reset ensemble contributes
$$
P_{\mathrm{reset}}
\ge r_{\mathrm{reset}}k_BT\,\bar H_q(P\mid R)
$$
only when its heat has not already been included in another ledger entry. Converting retained energy or power to stress-energy additionally requires a localized density, support, equation of state or response tensor, covariance/conservation check, and coarse-graining map. Neither an `Evolve` label nor addition of $\langle\hat H\rangle$ to reset power proves a total stress-energy source.

**Model-Form Scope and Robustness Tests (Section 6.7)**
Robustness is theorem-specific. The exact exponential Law of Prediction and its power-law images require their multiplicative-residual and logarithmic-map hypotheses; replacing the exponential by a logistic or Hill form changes those exact conclusions. A result is stable on an alternative function class only after that class, its common domain, uniform error or derivative bounds, preserved asymptotics, and propagated conclusion interval are stated and proved. SPAP, PCE, and the registered-reset inequality retain their own premises independently of any phenomenological interpolation.

**Summary of Theorem Z.1 (Structural Binary Active-Kernel Selection)**
On the PCE-reference branch, a registered binary quotient supplies $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$. The active subsystem must carry the sharp retained match/mismatch record, giving $a\ge2$; the entropy-capacity condition supplies $\ln a\ge\varepsilon_0$; and the stated quotientability and no-surplus comparison excludes nonminimal or response-null enlargement. On this conjunction,
$$
a=2.
$$
A physical reset and its heat ledger are independent implementation statements governed by Theorem 31.

**Immediate consequence (Inactive Subspace Dimension $b = 6$)**
$$
b = d_0 - a = 8 - 2 = 6
$$

**Summary of Corollary Z.2 (Minimal-Branch Determination of $d_0 = 8$)**
Perfect Hilbert distinguishability gives $d_0\ge8$. Equality holds only on Theorem Z.2's branch carrying an admissible eight-state comparator in the same optimization class, response-preserving quotientability of every surplus supported direction, strictly increasing maintained-support cost among response- and performance-equivalent candidates, and minimality. The Radon-Hurwitz/octet correspondence is a secondary algebraic coherence check.

**Summary of Theorem Z.5 (QFI Interface Mode Count M = 24)**
At the PCE-Attractor, the number of quantum Fisher information interface modes is:
$$
M = 2ab = 2 \cdot 2 \cdot 6 = 24
$$
These 24 modes mediate information exchange between active and inactive subspaces, with flat spectrum λ = 1.

**Summary of Theorem Z.6 (Capacity Saturation)**
At the PCE-Attractor, predictive information gain equals operational alphabet capacity:
$$
\mathcal{I}(u^*) = M \ln(1 + \lambda u^*) = \ln d_0
$$

**Summary of Theorem Z.7 (Bare Rate Coordinate on the Cap-Active LAN/QFI-Surrogate Branch)**
Assume the LAN/QFI surrogate $g_{\rm true}(u)$ is the branch capacity coordinate, the admissible set obeys $g_{\rm true}(u)\le\ln d_0$, the rate objective is strictly convex, and its unconstrained minimizer lies above the cap. With $M=24$, $\lambda_i=1$, and $d_0=8$, the unique constrained minimizer is the cap endpoint:
$$
u^*=8^{1/24}-1=2^{1/8}-1\approx0.0905077327.
$$
Proposition E.2a's completed-reset support cap $\ln d_0-\ln2$ is not the LAN/QFI cap in this theorem. Remark Z.7c records $u_{\mathrm{reset}}=2^{1/12}-1$ only on a separate branch carrying an explicit whole-retained-output bridge and saturation certificate.

**Summary of Corollary Z.8.2a (Capacity Shadow Price)**
The same constrained problem has active multiplier
$$
\zeta_{\mathrm{cap}}
=
-\frac{\phi'(u^*)}{g_{\rm true}'(u^*)}
=
\Gamma_0-\frac{2A_{\mathrm{PCE}}u^*(1+u^*)}{M}
>0
$$
on the cap-active branch. The physical coupling is the normalized boundary-coordinate image $\alpha_{\mathrm{em}}=u^*/(4\pi\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}})$.

**Summary of Theorem Z.13b.0a (Predictive-Recovery MacWilliams Rate Selection)**
On the predictive-recovery branch (Definition Z.13b.0), payload and syndrome are MacWilliams-dual PCE roles. The strict dual-asymmetry penalty
$$
V_{\mathrm{dual}}(C)=\lambda_{\mathrm{dual}}(2k-24)^2,\qquad\lambda_{\mathrm{dual}}>0,
$$
has zero penalty only at $k=12$, and the hyperbolic code $C_0=\{(x,x):x\in\mathbb F_2^{12}\}$ realizes that point. Lexicographic PCE rate selection then forces $k=12$ before distance maximization.

**Summary of Theorem Z.13 (Predictive-Recovery MacWilliams Golay Code Realization)**
On an abstract binary coordinate carrier $\mathbb F_2^{24}$, the predictive-recovery rate gate gives
$$
C=C^\perp\subset\mathbb F_2^{24}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
2\dim C=24
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
k=12,
$$
or, on the MacWilliams rate branch, by Theorem Z.13b.0a. Theorem Z.13 then proves that binary linear $[24,12,d]$ codes satisfy $d\le8$, supplies an extended-Golay attainer, and classifies all distance-$8$ attainers up to coordinate permutation. Only an accepted $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ of Theorem Z.13b selects that equivalence class for the retained interface:

- Block length $n=24$
- Dimension $k=12$ on the rate branch
- Maximum attainable distance $d_{\min}=8$
- Classical correction radius $t=3$ for a selected attainer

These are finite coding statements. A physical error-correction claim additionally requires the encoder, noise, syndrome, recovery, threshold, and resource ledger $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{GQEC}}$.

### Conditional Defect and Superselection Models (Section 14.5)

A particle-defect model requires a configuration space or operator algebra, dynamics, locality notion, and superselection criterion. A Golay syndrome or Leech vector alone is not a particle or physical vacuum. Any relation to the Standard Model gauge algebra requires a response-preserving representation map, not equality of two dimensions.

**Conditional hierarchy map.** The Appendix T relation
$$
\ln\left(\frac{m_j}{m_i}\right)=\alpha d_{ij}^2+\text{registered corrections}
$$
is a model equation after its distance, coefficient, effective-dimension, threshold, and remainder entries are fixed. Generation points are not derived from Golay data.

**Conditional Defect Excitation (Section 14.5.7)**
Fix a physical configuration space or operator algebra, its dynamics and locality net, a vacuum-constraint family, and a response-preserving map from a marked finite syndrome record into those constraints. A defect is then a localized nonzero class in the resulting physical constraint quotient. A Golay syndrome or Leech vector without this record is an abstract finite label, not a particle or physical-vacuum excitation.

**Conditional Superselection Sector (Section 14.5.7)**
After fixing the admissible local neutral-operation algebra $\mathcal L$ and the observable algebra $\mathcal A$, two realized defects lie in the same declared sector exactly when their images agree in the registered quotient by $\mathcal L$. Local indistinguishability is a necessary diagnostic and becomes sufficient only when the accepted quotient theorem proves that $\mathcal A$ separates the quotient classes.

**Conditional Discrete--Continuous Correspondence (Section 14.5.7)**
At MPU resolution, stabilizer-like generators can implement a finite operational equivalence. Only an accepted response-preserving coarse-graining/gauge-subspace map that intertwines the finite neutral updates with continuum gauge transformations and the corresponding Ward-null directions promotes its image to local gauge redundancy:

| Level | Structure | Dimension | Status |
|:------|:----------|:----------|:-------|
| Golay code | Signal subspace | $k=12$ | predictive-recovery rate branch; code class additionally needs $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{dist}}$ |
| Gauge algebra | $\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{SM}}$ generators | $\dim\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{SM}}=12$ | independent saturated gauge-capacity branch |

The equality of dimensions is a compatibility identity. It supplies neither the coarse-graining map nor a group, algebra, or observable isomorphism.

**Map entry: Theorem T.41.5 (Gaussian Overlap and Yukawa Suppression on the Toeplitz--Kraus Probability-Overlap Branch)**
For equal isotropic Gaussian packets on the declared probability-overlap branch,
$$
K_{ij}^{\mathrm{mag}}
\propto
\exp\!\left(-\frac{d_{g_B}^2}{2\sigma_B^2}\right).
$$
For pairs carrying Lemma T.41.4's finite certificate, $d_{g_B}^2=d_{E_8}^2/8$ exactly. The chordal fidelity distance $D_B$ obeys a different finite law. Generation centers and physical realization remain independent data.

remains; its physical assignment to glueball labels does not. The scalar-channel filter of Theorem Z.8k is a separate branch.

**Norm Gap and Conditional Physical Mass and Rest-Energy Gaps (Corollary Z.8g.1; Proposition Z.8d)**

## Part IV: Pre-Geometric Infrastructure

### Golay-Steiner Structure

**Summary of Theorem U.1 (Golay Code Uniqueness)**
Every binary linear code with parameters $[24,12,8]$ is coordinate-permutation equivalent to the extended binary Golay code $\mathcal G_{24}$. In particular, there is one coordinate-permutation equivalence class of self-dual doubly-even binary codes with these parameters; labeled coordinate copies are not identical subspaces.

**Summary of Theorem U.2 (Steiner System)**
The 759 octads of the Golay code form the Steiner system $S(5,8,24)$: every 5-subset of the 24-point set lies in exactly one octad. Its design parameters are:

| Parameter | Formula | Value | Interpretation |
|:----------|:--------|:-----:|:---------------|
| $v$ (points) | $M$ | 24 | Interface modes |
| $k_{\text{block}}$ (block size) | $d_0$ | 8 | MPU dimension |
| $t$ (design strength) | — | 5 | Steiner design strength |
| $b$ (blocks/octads) | $\binom{v}{t}/\binom{k_{\text{block}}}{t}$ | 759 | Distinct 8-element codewords |
| $r$ (blocks per point) | $bk_{\text{block}}/v$ | 253 | Octads containing any given mode |
| $\lambda$ (pairs per block) | $r(k_{\text{block}}-1)/(v-1)$ | 77 | Common octads for any pair |

The automorphism group is the Mathieu group $M_{24}$ (order 244,823,040), and the 5-design property ensures every 5-subset lies in exactly one octad. The same octad structure supplies the residual-noise spectroscopy fingerprint: a random weight-8 subset is an octad with probability
$$
\frac{759}{\binom{24}{8}}=\frac1{969},
$$
so octad clustering is a highly non-generic test of substrate-aligned residual syndrome structure. The first decoder-aliasing shell is equally rigid: weight-4 tetrads decompose into
$$
\frac{\binom{24}{4}}{6}=1771
$$
six-tetrad sextets under the Golay syndrome quotient, and $M_{24}$ acts transitively on those sextets.

**Summary of Corollary U.2a (Pair Multiplicity)**
For the Steiner system $S(5,8,24)$, every pair of points lies in exactly
$$
\lambda = \frac{\binom{22}{3}}{\binom{6}{3}} = 77
$$
octads.

**Summary of Theorem U.6 (Octad Regularity Parameter-Framework Identity)**
The Steiner parameters connect directly to PU constants:
$$
r - \lambda = d_0(M - a) = 8 \times 22 = 176
$$
Expanded: $253 - 77 = 8 \times (24 - 2)$. This identity links the combinatorial structure of $S(5,8,24)$ to the MPU constants $(d_0, M, a)$. By Theorem U.5, this is the non-zero eigenvalue of the centered quadratic-form operator, while the non-zero Hessian eigenvalue is $2(r-\lambda)=352$.

**Summary of Theorem U.7b (Certified QFI-Mode--$24$-Cell Marking)**
On Definition Z.9a's faithful tangent-shell branch, a fixed response-preserving injection labels the $24$ retained modes by the vertices of a chosen regular $24$-cell. Equality of cardinalities alone does not define this marking. Transfer of the spherical $5$-design quadrature to an Appendix-U Hessian or action calculation additionally requires the same marking in the applicable carrier/Hessian certificate.

### 24-Cell Geometry and Symmetry (Appendix U.6, U.18–U.19)

**Summary of Definition U.28 (Spherical-Design 24-Cell Vertex Set)**
The 24-cell vertices on $S^3 \subset \mathbb{R}^4$ are:
$$
V_{24} = \{\pm e_i : i = 1,2,3,4\} \cup \left\{\frac{1}{2}(\pm 1, \pm 1, \pm 1, \pm 1)\right\}
$$
yielding $|V_{24}| = 8 + 16 = 24$.

**Summary of Theorem U.30 (24-Cell as Spherical 5-Design)**
The 24-cell vertices form a spherical 5-design: for any polynomial $p$ of degree $\leq 5$,
$$
\frac{1}{24} \sum_{v \in V_{24}} p(v) = \frac{1}{\text{Vol}(S^3)} \int_{S^3} p(x) \, d\sigma(x)
$$
The 24-cell is not a 6-design ($S_6 = 576 \neq 0$).

**Summary of Corollary U.31 (Design Strength and Dimension)**
The design strength satisfies the arithmetic identity $t=5=D+1$ after inserting the independently established value $D=4$ from Theorem Z.11. Corollary U.31 does not establish a rigidity or causal relation between design strength and either the Euclidean response-carrier dimension or promoted spacetime.

**Summary of Theorem U.35 (Weyl Group Symmetry)**
The 24-cell symmetry group is the Weyl group of the exceptional Lie algebra $F_4$:

$$
\text{Aut}(V_{24}) = W(F_4), \qquad |W(F_4)| = 1152 = 2^7 \times 3^2
$$

**Summary of Definition U.32--U.33 (Twelve Antipodal Lines and Independent Amplitude Carrier)**
Each vertex $v\in V_{24}$ pairs with $-v$ to form the antipodal line $\ell_v=\{v,-v\}$, so
$$
\mathcal L_{12}:=V_{24}/\{\pm1\},
\qquad
|\mathcal L_{12}|=12.
$$
The independent line-amplitude carrier is $\mathcal S_{\mathrm{line}}:=\mathbb C^{\mathcal L_{12}}$. Two lines are adjacent when $|\langle v,w\rangle|=1/2$. Use of the Appendix-U reference plane $S_U$ requires a unitary marking $J_{12}:S_U\to\mathcal S_{\mathrm{line}}$; use of Golay coordinates additionally requires the registered coordinate-to-vertex marking and encoder/intertwiner.

**Summary of Lemma U.34 (Line Adjacency Graph)**
The line adjacency graph has degree $8$ and spectrum $\{8^{(1)},(-4)^{(2)},0^{(9)}\}$.

**Summary of Proposition U.34a–Theorem U.34c (Fixed Finite Graph Certificates).** The twelve-line graph is $K_{4,4,4}$. Its Bass–Ihara factors and those of the antipodal $24$-cell double cover follow from the exact spectra. Both fixed graphs are Ramanujan. Theorem U.34e excludes uniform expansion only for bounded-degree families with a uniform polynomial upper bound in the unweighted graph distance; applying it to Definition 36 requires the normalized Definition 36 metric-comparability gate of Remark U.34f.

**Octad Incidence and Adjacency-Parity Certificates (Theorem Z.13c.3; Corollary Z.13c.4; Theorem Z.13c.5).** The $759\times24$ octad incidence matrix satisfies
$$
H^TH=176I+77J,
$$
has real rank $24$ and binary rank $12$, and is simultaneously a redundant generator and parity-check system for $\mathcal G_{24}$ over $\mathbb F_2$. By contrast, the twelve-line adjacency matrix defines a $[12,10,2]$ parity-check code. These exact finite certificates are not inputs to the Appendix Z value of $\alpha^{-1}$.

**Summary of Theorem U.36 (Invariant Line-Amplitude Eigenspace Decomposition)**
The adjacency operator on the independent line-amplitude carrier has the $W(F_4)$-invariant orthogonal decomposition
$$
\mathcal S_{\mathrm{line}}
=
\mathcal S_8\oplus\mathcal S_{-4}\oplus\mathcal S_0,
$$
with dimensions $1$, $2$, and $9$ for eigenvalues $8$, $-4$, and $0$, respectively. Transport of this decomposition to $S_U$, a binary code, or a physical field requires the corresponding marking/intertwiner.

**Summary of Theorem U.3 (Configuration Dimension on the Registered Grassmannian Reference Branch)**
Section U.4 independently registers a complex carrier $E_U\cong\mathbb C^{24}$ and reference plane $S_U\cong\mathbb C^{12}$:

\mathcal M_U=\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbb C}(S_U,E_U)\cong\operatorname{Gr}_{\mathbb C}(12,24).

Writing $(r_U,N_U)=(12,24)$, Theorem U.3 proves

- Real dimension: $\dim_{\mathbb R}\mathcal M_U=2r_U(N_U-r_U)=288$

These are reference-carrier dimensions. The numerical equality $r_U=k=12$ supplies no binary-code-to-complex-plane map. Transport of a Hessian nullity to this carrier requires $\mathfrak C_{U,\mathrm{mark}}$, and its promotion to an action requires Proposition U.14.

**Summary of Definition U.1 (Reference Grassmannian Complexity Count)**
On the balanced reference branch Appendix U defines
$$
\kappa_{\max}^{\mathrm{ref}}:=r_U(N_U-r_U)=144.
$$
This is a complex-dimension count, not a saddle exponent, Fredholm weight, or physical vacuum observable.

**Summary of Corollary U.4 (Phase Space Dimension)**
$$
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}\!\big(T^*\text{Gr}_{\mathbb{C}}(12,24)\big)=2\cdot 288 = 576,\qquad\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\!\big(T^*\text{Gr}_{\mathbb{C}}(12,24)\big)=2\cdot 144 = 288.
$$

## Part V: Quantum Mechanics Emergence

### C*-Algebra Foundation (Section 8)

**Summary of Theorem 8.1 (PU-GNS Representation)**
Every positive normalized continuous state $\omega$ on the completed MPU observable algebra $\mathfrak A$ yields a cyclic Hilbert-space representation by GNS. Lemma 8.0a has already assumed complex scalars and a nonempty admissible representation class witnessed by an independently retained bounded Hilbert representation; GNS repackages that completed algebra and state and is not an independent derivation of a physical Hilbert carrier from prediction.

**Summary of Theorem 8.2 (Idle-Context Quotient for Predictive Weights)**
On the response-complete POP-risk branch with PPI admissibility, a strictly positive cost for retained context labels, and an attained global PCE minimum, every minimizing ledger is constant on operational response classes and therefore descends to $[E]$. PPI/PCE alone does not prove attainment or quotient descent.

**Summary of Lemma 8.2a (Finite-Response Additivity on the Quotient)**
On the additional branch with an affine branchwise payoff representation and equality of the coarse and refined response-complete payoff problems, payoff-refinement consistency makes the descended ledger finitely additive on the registered finite orthogonal decompositions.

**Summary of Definition 8.2b and Theorem 8.3 (Certified Born Selector)**
Quotienting and retained-refinement additivity do not establish the domain required by Gleason or Busch. An accepted $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{Born}}$ supplies either every projection/effect and its required additivity relations, or a finite informationally complete Hermitian operator basis with relation consistency and a positive trace-one reconstructed density matrix. Theorem 8.3 then gives
$$
p(E)=\operatorname{tr}(\rho E)
$$
on the certified domain, uniquely. The global all-projection or all-effect conclusion belongs only to the full-domain route.

**Summary of Theorem 8.5 (Schrödinger Form from Continuous Reversible Transition-Probability-Preserving Time Translations)**
Assume that the Internal Prediction ray maps are reversible, preserve transition probabilities, obey $\mathcal T_{t+s}=\mathcal T_t\circ\mathcal T_s$ with $\mathcal T_0=\mathrm{id}$, and admit weakly measurable implementers on the finite-dimensional carrier. Wigner's theorem [Wigner 1931] and the group law give a unitary lift, weak measurability gives strong continuity, and Stone's theorem [Stone 1932] supplies a self-adjoint generator $H$ such that
$$
U(t)=e^{-iHt/\hbar},
\qquad
i\hbar \frac{d|\psi(t)\rangle}{dt}=H|\psi(t)\rangle.
$$

| Step | Structure | Theorem |
|:-----|:----------|:--------|
| 1 | Finite-response $C^*$-envelope $\mathfrak A$ | Complex scalar branch plus independently witnessed nonempty bounded representation class (Lemma 8.0a) |
| 2 | Cyclic representation $\mathcal H_\omega$ | GNS representation of the already completed algebra and chosen positive normalized state (8.1); physical carrier selection remains Principle 8.0b/Theorem G.1.8 |
| 3 | Born rule on the certified domain | PPI/PCE quotient + retained finite-response additivity + Definition 8.2b full-domain or finite informationally complete positive reconstruction + Born selector (8.2, 8.2a, 8.2b, 8.3) |
| 4 | Unitary dynamics | Symmetry (8.5) |
| 5 | Schrödinger equation | Time translation generator |

### Superposition and Entanglement (Sections 8.2, 8.6)

**Summary of Proposition 6 (Superposition as Predictive Indeterminacy)**
On the accepted complex-Hilbert branch, superposition supplies amplitudes whose relative phases can affect retained interference responses. This excludes a classical ignorance representation only for a certified incompatible-effect domain under Corollary G.1.10; it does not by itself exclude every contextual or response-equivalent latent completion.

**Summary of Proposition 10 (Entanglement as Predictive Coupling)**
Entanglement encodes nonclassical predictive coupling; maximally entangled pure states maximize mutual information at fixed local dimension.

**Summary of Corollary 5 (Bell Violations without FTL Signaling)**
Suitable entangled states and local measurement settings can violate Bell inequalities without enabling deterministic FTL signaling.

### Born Rule Derivation

**Summary of Definition G.1.1 (Cost Frame Function)**
A cost frame function $f$ assigns a POP-consistent predictive weight to each retained projector event $P$, normalized by $f(\mathbf1)=1$. This payoff weight is identified with a realized single-run probability only on the separate probability-calibration, instrument, and single-outcome branch:
$$
f(\Pi)=\sum_i f(P_i),\qquad f(\mathbf{1})=1.
$$
The frame function must satisfy: (i) quotient non-contextuality, (ii) additivity over orthogonal sums, and (iii) $0\le f(P)\le1$.

**Layer 1: idle-context quotient**
Theorem 8.2 and Lemma G.1.1b remove response-null context labels. A weight may depend on the retained effect or projector, not on a private context label that changes no response-presheaf profile.

**Layer 2: finite-response additivity**
Lemma 8.2a and Lemma G.1.1ba impose coarse/refined payoff consistency:
$$
f(P+Q)=f(P)+f(Q)\qquad(PQ=0).
$$
This is the stable reconstruction layer. It does not yet select the squared-amplitude measure by itself.

**Layer 3: certified Born domain and selector**
Definition 8.2b supplies the missing coverage gate. On its full-domain route, Theorem 8.3 and Theorem G.1.3 apply Gleason/Busch. On its finite route, a full-rank informationally complete effect basis and its trace-dual reconstruct a unique positive trace-one $\rho$. Thus
$$
f(E)=\operatorname{tr}(\rho E)
$$
for every certified effect, and for all projections/effects only on the full-domain route. For a certified pure ray and retained rank-one outcome this gives $f(P_i)=|\langle i|\psi\rangle|^2$. Power alternatives fail the registered coarse/refined relations unless $\gamma=2$, but that calculation does not replace domain completeness.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.7 (Born Rule from Quotient Additivity and the Certified Born Selector)**
The trace representation follows on the complex MPU carrier only after quotienting, retained-refinement additivity, independent noncontextuality, and an accepted Definition-8.2b Born-domain certificate are jointly supplied. Theorem G.1.7 gives uniqueness on that certified domain; interpreting the weights as realized single-run probabilities additionally requires the probability-calibration and actualization/instrument gates. SPAP alone supplies none of these physical selector premises.

**Summary of Theorem 8.6 (SPAP-to-Quantum Dependency Separation and Certified Born Uniqueness)** [§8.3]
The route has six logically distinct gates: the SPAP diagonal obstruction; the invariant binary response law; complex-carrier selection; quotienting and retained-refinement additivity; Definition 8.2b's full-domain or informationally complete positive-reconstruction certificate followed by Theorem 8.3; and Principle 8.0c's registered single-outcome identification. Born uniqueness holds on the certified domain. A sparse retained context is neither globally complete nor density-informationally complete, and Steps 1--2 imply none of the later physical gates.

**Example 8.1 (Qubit Measurement Illustration)** [§8.5.2]
Consider an MPU qubit in state $|\psi\rangle = c_0|0\rangle + c_1|1\rangle$:

| Measurement | Perspective | Outcomes | Post-Measurement State |
|:------------|:------------|:---------|:-----------------------|
| **Z-basis** | $s_Z$ | $\lvert0\rangle$ with $P=\lVert c_0\rVert^2$, $\lvert1\rangle$ with $P=\lVert c_1\rVert^2$ | $(\lvert k\rangle, s^\prime_{Z,k})$ |
| **X-basis** | $s_X$ | $\lvert+\rangle$ with $P=\lVert(c_0+c_1)/\sqrt{2}\rVert^2$ | $(\lvert +\rangle, s^\prime_{X,+})$ |

*Conditional perspective-indexed record:* On the accepted instrument and single-outcome branch, result $0$ is registered relative to $s'_{Z,0}$. Re-expanding $|0\rangle$ in the $X$ basis is a basis change, not a second outcome record. The example does not by itself exclude every absolute or latent response-equivalent ontology.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.8 (Complex Hilbert-Space Uniqueness on the Carrier-Certified Branch)**

1. **Local tomography:** For a scalar branch $\mathbb D$ with single-system parameter count $N_{\mathbb D}(d)$, compositional closure under joint-system tomography requires
$$
N_{\mathbb D}(mn)+1=(N_{\mathbb D}(m)+1)(N_{\mathbb D}(n)+1).
$$
The complex branch has $N_{\mathbb C}(d)=d^2-1$, so $N_{\mathbb C}(mn)+1=(mn)^2=(m^2)(n^2)=(N_{\mathbb C}(m)+1)(N_{\mathbb C}(n)+1)$, and the identity holds for all $m,n\ge2$. The real branch has $N_{\mathbb R}(d)=d(d+1)/2-1$ and the quaternionic branch has $N_{\mathbb H}(d)=d(2d-1)-1$. Direct evaluation at $m=n=2$ already excludes both: $N_{\mathbb R}(4)+1=10\ne9=(N_{\mathbb R}(2)+1)^2$ and $N_{\mathbb H}(4)+1=28\ne36=(N_{\mathbb H}(2)+1)^2$. The same strict inequality persists for every $m,n\ge2$ on the real and quaternionic branches [Hardy 2001; Masanes & Müller 2011], so neither branch is closed under joint-system tomography.

2. **Connected phase transport:** The real branch has only $\{\pm1\}$ and cannot supply a nontrivial connected scalar phase. The quaternionic branch has $Sp(1)\cong SU(2)$, adding two surplus scalar-phase generators beyond the minimal connected abelian $U(1)$ phase of the complex branch.

3. **PPI/PCE quotient:** Quaternionic extra generators are either response-changing, hence a different gauge branch, or response-null, hence removed by PPI/PCE. Other Euclidean-Jordan branches are excluded only by the $\mathcal J_{\mathrm{excl}}$ record inside $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{car}}$.

Complex amplitudes are not assumed on the carrier-certified branch; they are the unique retained Hilbert scalar branch satisfying compositional closure, local tomography, connected phase transport, and minimal phase redundancy after the carrier certificate has supplied the ordered-cone reconstruction.

**Summary of Corollary G.1.10 (Conditional Non-Simplex Exclusion).** A finite-dimensional branch with a certified pair of response-active sharp observables that are not jointly measurable cannot be a classical simplex theory carrying the full affine effect set. SPAP alone does not supply the incompatibility premise, and complex-Hilbert reconstruction requires the separate carrier certificate.

### Detailed Balance and Gibbs Structure

**Summary of Definition G.1.9.2a (Spohn Entropy-Production Ledger)**
The finite semigroup ledger is $\Sigma_\sigma[\rho;t]=D(\rho\Vert\sigma)-D(\Phi_t\rho\Vert\sigma)\ge0$. Detailed balance does not make this vanish for arbitrary nonequilibrium states.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.1 (Active-Support Contractivity and Conditional Primitivity of ND-RID)**
On the optional active-support refresh branch of Definition G.1.9.1a,
$$
\mathcal E_{N,a}=(1-p)\Psi_a+pT_{\sigma_a},
\qquad p\in(0,1],
$$
is trace-distance contractive on $\mathcal H_a$ with factor $1-p<1$. If $\sigma_a\succ0$, the active-support channel is primitive and has a unique faithful active-support stationary state. Neither the decomposition nor a positive value of $p$ follows from the reset ledger, and no full-carrier primitivity conclusion is asserted.

**Summary of Proposition G.1.9.2 (Conditional PCE Preference for Lower Production)**
If two admissible ND-RID semigroups have the same stationary state and predictive utility, and the registered PCE functional is strictly increasing in a specified integrated-production functional, PCE prefers the candidate with smaller registered production. This comparison does not establish admissibility, uniqueness, or selection of a detailed-balance generator.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.3 (Detailed-Balance Reversibility and Relaxation Production)**
For a finite-dimensional GKLS generator with faithful stationary state $\sigma$, the registered $\sigma$-GNS detailed-balance condition is
$$
\langle A,\mathcal L_D(B)\rangle_\sigma
=
\langle\mathcal L_D(A),B\rangle_\sigma.
$$
This equilibrium-reversibility condition is compatible with positive relaxation production away from $\sigma$; it does not imply zero production for every initial state.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.4 (Gibbs Structure of PCE-Attractor)**
The PCE-attractor has the modular representation $\tau^*\propto e^{-K^*}$ on its support. This identity does not establish physical thermality without the independent selector of Theorem G.1.9.3c.

A faithful stationary state $\rho^*$ always admits the modular representation $\rho^*=Z^{-1}e^{-K^*}$ and satisfies the KMS identity in its own modular parameter. Detailed balance relative to $\rho^*$ does not identify that parameter with physical time or $\rho^*$ as a Gibbs state for a registered Hamiltonian; that interpretation requires the independent complete-passivity/physical-time selector of Theorem G.1.9.3c.

**Summary of Definition G.1.9.4a (Modular Hamiltonian)**
For a faithful density operator $\rho$, the modular Hamiltonian is:
$$
K_\rho := -\ln \rho
$$
With $K_\rho=-\log\rho$, the Appendix-F modular convention is $\sigma_t^\rho(A)=e^{-iK_\rho t}Ae^{iK_\rho t}=\rho^{it}A\rho^{-it}$ with lower-strip boundary $t-i$; identifying its parameter with physical time requires the independent equilibrium certificate.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.5 (Modular KMS Identity and Physical-Time Boundary)**
Every faithful state obeys the modular KMS identity in its own modular parameter; physical time and thermality require an independent selector.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.6 (Conditional Modular-Hamiltonian Representations)**
The theorem records three branch-dependent representations:

- **Reference identity:** For every faithful active-support state $\rho$, $K_\rho=-\log\rho$ and $K_\rho=K^*_{PCE}+K_{constraint}$ by definition.
- **Thermal selector branch:** Maximizing von Neumann entropy subject to normalization and a specified mean energy gives $K_\rho=\beta H+(\log Z)I$; the scalar $K^*_{PCE}$ can be absorbed into normalization.
- **Rindler-wedge QFT branch:** Under the Bisognano-Wichmann hypotheses, $K_{\mathrm{horizon}}=2\pi B=(2\pi/(\hbar\kappa))H_\chi$.

The thermal and horizon forms require their respective maximum-entropy/complete-passivity and QFT modular certificates; ND-RID does not select either form by itself.

**Summary of Theorem G.1.9.9 (Conditional Landauer and Carrier Ledger)**
The following rows have independent branch hypotheses and hold together only on their intersection:

- Structural: $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ is the log-cardinality of a registered reachable binary quotient.
- Thermodynamic: $Q_{\mathrm{bath}}\ge k_BT\ln2$ requires a conditionally uniform binary reset with no retained copy; equality also requires zero dissipative overhead.
- Information: $a=2$ requires Theorem Z.1's sharp-record, entropy-capacity, and no-surplus branch.
- Geometric: $M=2ab=24$ requires Theorem Z.5 together with $d_0=8$, $a=2$, and $b=6$.

No row implies any other row.

### Conditional Capacity-Bound Ledger

**Summary of Theorem 6.0.1 (Branchwise Capacity Consequences)**
The finite-transfer bounds have the following distinct scopes:

| Domain | Required branch | Conclusion |
|:-------|:----------------|:-----------|
| Completed binary reset support | tensor-factor support certificate | $C_{\max}\le\ln d_0-\ln2$ |
| Refresh channel | nonzero input-independent full-state refresh | $C_{\max}<\ln d_0$ |
| Boundary entropy | geometric channel-density certificate | $S_{\mathrm{boundary}}/k_B\le N_{\mathrm{eff}}C_{\max}$ |
| Gravitational calibration | density, capacity saturation, and local KMS/Clausius certificates | $G=\eta\delta^2c^3/(4\hbar\chi C_{\max})$ |

A registered reset separately obeys $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}\ge H_q(P\mid R)$. Neither its heat ledger nor the structural value $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ implies a refresh channel. On the completed binary support branch with $d_0=8$ and residual-budget saturation,
$$
C_{\max}^*=\ln d_0-\varepsilon_0=2\ln2.
$$
Without saturation, this remains an upper bound.

### Distinct Conditional Probability Ledgers

The manuscript uses three separate constructions:

1. On the stochastic complex-Hilbert branch, normalized noncontextual finite-effect additivity gives Born weights by Theorem 28a.
2. A reachable binary record independently gives $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$; this is log-cardinality, not a probability selector.
3. On a branch carrying detailed balance with physical-time identification or complete passivity, a declared constraint family $\mathcal C$ may have an equilibrium state
$$
\rho^*_{\mathcal C}
=
Z_{\mathcal C}^{-1}e^{-K^*(\mathcal C)}.
$$

| Constraint family | Additional input | Scoped conclusion |
|:------------------|:-----------------|:------------------|
| Reference quantum state | Hilbert/Born selector and supplied $\rho_{\mathrm{phys}}$ | $K=-\log\rho_{\mathrm{phys}}+\text{constant}$ is a representation of that state |
| Thermal equilibrium | physical Hamiltonian, inverse temperature, and QDB/complete-passivity certificate | Gibbs/KMS state on that branch |
| Gravitational horizon | local boost generator and Rindler/KMS horizon certificate | Unruh-Hawking modular state on the covered wedge |

These ledgers can be compatible on a common certified branch, but none is derived from SPAP or from the binary structural alphabet alone.

### Law of Prediction: Corollaries (Section 6.3)

**Summary of Corollary 19.1 (Conditional Power-Law Learning Curves)**
On a registered branch satisfying both Theorem 19's exact multiplicative residual-composition hypothesis and the independent logarithmic budget $C(N)-C_{op}=\eta_{\ell}\ln(N/N_0)$,
$$
\beta-\overline{PP}(N)=(\beta-\alpha)(N/N_0)^{-s},
\qquad
s=\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}\eta_{\ell}/\hat C_{\mathrm{target}}.
$$
The value at $N=N_0$ is the formal analytic anchor; physical $PP$ is restricted to $N>N_0$. The identity is branch-conditional and does not follow from POP or PCE alone.

**Summary of Corollary 19.2 (Conditional Size- and Data-Coordinate Power Laws)**
For a registered aggregate branch satisfying Theorem 19 and the exact size-to-complexity relation $C(N_p)-C_{op}=\eta_p\ln(N_p/N_{p,0})$,
$$
\beta-\overline{PP}(N_p)=(\beta-\alpha)(N_p/N_{p,0})^{-s_p},
\qquad
s_p=\kappa_{\mathrm{eff}}\eta_p/\hat C_{\mathrm{target}}.
$$
The anchor $N_p=N_{p,0}$ is formal; the physical statement has $N_p>N_{p,0}$. Data-size and multivariate factorized identities require their corresponding exact logarithmic maps and the same endpoint discipline.

**Summary of Proposition 19.2a and Remark 19.2 (Registered Finite-Record Compatibility Test)**
For the formal boundary datum $P_0=\alpha$ and exact registered off-boundary observations, the composite curve holds for one $s>0$ exactly when every off-boundary log-slope
$$
-\frac{\ln((\beta-P_k)/(\beta-\alpha))}{\ln(N_k/N_0)}
$$
is defined, positive, and equal. The boundary equality tests analytic normalization, not validation data. This establishes compatibility only at the registered off-boundary points. With noisy observations, one joint uncertainty region, including registered covariance, and the acceptance rule must be fixed before holdout observation. Failure rejects only the registered composite branch; survival identifies neither its antecedents nor a shared mechanism across carriers.

**Summary of Remark 19.3 (Weber-Fechner from Equation (5))**
Since the representative reflexive-information cost rate is logarithmic, $R_I(C)=(r_I/\ln 2)\ln(C/K_0)$ (Equation 5), the condition $\Delta R_I=\delta_R$ is equivalent to $C_2/C_1=\exp(\delta_R\ln 2/r_I)$. In the small-increment limit this gives $\Delta C/C\approx\delta_R\ln 2/r_I$, the Weber-Fechner constant-ratio law.

**Summary of Corollary 22.1 (Gaussian Attractor under Quadratic-Cost PCE)**
Near any unique non-degenerate PCE attractor with Hessian $H=\nabla^2V(x^*)\succ0$, the detailed-balance low-noise Gibbs measure $\pi_\theta\propto\exp[-V/\theta]$ rescales as $\xi=(x-x^*)/\sqrt\theta$ to $\mathcal N(0,H^{-1})$ as $\theta\downarrow0$. Coordinate projections and independent stationary samples obey the ordinary central-limit behavior in this local low-noise regime.

## Part VI: Property R and Computational Foundations

### LITE Construction

**Summary of Theorem A.4.1 (Total Computable LITE Fixed-Point Witness)**
Kleene's Second Recursion Theorem supplies a code $\beta$ for one total computable function
$$
f(n)=
\begin{cases}
n+H_1(n),&\operatorname{Prf}_{\le g(n)}(\ulcorner\phi_\beta(n)\urcorner),\\
n+H_2(n),&\neg\operatorname{Prf}_{\le g(n)}(\ulcorner\phi_\beta(n)\urcorner)
\land\operatorname{Prf}_{\le g(n)}(\ulcorner\neg\phi_\beta(n)\urcorner),\\
n+1,&\text{otherwise},
\end{cases}
$$
where the proof searches are bounded and therefore terminate. This is an explicit self-referential verifier presentation: it carries its own code, performs bounded proof search, and branches on the registered predicates.

**Scope of the LITE Witness (Appendix A.4.3--A.4.5; Theorem A.4.1; Corollary A.4.1b)**
The construction proves existence of this total computable fixed-point witness in the stated arithmetic. It does not by itself establish the full uniform-class scalability, composition depth, memory, or coding premises of Property R; it proves neither PCE minimality nor substitution for $C_P$, proxy alignment, QEC closure, or physical implementation. Full Property R remains the conclusion of Theorem A.0.1 and Corollary A.0.1 only under their uniform predictive-class, scalable finite-resource, and representing-arithmetic hypotheses.

### Retrospective Trace Certification and Live-Challenge Scope

**Appendix A.5 (Predictor-Indexed PA Trace Objects)**
Appendix A.5 defines a retrospective PA transcript family $G_S$. Each $G_S$ is trace-certified and PA-provable. This does not identify $G_S$ with Theorem M.10.4's live joint diagonal challenge and does not establish $\mu_S(G_S)=\infty$ or pointwise infinite processing cost. Conditional external reachability requires an explicit finite external-processing certificate. The live limitation is instead phase-indexed: $E_{B,t}$ cannot be correctly processed by $B$ at the targeted cycle, while external and historical processing are possible under Theorem A.5.6a.4.

**Appendix A.6 (No Total Standard-Sound Live-Diagonal Self-Closure)**
Appendix A.6 packages the time-indexed live diagonal theorem into a scoped closure result. If a same-cycle domain contains $E_{S,t}$, any active Boolean output by $S$ is wrong on that sentence; abstention avoids error but defeats totality. Therefore no same-cycle closure on a live-diagonal-complete domain is both total and standard-sound. Historical recovery at $t+1$ remains compatible with Theorem A.6.2.

**Axiom-Stabilizer Scope**
A nontrivial finite prediction-update cycle induces a stable axiom-stabilizer only when it satisfies every hypothesis of Theorem A.6.4, including finite-budget retention and the live-diagonal guard. The retained tuple is
$$
\mathfrak A_{S,B}
=
(D_{S,B},J_{S,B},\vdash_{S,B},V_{S,B},q_{S,B}).
$$
Theorem A.6.2 separately requires a same-cycle standard-sound treatment of $E_{S,t}$ to omit it, return $\bot$, or defer it. Theorem A.6.4 constructs a stabilizer from the additional finite-cycle hypotheses; live-diagonal non-closure alone neither constructs nor uniquely selects one. Theorem A.6.6 preserves the status of every earlier theorem, branch, certificate, and numerical value.

### Conditional PCE/QEC Route to Effective Property R

**Summary of Theorem A.0.2 (Conditional Effective Property R at a Certified PCE Optimum)**
Under the refresh-branch condition, an accepted QEC compatibility certificate $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{QEC}}$ or accepted Golay bootstrap, Theorem A.0.5's optimizer hypotheses, Dominant Cost Convexity, a separate robustness certificate, and the working-memory, protected-gate, overhead, and execution ledger:

1. a unique minimizer $p_{\text{err}}^*>0$ exists;
2. the separate robustness record gives $p_{\text{err}}^*<1/2$ on its declared protected window;
3. the implementation ledger supplies the finite representation, protected simulation, and predicate-evaluation resources; and
4. the resulting network possesses Effective Operational Property R only for the finite tasks and windows covered by those records.

**Summary of Theorem A.0.2a (Golay Bootstrap Discharges the QEC Certificate on Covered Branches)**
On an accepted Golay-QEC bootstrap record $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{GQEC}}$, the Appendix Z Golay branch supplies the code witness while the physical syndrome/recovery/threshold ledger supplies the missing implementation entries. The chain is

$$
K_0=3,
\quad d_0=8,
\quad M=24
\Rightarrow
\mathcal G_{24}
\Rightarrow
[[23,1,7]]
\Rightarrow
\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{QEC}}
\Rightarrow
\text{Effective Property R on covered protected windows}.
$$
This is a certificate-discharge route through the displayed implication, not an unconditional primitive derivation of QEC compatibility.

**Summary of Theorem A.0.6 (Conditional Network Universality with Explicit Overhead Scope)**
A network of $n$ MPUs with error rate $p_{\text{err}}^*$ can realize full Property R provided the computation also has sufficient working-memory, QEC, and circuit-execution overhead resources:

1. **Representation**: the displayed direct transition-table encoding uses $n_{\mathrm{desc}}=\Theta(km\log(km))$ three-bit carriers; this is a sufficient count for that encoding, not a lower bound against compressed descriptions
2. **Simulation**: Solovay-Kitaev + QEC provide conditional simulation capability once the required overhead resources are available
3. **Evaluation**: the accepted fault-tolerance certificate supplies the recorded logical-error target for the nominated finite computation

**Summary of Corollary A.0.3 (Resource Requirements for SPAP/RUD)**

- The displayed direct description storage uses $n_{\mathrm{desc}}=\Theta(km\log(km))$ carriers
- Total MPU count depends additionally on working memory, QEC overhead, and required circuit depth; no universal numeric count follows from Appendix A alone

### Two Complementary Foundations for Property R (Appendix A.0)

Property R is supplied through two complementary conditional routes:

| Foundation | Source | Question Answered | Status |
|:-----------|:-------|:------------------|:------:|
| **I: Conditional Logical Construction** (§A.0.2) | Declared composition, memory, uniformity, scalability, and arithmetic-coding hypotheses | *Which formal conditions suffice for Property R?* | Conditional theorem |
| **II: Physical Instantiation** (§A.0.3–A.0.5) | MPU framework under POP/PCE with ND-RID and accepted implementation certificates | *How can those conditions be represented under resource constraints?* | Certificate-gated physical realization |

**Foundation II Derivation Chain:**

$$
\text{Individual MPU capacity } (K_0 = 3 \text{ bits})
\to
\text{POP/PCE optimization}
\to
\text{Network composition}
\to
(\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{QEC}}\ \text{or covered }\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{GQEC}})
\to
\text{Operational Property R}
$$

**Summary of Proposition A.0.3 (Conditional Self-Referential Carrier Capacity of an MPU)**
If an MPU realizes the registered SPAP sub-dynamics, satisfies (O1)–(O3) and (FC), represents the eight contexts as mutually perfectly distinguishable Hilbert alternatives, and obeys $C_P\ge C_{cap}$, then it has at least eight visited contexts, $d_0\ge8$, and $C_P=C_{op}\ge K_0=3$. MPU status or the numerical inequality $C_P\ge3$ alone does not entail an eight-context carrier or Effective Operational Property R.

**Summary of Proposition A.0.6 (Compositional Enhancement)**
For fixed $n$ MPUs with individual Hilbert spaces $\mathcal{H}_0 \cong\mathbb C^8$, the composite state space has finite dimension $\dim(\mathcal H_{\mathrm{composite}})=8^n$. Arbitrarily large finite descriptions require a scalable family with unbounded $n$, and execution additionally requires the working-memory, protected-gate, QEC, runtime, and reliability records of Theorems A.0.2 and A.0.6.

**Synthesis:** The conditional logical construction identifies formal hypotheses sufficient for Property R; the physical route identifies certificates sufficient to represent that construction on covered finite tasks and windows. Neither route proves that prediction alone must possess those hypotheses.

### D-RID and ND-RID Formal Definitions (Appendix A.2)

**Summary of Definition A.2.1 (D-RID — Deterministic Reflexive Interaction Dynamics)**
A D-RID system is a tuple $S = (X, Y, O, V, T)$ where:
- $X$ = set of states, $Y$ = set of interactions, $O$ = set of outcomes
- $V: X \times Y \to O$ — deterministic outcome function
- $T: X \times Y \times O \to X$ — deterministic state transformation

**Summary of Definition A.2.2 (ND-RID — Non-Deterministic Reflexive Interaction Dynamics)**
An ND-RID system is a tuple $S = (X, Y, O, V_{prob}, T_{prob})$ where:
- $V_{prob}: X \times Y \to \Delta(O)$ — probabilistic outcome function
- $T_{prob}: X \times Y \times O \to \Delta(X)$ — probabilistic state transformation

The MPU 'Evolve' process (Definition 27) is modeled as an instance of ND-RID. Lemma 2 supplies the following scope-qualified properties:
1. **Potential irrecoverability:** the state transformation may be noninvertible, so recovery is not guaranteed.
2. **Context shift:** information pertains to its acquisition context.
3. **Conditional predictive instability:** stable accurate self-prediction can fail when the dynamics is designed to react counter-predictively to the prediction; outcome dependence alone does not imply instability.

## Part VII: Gauge Structure and Standard Model

### Electromagnetic Gauge Emergence

**Equation (G.2.1) (Local $U(1)$ Phase Freedom on the Rank-One Frame Branch)**
On the registered Hermitian complex-line certificate, point-local Born weights descend to rays and independent local frame changes act as
$$
|\Psi(x)\rangle \longrightarrow |\Psi'(x)\rangle = e^{iq\theta(x)}|\Psi(x)\rangle, \qquad \theta(x) \in \mathbb{R}/2\pi\mathbb Z.
$$
Point-local ray invariance alone does not create independent local frames or a connection. Under Assumptions G.3.1--G.3.5, Theorem G.3a proves that unitary edge transporters give the unique exact edge-local comparison within the declared quadratic complex-linear comparison class; Corollary G.3a.1 yields the smooth connection field $A_\mu$ in the continuum limit.

**Summary of Definition G.4.1 (Covariant Derivative $D_\mu$)**
The gauge-covariant derivative implementing minimal coupling is:
$$
D_\mu\Psi(x) = (\partial_\mu + \Omega_\mu + iqA_\mu(x))\Psi(x)
$$

**Equations (G.5.1)–(G.5.3) (Field Strength and Conditional Maxwell Action)**
For the abelian connection,
$$
F_{\mu\nu}(x) = \partial_\mu A_\nu(x) - \partial_\nu A_\mu(x).
$$
On the regular Lorentzian, parity-even/CP-even, single-$U(1)$, two-derivative branch with no kinetic mixing, a specified topological sector, and positive normalization $\kappa_F$, the local quadratic action is
$$
S_{\text{field}}[A] = \int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\left(-\frac{1}{4\kappa_F} F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right).
$$
A field rescaling accompanied by the inverse rescaling of $q$ gives the canonical representative
$$
S_{\text{field}}[A] = \int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\left(-\frac{1}{4} F_{\mu\nu}F^{\mu\nu}\right).
$$
Higher-order gauge invariants and the globally relevant topological term $F\wedge F$ lie outside this quadratic specified-sector statement.

**Equation (G.6.1) (Minimal Coupling for Matter Fields)**
Under the regularity, uniform link-expansion, boundary, quadratic-link-cost, and Euclidean-to-Lorentzian continuation hypotheses of Theorem G.6a, the leading scalar matter Lagrangian in signature $(-,+,\ldots,+)$ is
$$
\mathcal{L}_{\text{matter}} = -g^{\mu\nu}(D_\mu \phi)^* (D_\nu \phi) - m^2 |\phi|^2.
$$
This is the continuum limit of the registered nearest-neighbor quadratic coherence cost. Local gauge invariance alone does not exclude higher-derivative or higher-order gauge-invariant matter terms.

### Standard Model Gauge Group

**Summary of Proposition G.M1 (Conditional Gauge Bundle from Registered Local Frame Data)**
Assume a smooth local-frame torsor with a free transitive compact-group action, a registered equivariant horizontal complement, the inactive-sector decomposition
$$
\mathbb C^6\cong\mathbb C^3\oplus\mathbb C^2\oplus\mathbb C^1,
$$
and an injective isotropic gauge-response map whose retained algebra contains the full blockwise $\mathfrak{su}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)$ action and one specified determinant-compatible abelian character. Then the frame bundle is principal, the horizontal distribution is a connection, and the retained algebra is
$$
\mathfrak{su}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)\oplus\mathfrak u(1),
\qquad
\dim\mathfrak g=12.
$$
The proposition consumes these registered frame and response data; it does not derive the Standard Model gauge group from PCE cost minimization alone.

**Summary of Corollary G.8.4c.0a.1 (Block-Frame Stabilizer-Code Form of the Gauge Algebra)**
The PCE-selected inactive-sector block frame on the positive-marginal branch,
$$
\mathcal B=\mathbb C^3\oplus\mathbb C^2\oplus\mathbb C^1,
$$
can equivalently be read as a finite-response syndrome code. Its capacity-saturating determinant-compatible retained stabilizer algebra is
$$
\mathfrak g_{\mathrm{stab}}
=
\mathfrak{su}(3)\oplus\mathfrak{su}(2)\oplus\mathfrak u(1),
$$
so the Standard Model algebra is the continuous stabilizer algebra of the finite block-frame code, not an additional postulate.

**Summary of Theorem G.8.2e (Geometric Bound on Isotropic Generator Families)**
In the 24-dimensional symplectic tangent space, every $\omega$-isotropic subspace satisfies
$$
\dim L\le12,
$$
and a Lagrangian subspace attains equality. The independently certified predictive-recovery Golay branch also has $k=12$. Proposition G.8.2h constructs an Eisenstein complex-structure candidate, but equality of dimensions and the candidate alone do not identify the subspaces or establish a common physical carrier; an explicit isometry $F$ with $F\sigma=J_{\mathrm{QFI}}F$ and the Golay-subspace identification remain certificate entries.

**Summary of Lemma G.8.2c (Canonical Symplectic Decomposition)**
The QFI tangent space at the PCE-Attractor decomposes into $ab = 12$ canonical symplectic 2-planes:
$$
T_{\rho_0} = \bigoplus_{(\alpha,\beta) \in A \times B} \text{span}(X_{\alpha\beta}, Y_{\alpha\beta})
$$
with symplectic form $\omega(X_{\alpha\beta}, Y_{\alpha\beta}) = 1$.

**Summary of Definition G.8.2b (Symplectic Incompatibility Form)**
The QFI symplectic structure induces an incompatibility form $\omega$ on the space of estimable parameters. For directions generated by $G_1,G_2$,
$$
\omega(G_1,G_2)\ne0
$$
obstructs simultaneous attainment of the individual optimal estimation bounds; it does not make joint estimation impossible. An $\omega$-isotropic generator family avoids this obstruction, and Theorem G.8.2e bounds its dimension by $12$. A Lagrangian subspace attains that geometric bound, but identifying a physical jointly optimal family still requires the corresponding estimator and compatibility certificate.

**Summary of Remark G.8.2g (Numerical Symplectic--Golay Correspondence)**
The Lagrangian tangent-rank bound gives $12$, while the independently certified predictive-recovery Golay branch has code dimension $k=12$. Equality of dimensions does not identify the subspaces or establish a common physical carrier; that requires a separate intertwining certificate.

**Summary of Theorem G.8.4a (No Simple 12-Dimensional Compact Gauge Algebra)**
No compact real simple Lie algebra has real dimension $12$. Thus a compact gauge algebra saturating the certified real-dimension bound cannot be simple. Dimension alone determines neither its reductive factorization nor its matter realization. Excluding larger compact simple candidates additionally requires the certified capacity branch.

**Summary of Theorem G.8.4b (Finite-Response Module Decomposition Classification).**
Exhaustive classification of viable finite-response syndrome partitions of $b = 6$ under:

- Capacity constraint: $n_G \le 12$
- PPI quotient of response-null common phases
- Positive-marginal PCE saturation
- Full featureless $SU(n_i)$ frame symmetry on every retained nontrivial complex block

uniquely selects the $(3,2,1)$ decomposition in the capacity-saturating block-frame regime of Appendix G.8.4b. The non-abelian contribution is $8+3=11$, and exactly one abelian response direction remains because the Lagrangian capacity is $12$.

**Summary of Corollary G.8.4c (Gauge Algebra Dimension).**
$$
\dim(\mathfrak{g}_{\text{SM}}) = n_G = 12
$$
This saturates the capacity bound in the same regime and equals the Golay code signal dimension.

**Summary of Remark G.8.4d (Complementary Derivation Structure)**
Proposition G.M1 identifies the gauged algebra from the decomposition-preserving local symmetry, while Theorem G.8.4b derives the unique PCE-selected block-frame module decomposition from capacity, finite-response positivity, and determinant-compatible anomaly-admissibility inside the accepted block-frame family. **Summary of Theorem G.10.2 (Projective Realization of SPAP Involution and SU(2) Lift)**
The logical involution $\iota$ is realized as a nontrivial projective unitary involution:
$$
[U_\iota]^2=[I],
\qquad
[U_\iota]\ne[I].
$$
On the active kernel $\mathcal H_a\cong\mathbb C^2$, a determinant-one amplitude lift is
$$
U_\iota=i\sigma_x\in SU(2),
\qquad
U_\iota^2=-I,
$$
so the involution is exact on rays while its amplitude lift carries the double-cover sign.

**Summary of Theorem G.10.3 (Conditional $SU(2)$ Active-Kernel Lift)**
The full group of complex-linear unitary ray automorphisms is
$$
PU(2)=U(2)/U(1)\cong SO(3).
$$
If a registered connected physical projective-symmetry subgroup preserves transition probabilities and acts transitively on all pure active-kernel rays, then that subgroup is the full group $PU(2)$ and its simply connected determinant-one amplitude lift is $SU(2)$:
- global phase is response-null and quotiented;
- on the separate implementation branch of Theorem G.10.2, the SPAP involution is represented by the projective class of $i\sigma_x$;
- the registered subgroup's transitivity and connectedness force its Lie algebra to be $\mathfrak{so}(3)$;
- $SU(2)$ is the simply connected amplitude lift with kernel $\{\pm I\}$.

**Summary of Corollary G.10.4.1 (Spinor Structure)**
Fields transforming under SU(2) fundamental representation acquire sign flip under 2π rotation:
$$
U(2\pi, \hat{n}) = \exp(-i\pi \hat{n} \cdot \vec{\sigma}) = -I
$$
This is the defining property of spinors.

**Summary of Remark G.10.1 (Branch-Indexed Relation Between Two $\mathbb Z_2$ Structures)**
The SPAP logical involution and the kernel $\{I,-I\}$ of $SU(2)\to SO(3)$ are distinct objects. On the branch carrying the registered match/mismatch record, structural binary ledger, PPI/PCE no-surplus selection of $a=2$, and projective-unitary realization of Theorems G.10.2–G.10.4, the logical involution admits a determinant-one amplitude lift whose square is $-I$. This branch construction relates the two structures without identifying them.

**Summary of Theorem G.10.5 (Compatibility of the Spinor Sector with $Spin(1,3)$)**
Once Lorentzian $3+1$ geometry and the internal $SU(2)$ spin sector are independently present, the standard relativistic completion is $Spin(1,3)\cong SL(2,\mathbb C)$.

**Summary of Theorem G.10.6 (Conditional Active-Kernel Spinor--Mass Correspondence)**
On $\mathfrak B_{mass}$ and the direct active-kernel provenance/Lorentz branch, a field carrying that registered relational mass ledger transforms as a spinor; no existence or absolute-mass claim follows from SPAP alone.

A field with nontrivial $\mathcal I_{\mathrm{rel}}$ transforms as a spinor only on the intersection of the registered mass-ledger branch, the direct active-kernel provenance branch, and the Lorentzian spin bridge of Theorem G.10.6.

**Summary of Proposition G.10.7 (Fermionic Exchange Sign on the AQFT/Modular Branch)**
The active-kernel branch derives the spinor $2\pi$ sign,
$$
U(2\pi)=-I,
$$
but this sign alone does not prove exchange antisymmetry. The fermionic exchange sign
$$
\psi(x_1,x_2)=-\psi(x_2,x_1)
$$
is theorem-level only on the Appendix F AQFT/modular spin-statistics branch, where locality, positive-energy/modular transport, and the statistics operator identify half-integer spin with the sign representation.

The unordered two-particle configuration space in $\mathbb R^3$ has relative factor homotopy equivalent to $\mathbb{RP}^2$ and fundamental group $\mathbb Z_2$. This topology permits both $+1$ and $-1$ exchange characters; it does not by itself select the fermionic sign.

**Summary of Corollary G.10.8.1 (Conditional Spinor--Mass Compatibility)**
The spinor and mass ledgers are compatible only on the intersection of the registered Lorentz/active-kernel branch and $\mathfrak B_{mass}$. SPAP alone proves neither massive-fermion existence nor an absolute mass coefficient.

## Part VIII: Electroweak Sector

### Electroweak Scale

**Summary of Theorem T.5 (Marked-Pair Steiner Action, $\kappa_{\mathrm{St}}=38.5$)**
For the $759\times276$ octad-by-pair incidence matrix $B$ of $S(5,8,24)$,
$$
N:=B^{\mathsf T}B=72I+16A_1+5J,
$$
with spectrum $\{2156^{(1)},392^{(23)},40^{(252)}\}$ and diagonal entries $N_{pp}=77$. For a marked unit-pair endpoint, the unique fixed-time minimizer is $x_*(t)=te_p$ and
$$
\kappa_{\mathrm{St}}=\frac12e_p^{\mathsf T}Ne_p=\frac{77}{2}=38.5.
$$
This is the native finite action theorem. Only an accepted $\mathfrak C_{A\to\mathrm{St}}$ transports it to the covered electroweak action $\kappa_{EW}$; determinants, gauge volumes, and the residual orbit remain outside both classical actions.

**Summary of Corollary T.5c (Steiner Incidence Diagonal Identity)**
$$
2\kappa_{\mathrm{St}}=e_p^{\mathsf T}B^{\mathsf T}Be_p=\lambda_2(S(5,8,24))=77.
$$
Here $\lambda_2=\binom{22}{3}/\binom63=77$ counts octads through a fixed pair, and Theorem T.5 proves attainment by the straight unit-update path.

**Summary of Theorem T.5d.2 (Steiner Pair-Projector Trace Identity)**
Define $\mathcal N_{\mathrm{GS}}=B^{\mathsf T}B$ and the marked-pair Steiner projector $P_{\mathrm{St}}^{(2)}=|e_p\rangle\langle e_p|$. Then
$$
\operatorname{Tr}\!\left(P_{\mathrm{St}}^{(2)}\mathcal N_{\mathrm{GS}}\right)=77,
\qquad
\kappa_{\mathrm{St}}=\frac12\operatorname{Tr}\!\left(P_{\mathrm{St}}^{(2)}\mathcal N_{\mathrm{GS}}\right).
$$
This closes the native marked-pair gate without validation data. Its electroweak interpretation requires accepted $\mathfrak C_{A\to\mathrm{St}}$.

**Summary of Theorem T.6 (Electroweak Scale on the Full Action-Transport Branch)**
On the registered Steiner response-action branch of Theorem T.5 together with Principle T.13a and the semiclassical scale bridge of Principle T.6a, with $M_{Pl}$ in the unreduced convention and $A_{EW}$ the forward determinant, gauge-volume, and matching prefactor computed on the same saddle,

$$
\frac v{M_{Pl}}
=
A_{EW}e^{-\kappa_{EW}},
\qquad
\kappa_{EW}=\frac{77}{2},
$$

and therefore

$$
v
=
A_{EW}e^{-77/2}M_{Pl}.
$$

The Steiner counting identity alone does not determine $v$, and the residual-budget relation $S_{\mathrm{inst}}=2\kappa$ of Proposition T.60 belongs to the vacuum branch rather than to this scale bridge.

With M_Pl = 1.2209 × 10¹⁹ GeV and the determinant-model value A_EW ≈ 1.084:
$$
v = 1.084 \times e^{-38.5} \times 1.2209 \times 10^{19}\;\text{GeV} = 252\;\text{GeV}
$$

Experimental value: $v_{\text{exp}} = 246.22$ GeV (2.3% agreement).

### Weinberg Angle

**Summary of Theorem T.9 (Traceless Hypercharge)**
The hypercharge generator Y on the electroweak 5-plane W₅ = ℝ³ ⊕ ℝ² satisfies:
$$
Y = \text{diag}(y_c, y_c, y_c, y_w, y_w)
$$
with tracelessness constraint:
$$
3y_c + 2y_w = 0
$$

**Summary of Theorem T.14 (Weinberg Angle at the Matching Scale)**
Under the normalized block-scalar hypercharge and weak-generator branches of T.9--T.12 together with T.13's common-stiffness response-image certificate, the PU-normalized branch coordinate is $\sin^2\theta_{W,\mathrm{PU}}^{(0)}=3/8$. In SM-canonical normalization the matching value is
$$
\sin^2\theta_W(\mu_G)=\frac{3Z_2}{3Z_2+5Z_1},
$$
and equals $3/8$ only on the additional isotropic branch $Z_1=Z_2$. The PU-normalized coordinate and SM-canonical isotropic match are distinct claims.

**Summary of Theorem T.16 (Low-Energy Weinberg Angle)**
Starting from the SM-canonical matching value
$$
\sin^2\theta_W(\mu_G)=\frac{3Z_2}{3Z_2+5Z_1}
$$
at $\mu_G\sim 10^{15}\,\mathrm{GeV}$, with
$$
Z_i=1+\frac{\Delta_i}{24}
$$
determined by the lifted spectral problem of Theorem T.18 on $\widetilde X=\mathrm{Flag}_{1,2,3}(Q)$, one-loop SM running gives the Z-pole angle once the threshold tuple is supplied. Remark T.17a.4 and Proposition T.17a.5 show that the sector-independent local affine truncation cannot produce the required negative hypercharge-sector contribution, so the tuple must be supplied by the global sector-resolving $\mathrm{MS2}_{\mu_G}$ block functional rather than by local affine data.

**Summary of Theorem T.17 (Isotropy of Residual Thresholds)**
The leading common residual threshold correction $\delta_{\mathrm{avg}}$ is isotropic, and on the canonical minimal $\mathrm{MS2}_{\mu_G}$ ledger it is $\delta_{\mathrm{avg}}=0$. Thus residual shifts do not affect the separations $\alpha_i^{-1}-\alpha_j^{-1}$ that would be set by completed principal lifted spectral thresholds $\Delta_i$ (equivalently $Z_i$). Subleading anisotropic splittings require a nonminimal heavy ledger and are $O(M^{-1})$-suppressed (Corollary T.17.1). If an appended forward lifted-threshold computation of Theorem T.18 derives the validation tuple $(\Delta_1,\Delta_2,\Delta_3)=(15.14,20.94,18.41)$, the corresponding validation-run one-loop Z-pole prediction is $\sin^2\theta_W(M_Z)\approx 0.231$. The local affine truncation is ruled out by Proposition T.17a.5; the operative lifted-threshold calculation is the global sector-resolving block functional of Definition T.17a and Theorem T.70, while Theorem T.78.5 proves that the current canonical ledger does not supply the required positive branch package.

### Higgs Sector

**Summary of Theorem T.25 (Zero-Slack Cancellation on the Moment-Map-Normalized Target-Shift Branch)**
At the PU fixed point $\mathfrak{A}_{\text{PU}}$, the target-shift normalization $\gamma$ gives
$$
\lambda_{\text{PU}}(\mathfrak{A}_{\text{PU}};\gamma)=\frac{\gamma^2-1}{36}.
$$
The zero-slack boundary $\lambda_{\text{PU}}(\mathfrak{A}_{\text{PU}})=0$ holds only on the accepted moment-map-normalized branch $\gamma=1$. Theorem T.22b fixes $\gamma=1$ only after the even-displacement condition (T.22a.2) and the $L_{\mathrm{info}}$-norm datum (T.22a.3) are independently verified. Remark T.22c gives the unit-speed Pauli curve's even quadratic norm $1/4$. Theorem T.22d classifies the complete fixed-$u$ homogeneous Pauli family: its exact stabilizer/reservoir-isometry quotient is indexed by the response speed $\upsilon>0$, and $\gamma=\upsilon^2/4$. Stabilizer rotations fix the quadratic direction and reservoir isometries preserve its norm, so the equivalence does not remove $\upsilon$; rescaling $u$ is excluded because it changes the fixed QFI normalization. The natural unit-QFI member $\upsilon=1$ is therefore a non-rigidity counterexample. Direct substitution of the $\upsilon=2$, $F_Q=4$ curve supplies the formal unit coefficient on its distinct QFI branch. Resolution record T.22d-R1 resolves the forcing proposition, while Definition T.22a retains the separate response-preserving carrier/intertwiner gate for the accepted same-$u$ unit datum. These are abstract theory-space statements.

**Summary of Definition T.25.2 (PU-to-SM Quartic Matching Record)**
A running SM quartic boundary requires a separately accepted map from the PU theory-space coordinate to $\lambda_{\mathrm{SM}}(\mu_G)$, with normalization, scheme, threshold, and forward-lock data. The target-shift datum and threshold triplet do not supply this record by themselves.

**Summary of Theorem T.28 (Conditional Higgs Pole-Mass Map)**
On a forward-locked branch carrying a completed threshold record together with $\mathfrak H_T=(\mathcal M_\gamma,\mathfrak M_\lambda,\mathcal C_{\mathrm{crit}},\mathcal R_{\mathrm{RG}},\mathcal C_{\mathrm{dec}},\mathcal C_{\mathrm{pole}})$, a trajectory on the external SM metastability boundary maps to
$$
m_H \approx 125\;\text{GeV}.
$$
No accepted quartic matching record connects $\mathfrak A_{PU}$ to $\mu_G$ or the distinct crossing scale $\mu_\lambda$, so this is a validation value rather than a theorem-level PU prediction. Experimental value: $125.25\pm0.17$ GeV.

### Unified Coupling

**Summary of Theorem T.39a (Unified Coupling on the Bures-Gauge Calibration Branch)**
Let $\chi_U>0$ satisfy $\oint_\gamma\mathcal A_B=\chi_Ug_U^2$, and assume $M$ democratically assigned mode loops with Bures holonomy $2\pi/M$. Then
$$
g_U^2=\frac{2\pi}{M\chi_U}.
$$
On the additional branch $M=24$ and $\chi_U=1/2$,
$$
g_U^2=\frac{\pi}{6},\qquad\alpha_U^{-1}=\frac{4\pi}{g_U^2}=24.
$$

### Branch-Qualified PU Fixed-Point and Matching Ledger (Theorem T.35)

**Summary of Theorem T.35 (Branch-Qualified Electroweak Boundary Ledger)**
On the individually stated structural, normalization, and matching branches, Appendix T collects eight boundary entries. The lifted gauge-threshold data supply only their threshold slots; they do not supply the independent Higgs or flavor records:

| Condition | Value | Origin | Reference |
