### Conditional Coupling Construction on the Registered Appendix-Z Branches

**Summary of Theorem Z.14 (Bulk Normalization on the unit Predictive-Ward branch)**
At the PCE-Attractor, on the unit Predictive-Ward branch identifying $\mathcal G=\mathcal K^{-1}$ in QFI-natural units, Legendre duality and the predictive Ward identity give $\Gamma^{(2)}=\mathcal K$ on the QFI-active subspace. With $\lambda=1$ (Theorem Z.5) and $u^*$ fixed by capacity saturation (Theorem Z.7), the remaining $U(1)$ field-rescaling freedom is obstructed and the bulk normalization is uniquely fixed:

**Summary of Theorem Z.14e (Thomson-Ward Unit-Jacobian Closure)**
On a branch carrying an accepted Thomson-Ward spectral-weight certificate $\mathfrak W_T$ (Definition Z.14d), the standard $f$-sum/TRK identity gives
$$
\int_0^{\Omega_{\mathrm{PW}}}\frac{d\omega}{\pi}w_T(\omega)\operatorname{Im}\chi_{JJ}^{\mathrm{op}}(\omega)=\mathcal Q_{\mathrm{PU}}=a=2,
$$
while the QFI-canonical Ward map evaluates the same integral as $c_{\mathrm W}^{-1}\mathcal Q_{\mathrm{PU}}$. Comparison forces $c_{\mathrm W}=1$ and closes the unit Predictive-Ward normalization $\kappa^*_{\mathrm{bulk}}=1$ from operationally accessible spectral data on that branch.
$$
\kappa^*_{\mathrm{bulk}} = 1
$$

**Summary of Proposition Z.14f (Finite-Rank Kubo--QFI Spectral Identity)**
Let $\dim A=2$, $\rho_0=P_A/2$, and let $O$ be Hermitian. If $H=H_A\oplus H_B$ commutes with $\rho_0$, every $B$-energy is strictly above every $A$-energy, and $\Omega$ includes every positive $A\to B$ transition, then
$$
\frac4\pi\int_0^\Omega\chi''_{OO}(\omega)\,d\omega
=F_Q[\rho_0,O]
=2\|P_AOP_B\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.
$$
This finite identity does not identify $O$ with the operational electromagnetic current or discharge Definition Z.14d.

**Summary of Theorem Z.17 (Interface Correction on the Canonical First-Order Interface-Derivative Branch)**
On the canonical first-order interface-derivative branch, the discrete-to-continuous embedding correction is:
$$
\delta\kappa = -\frac{a}{d_0} \cdot \frac{u^*}{\sqrt{K_0}} = -\frac{1}{4} \cdot \frac{u^*}{\sqrt{3}}.
$$
This entry inherits the unit bulk-Ward branch of Theorem Z.14 and the projected response-sign branch of Theorem Z.16.

**Summary of Theorem Z.23 (Intrinsic Bures Curvature)**
The mean sectional curvature of the Bures metric on $\operatorname{Gr}(2,8)$ is
$$
K_{\text{avg}}^{\text{Bures}}=\frac{32}{23}.
$$
Proposition Z.23b gives the rank-one full Jacobi spectrum $\{8^{(1)},2^{(12)},0^{(11)}\}$ and the equal-singular-value rank-two spectrum $\{4^{(4)},1^{(16)},0^{(4)}\}$. The two spectra differ, so the full Jacobi spectrum is direction-dependent, whereas its Ricci trace is the isotropic value $32$.

**Summary of Theorem Z.24 (Conditional Visible-Response Curvature Factor)**
Introduce the independent response coefficient $L_{\mathrm{vis}}$ by
$$
K_{\mathrm{eff}}
:=
(M-1)K_{\text{avg}}^{\text{Bures}}L_{\mathrm{vis}}.
$$
On the democratic visible-response branch $L_{\mathrm{vis}}=1/(ad_0)$,
$$
K_{\mathrm{eff}}
=
\frac{32}{23}\frac{23}{16}
=2.
$$
Theorem Z.23 supplies the intrinsic curvature; it does not determine $L_{\mathrm{vis}}$ or the electromagnetic response projection.

**Summary of Lemma Z.14 (Conditional Normalized Chern-Flux Branch)**
On the standard complex orientation of the Schubert generator, the tautological rank-$a$ bundle $S$ has
$$
\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}c_1(\det S)=-1,
\qquad
\int_{\mathbb{CP}^1}c_1((\det S)^*)=1.
$$
The positive unit-flux convention is therefore realized by the dual determinant line, or by the determinant line with reversed orientation. The sign is fixed only after the line bundle and orientation are selected, and this choice does not select an electromagnetic charge line.

**Summary of Theorem Z.26 (Conditional Sinc-Core Evaluation)**
The Thomson-limit fine-structure certificate core is:
$$
\alpha^{-1}_{0}
=
\frac{4\pi}{u^*}
-
\frac{\pi}{\sqrt{K_0}}
+
\frac{\pi u^*}{24\sqrt{K_0}}\operatorname{sinc}(u^*),
\qquad
\operatorname{sinc}(u)=\frac{\sin u}{u}.
$$

where:

### Alphabet-Constant Identities (Appendix W)

Appendix W derives convexity, Jensen-cap, and spectral-gap identities for its declared nonnegative finite-spectrum rate-level potential. Coupling equalities apply only on the corresponding cap-saturated flat-spectrum branches, and electroweak sector comparisons additionally inherit Assumption W.3.A.

**Rate-Level PCE Potential (Equation W.0.1; Section W.0)**
$$
\phi(u) = A_{\text{PCE}} u^2 - \Gamma_0 \sum_{i=1}^{M} \ln(1 + \lambda_i u), \quad u = g^2 \ge 0
$$
where $\{\lambda_i\}$ are the SLD-QFI eigenvalues of the probe channel at $g = 0$.

**Spectral Statistics (Equations W.0.2–W.0.4; Section W.0)**
$$
S_1 = \sum_i \lambda_i, \quad S_2 = \sum_i \lambda_i^2, \quad x = \frac{S_1}{M}, \quad \sigma^2 = \frac{S_2}{M} - x^2
$$

**Summary of Definition W.7 (Alphabet Constant)**
Given $d_0$ and $M$:
$$
a_{\mathrm{cap}} = d_0^{1/M}, \quad D_{\mathrm{cap}} = a_{\mathrm{cap}}(a_{\mathrm{cap}} - 1), \quad K_{\mathrm{alph}}(d_0, M) = \frac{a_{\mathrm{cap}} - 1}{4\pi}\left(1 + \frac{1}{D_{\mathrm{cap}}}\right)
$$

For $(d_0,M)=(8,24)$: $a_{\mathrm{cap}}=8^{1/24}=2^{1/8}=1.0905077327\ldots$, $D_{\mathrm{cap}}=0.0986993823\ldots$, and $K_{\mathrm{alph}}(8,24)=0.0801752397\ldots$.

**Summary of Definition W.9 (Spectral Form Factor)**
$$
F_\lambda := \frac{Mx^2}{S_2} = \frac{1}{1 + \sigma^2/x^2} \in (0, 1]
$$
Measures deviation from flat spectrum; $F_\lambda = 1$ iff $\sigma^2 = 0$.

**Summary of Lemma W.1 (Strict Convexity)**
With $\gamma_{\text{eff}} = 2$, $\phi''(u) > 0$ for all $u \ge 0$. Hence $\phi$ is strictly convex with unique constrained minimizer.

**Summary of Lemma W.2 (Jensen-Cap Boundary)**
When the Jensen capacity bound is active:
$$
u^*_J = \frac{a_{\text{cap}} - 1}{x}, \quad \text{where } a_{\text{cap}} = d_0^{1/M}
$$

**Summary of Lemma W.3 (Gap Monotonicity)**
The capacity gap $\Delta_{\text{cap}}(u) := g_J(u) - g_{\text{true}}(u) \ge 0$ and $\Delta'_{\text{cap}}(u) \ge 0$ for all $u \ge 0$.

**Summary of Lemma W.4 (Quadratic Gap Bound)**
For $\lambda_{\min} = \min_i \lambda_i$ and all $u \ge 0$:
$$
0 \le \Delta_{\text{cap}}(u) \le \frac{M\sigma^2 u^2}{2(1 + \lambda_{\min}u)^2}
$$

**Summary of Theorem W.5 (Ordering of Optima)**
Under active capacity constraints: $u^*_T \ge u^*_J$ (true capacity optimum exceeds Jensen cap).

**Summary of Theorem W.10 (Alphabet Constant Bound and Conditional Identity)**
At the Jensen-cap-saturated optimum:
$$
\boxed{\alpha_{\text{em}}(\text{MPU}) \cdot \frac{C_{\text{cyc}}}{C_{\text{cap}}} \le K_{\mathrm{alph}}(d_0, M_e) \cdot \frac{1}{F_{\lambda,e}}}
$$
**Equality** holds iff the spectrum is flat ($F_\lambda = 1$) and the unconstrained minimizer lies on the cap boundary ($u_0 = u_{\mathrm J}^*$). Flatness is supplied on the stated PCE-Attractor branch; the cap-boundary equality is an independent condition.

**Map entry: Lemma W.2 + Capacity Saturation (Coupling-Alphabet Identity)**
At the PCE-Attractor on the unit-flat QFI branch $\lambda_i=1$ for every $i$,
$$
\alpha^{-1}=\frac{4\pi}{u^*}=\frac{4\pi}{d_0^{1/M}-1}.
$$
For $d_0=8$ and $M=24$, $u^*=2^{1/8}-1=0.0905077327\ldots$, yielding $\alpha^{-1}=138.8430606\ldots$ for the bulk term. For a uniform spectrum $\lambda_i=\lambda\ne1$, the corresponding formulas are $u^*=(d_0^{1/M}-1)/\lambda$ and $\alpha^{-1}=4\pi\lambda/(d_0^{1/M}-1)$.

**Summary of Corollary W.17 (Lower Bound on $r_e$ and Variance Effect)**
With $r_e=S_1/(2\tilde A_{\rm PCE}+S_2)$,
$$
r_e\ge
\frac{1}{x}\frac{1}{(1+\sigma^2/x^2)(1+1/D)}.
$$
Increasing spectral variance decreases this lower bound and enlarges the upper bound in Equation (W.5.1) through $1/F_\lambda=1+\sigma^2/x^2$. Corollary W.17 supplies no bound on $|\alpha^{-1}-\alpha^{-1}_{\mathrm{uniform}}|$.

### SLD-QFI Existence (Theorem W.18)

**Summary of Theorem W.18 (SLD Existence and QFI Quadratic Form)**
Let $\{E_g\}_{g \in \mathbb{R}}$ be a $C^1$ family of CPTP maps on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, let $\rho_0$ be stationary at $g=0$, and let $P$ be its support projector. Assume $\rho_0$ is full rank on $\operatorname{Ran}(P)$ and the tangent satisfies $P\dot\rho P=\dot\rho$. Then the SLD exists as a Hermitian operator supported on $\operatorname{Ran}(P)$, the QFI is finite, and the SLD-based QFI is a positive semidefinite quadratic form on the supported tangent space.

*Dependency sketch:* On supported Hermitian operators the Jordan map $\mathcal J_{\rho_0}(X)=(\rho_0X+X\rho_0)/2$ is positive definite and invertible. The support condition permits $L=\mathcal J_{\rho_0}^{-1}(\dot\rho)$, extended by zero on $\ker\rho_0$, and $F_Q=\operatorname{tr}(\rho_0L^2)\ge0$. ∎

### Gauge Mode Counting at PCE-Attractor (Section W.3a)

**Summary of Proposition W.3a.2 (Flat Spectrum)**
At the PCE-Attractor, the QFI spectrum is flat:
$$
\lambda_i = \lambda_0 = 1 \quad \forall i \in \{1, \ldots, M\}
$$

**Summary of Definition W.3a.1 (Electroweak Mode Sector)**
- $M_2 = \dim[\mathfrak{su}(2)] = 3$ (weak isospin generators)
- $M_Y = \dim[\mathfrak{u}(1)] = 1$ (hypercharge generator)
- $M_{\text{EW}} = M_2 + M_Y = 4$ (total electroweak modes)

**Summary of Remark W.3a.4 (Mode Ratio)**
At the PCE-Attractor with uniform per-generator capacity:
$$
\frac{M_2}{M_Y} = \frac{3}{1} = 3
$$

### Topological Mechanism

**Summary of Theorem R.1.1 (Homotopy of Perspective Space)**
$$
\pi_2(\Sigma_8) \cong \mathbb{Z}^7
$$
providing seven independent integer topological charges.

**Summary of Theorem R.3.1 (Marked Torus-Character Map)**
A registered marked embedding induces
$$
\Phi:\pi_2(\Sigma_8)\longrightarrow X^*(T_{\mathrm{SM}}).
$$
The representation and ordered torus characters are part of the certificate.

**Summary of Proposition R.3.3 (Center Neutrality on the Cartan-Neutral Candidate Sublattice)**
The kernel $L_{\mathrm{light}}=\ker_{\mathbb Z}C$ of Definition R.3.2 has zero semisimple Cartan projection, and Proposition R.3.3 gives trivial $SU(3)$ center charge; the $SU(2)$ center character is likewise trivial. Full $SU(3)\times SU(2)$ singletness additionally requires the trivial-isotypic/root-action certificate; no vanishing hypercharge claim is made.

**Summary of Theorem R.3.4 (Family-Charge Anomaly Constraints on the SM15 and SM16 Descent Branches)**
On SM15, anomaly descent gives $\sum_gF_g=\sum_gF_g^3=0$, which at the smallest CP-capable count selects $\{a,-a,0\}$. On SM16, anomaly descent gives only $\sum_gF_g=0$; primitive normalization and the strict charge-norm objective select the same orbit. Proposition R.3.5.1a separately selects the realized count under its additive-monotone family objective. Larger-family solutions remain outside that selection.

## Part XIV: Prediction Relativity

### PCE Potential Branch Schema

**Section N.1 (Certificate-Relative PCE Potential)**
$$
V_i = \underbrace{V_{op}(i)}_{\text{Operational Cost}} + \underbrace{V_{prop}(i)}_{\text{Propagation Cost}} - \underbrace{V_{benefit}(i)}_{\text{Predictive Benefit}}.
$$
The summands are rates only on a branch with a common unit and metering record. The stochastic gradient model
$$
dx(t)=-\eta(x)\nabla V(x)\,dt+\sqrt{2D(x)}\,dW(t)
$$
is shorthand for
$$
dx_t=-\eta(x_t)\operatorname{grad}_gV(x_t)\,dt+\sigma(x_t)\,dW_t,
\qquad \sigma\sigma^*=2D,
$$
and is nominated only after a smooth state manifold $X$, Riemannian metric $g$, tangent mobility $\eta_x:T_xX\to T_xX$, diffusion factor $\sigma_x:\mathbb R^m\to T_xX$ with $\sigma_x\sigma_x^*=2D_x$, coefficient regularity and domains, filtered $m$-dimensional Brownian motion, boundary behavior, and either a Stratonovich convention or an Itô convention with the required connection/chart data are fixed. Units and metering are separate required entries. Entropy per reset becomes a rate only after a reset frequency is supplied, and physical power additionally needs the registered energy conversion. The formula does not by itself prove well-posedness, minimizer existence or attainment, uniqueness, ergodicity, or convergence. Theorem N.1a supplies all of those conclusions, including an exact contraction rate and invariant law, for the complete constant anisotropic quadratic package on $\mathbb R^d$; it does not construct the physical MPU state or derive its coefficients.

### Divergence Laws

**Map entry: Theorem 14 (Predictive Complexity Divergence Near $\alpha_{SPAP}$)**
Fix a registered task, score, admissible class, quantitative boundary $\alpha_{SPAP}$, and preregistered target $\alpha_{\mathrm{tgt}}$. Under certificate $\mathfrak C_{B.2}$, if $\delta_{SPAP}:=\alpha_{SPAP}-\alpha_{\mathrm{tgt}}\downarrow0$, then the worst-case verification/update operation count satisfies
$$
C_{\text{uni}}(\delta_{SPAP}) = \Omega\left(\frac{\log(1/\delta_{SPAP})}{\delta_{SPAP}^2}\right).
$$
The same asymptotic lower bound transfers to $C_P(\alpha_{\mathrm{tgt}})$ only when a separate bridge proves $C_P(\alpha_{\mathrm{tgt}})\ge C_{\text{uni}}(\delta_{SPAP})$ for the same implementation ledger. An observed $PP$ may replace $\alpha_{\mathrm{tgt}}$ only under a registered identification retaining the confidence clause.

**Summary of Theorem B.2 (Certified Two-Point Complexity Bound)**
Under $\mathfrak C_{B.2}$, every admissible $\delta_{SPAP}$-accurate strategy must calibrate or verify the registered bounded success indicator to additive accuracy $\delta_{SPAP}$ with failure probability at most $\delta_{SPAP}$. The two-point testing reduction yields
$$
C_{\mathrm{uni}}(\delta_{SPAP})
=
\Omega\left(\frac{\log(1/\delta_{SPAP})}{\delta_{SPAP}^2}\right)
$$
as $\delta_{SPAP}\downarrow0$. The conclusion is task-, class-, and certificate-relative; SPAP by itself supplies neither $\alpha_{SPAP}$ nor this cost bound.

Corollary B.2.2 gives the finite certified floor
$$
\operatorname{Cost}_{\mathrm{integrate}}(S,E)
\ge
\frac{3c_s}{128}\mu_S(E)^2
\ln\!\left(\frac{\mu_S(E)}4\right)
$$
when $\mu_S(E)\ge8$ and the uniform reduction retains Theorem B.2's constants. A finite contradiction requires a fixed-path count or a verified all-path upper bound below this floor; an average, regression, or single execution does not test the worst-case theorem.

**Summary of Definition B.2 (Unified Complexity Functional)**
$$
C_{uni}(\delta_{SPAP}) := \inf_{S\ \delta_{SPAP}\text{-accurate}} \mathrm{Cost}(S;\delta_{SPAP}),
$$
where $\mathrm{Cost}(S;\delta_{SPAP})$ counts elementary physical operations, including acquisition of verification outcomes and internal update steps (Appendix B.3, Eq B.4).

**Equation N.3 (Relativistic Divergence)**
$$
\mathcal{E}_{\text{kin}}(v) = m_0c^2(\gamma(v)-1), \qquad \gamma(v)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-v^2/c^2}}
$$

If invariant mass and internal stored energy return to their initial values, emitted comoving momentum is isotropic, and the predictive-loss ledger is disjoint from kinetic energy, then

W_{\mathrm{tot}}^{\mathrm{lab}}
\ge
m_0c^2(\gamma_f-1)
+\int_0^{\tau_f}\gamma(\tau)R_{\mathrm{com}}(\tau)\,d\tau.
$$
Changes in invariant mass, internal energy, or emitted momentum require additional explicit terms.

### The Conditional Unruh–Landauer Coefficient Calibration

**Equation N.17 (Saturation Calibration, Section N.10.3)**
$$
\boxed{c_\gamma=c_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{sat}}=c}
$$

**Summary of Definition N.5 (Unruh–Landauer Saturation Coefficient).** On the declared detector-response, additive-temperature, and Landauer-saturating refresh branch,
$$
c_\varepsilon^{\mathrm{sat}}
:=
\frac{\hbar\varepsilon_0 aC}{2\pi E_{\mathrm{pred}}^{\mathrm{sat}}(a,C)}.
$$
The equality follows because the imported Unruh formula already contains $c$. For an actual nonsaturating dissipation, the analogous diagnostic is at most $c$. Equation N.17 therefore does not derive a second epistemic speed, identify the SPAP and kinematic limits, or supply the independent causal-frontier hypotheses of Theorem 46.

**Summary of Proposition N.10.3a (No Margolus-Levitin/Gamma Product Bound)**
Equation (N.17) is a saturation calibration using the $c$ already present in the Unruh formula, not a joint saturation condition for relativistic motion and internal orthogonalization. The valid covariant quantum-speed-limit statement is

\qquad
\tau_{\mathrm{lab}}=\gamma\tau_0,
\qquad
E_{\mathrm{ML}}\frac{\tau_{\mathrm{lab}}}{\gamma}\ge\frac{\pi\hbar}{2}.

After normalization by $m c^2$ this becomes

$$
so no universal lower bound for $(E_{\mathrm{ML}}/mc^2)/\gamma$ follows from Theorem N.3, Definition N.5, or Equation (N.17).

**Summary of Remark (Calibration and Causal-Frontier Separation):** Equation N.17 does not derive $c$, $\tau_{\min}$, or $\delta$. Theorem 29 supplies a task-dependent characteristic timescale, not a universal positive edge-update duration. ## Part XV: Spacetime and Gravity

### Temporal Structure and Arrow of Time

**Summary of Theorem O.1 (Conditional PCE Penalty for Desynchronization)**
Assume a finite connected interaction graph, a declared synchronization-defect functional, strictly positive local penalty coefficients on active edges, and the propagation/benefit comparison specified in O.1. The desynchronization contribution to $V=V_{op}+V_{prop}-V_{benefit}$ is nonnegative and is strictly positive when the registered defect is nonzero on an active edge. No universal temporal-coherence penalty follows without this ledger.

**Summary of Theorems O.2--O.2a (Synchronization and Lifted-Phase Frustration)**
If the graph is connected, every edge phase penalty is nonnegative and vanishes exactly at zero phase difference, all remaining potential terms are phase-independent, and the compactness, ergodicity, detailed-balance, and low-noise hypotheses hold, the phase-sector minimizers are the globally synchronized configurations up to one common phase and $\pi_\beta(U)\to1$ for every neighborhood $U$ of that set as $\beta\to\infty$. For every finite weighted lifted quadratic offset model, Theorem O.2a gives the full pseudoinverse minimizer fiber, cycle-space frustration residual, disconnected-component shifts and spectral gradient-flow rate. Its exact triangle witness has a zero-cost nonsynchronous state, so synchronization is not universal once nonzero edge offsets are admitted. Periodic nonlinear penalties and the physical MPU edge law remain open.

**Summary of Theorem O.3 (Conditional Pathwise Arrow Bound)**
Suppose a coherent macroscopic step consists of $N$ cycles with forward and reversed path measures on the same event algebra,
$$
\Sigma_{\mathrm{tot}}=\log\frac{P_F}{P_R}=\sum_{k=1}^N\sigma_k,
$$
and suppose a preregistered pathwise certificate gives $\sigma_k\ge h_{\min}>0$ for every selected cycle. Then
$$
\frac{P_R}{P_F}\le e^{-Nh_{\min}}.
$$
The specialization $P_R/P_F\le2^{-N}$ requires $h_{\min}\ge\ln2$. Conditional Landauer bounds reset heat but does not itself supply this pathwise stochastic-entropy hypothesis.

**Summary of Theorem O.3a (Conditional Single-Cycle Irreversibility Bound)**
Let $c$ and $c^\dagger$ have forward and reversed weights on the same event algebra and suppose
$$
\sigma(c)=\log\frac{P_F(c)}{P_R(c^\dagger)}.
$$
If an independent guarantee-level certificate gives
$$
\sigma(c)\ge h_{\min}>0,
$$
then
$$
\frac{P_R(c^\dagger)}{P_F(c)}
=e^{-\sigma(c)}
\le e^{-h_{\min}}.
$$
In particular, a factor $1/2$ requires $h_{\min}\ge\ln2$. The registered-reset heat identity of Appendix J does not supply the pathwise stochastic-entropy hypothesis.

**Summary of Corollary O.3a.4 (Forward-Locked Orientation Ledger)**
For an oriented involution-paired event algebra with entropy production
$$
\sigma(e)=\log\frac{P_F(e)}{P_R(e^\dagger)},
$$
the forward-oriented expectation is the pairwise antisymmetric ledger
$$
\langle\chi\rangle_F=\sum_{[e]}\chi(e)(P_F(e)-P_F(e^\dagger)).
$$
If the pathwise guarantee $\sigma(e)\ge\varepsilon_0=\ln2$ holds on a pair, then
$$
\frac{P_R(e^\dagger)}{P_F(e)}
=e^{-\sigma(e)}
\le e^{-\varepsilon_0}
=\frac12.
$$

**Fluctuation Theorem and Source-Rate Completion.** On a microreversible branch carrying an integral fluctuation relation,
$$
\langle e^{-\Sigma_{\mathrm{pred}}}\rangle=1,
$$
Jensen's inequality gives only $\langle\Sigma_{\mathrm{pred}}\rangle\ge0$. A registered reset separately obeys the Appendix J average heat ledger; it does not imply a strict trajectorywise lower bound. On a branch carrying the three-term resolution of Theorem O.3b,
$$
\Sigma_{\mathrm{PU}}=\beta Q+N_{\mathrm{SPAP}}\ln2+\Delta\Phi_{\mathrm{PCE}},
$$
the cumulant and rate conclusions require the stated path-measure, integrability, stationarity, and Foster--Harris hypotheses. If an independent pathwise certificate gives $\sigma_k\ge h_{\min}>0$, then $P_R/P_F\le e^{-Nh_{\min}}$; the specialization $2^{-N}$ requires $h_{\min}\ge\ln2$.

The statistical arrow is therefore conditional on the chosen forward/reverse event algebra, path measures, entropy-production resolution, and any pathwise certificate. Reset heat alone does not select those data or eliminate initial-state and boundary assumptions.

**Summary of Theorem O.3i (Finite Bidirectional Markov Arrow).** For every finite irreducible stationary chain with bidirectional support, the forward and stationary reverse laws are mutually absolutely continuous. The exact edgewise likelihood ratio gives a source-separated entropy-production rate
$$
\sigma=\sum_{\substack{i<j\\q_{ij}>0}}(\pi_iq_{ij}-\pi_jq_{ji})
\log\frac{\pi_iq_{ij}}{\pi_jq_{ji}}
\ge\sum_{\substack{i<j\\q_{ij}>0}}\frac{2(\pi_iq_{ij}-\pi_jq_{ji})^2}{\pi_iq_{ij}+\pi_jq_{ji}}.
$$
It vanishes exactly at detailed balance and decomposes without double counting over every preregistered partition of the undirected edges. A physical MPU/bath/source realization remains separate; in particular the equilibrium predicate of `TV-M-02` is not an arrow theorem.

### Computational-Thermodynamic Asymmetry Boundary (Section O.5.2)

Section O.5.2 separates five typed coordinates: SPAP diagonal obstruction, verifier-versus-witness generation, fixed-family average-case inversion, protocol-specific reset cost, and forward/reverse path asymmetry. Log-support bounds entropy but not search time. The no-proxy theorem prevents SPAP, Landauer cost, or a large phase space from being relabeled as one-wayness. For the one-way-function route developed there, a finite proof-carrying certificate for one fixed uniform function family is the separation gate: if unconditionally accepted, its prefix-search theorem yields $\mathsf{P}\ne\mathsf{NP}$; no current certificate is accepted.

### Scoped Relativization and Certificate Status (Section O.5.3)

Section O.5.3 proves a relativization barrier only for oracle-stable premises connected by steps separately certified oracle-natural; merely admissible bridges are not presumed to relativize. It registers certificate audit outcomes without classifying every possible route, proves a deterministic fixed-exponent all-image obstruction, and gives a weak $\mathsf{AC}^0$ inversion bound that does not discharge negligible inversion. Theorem O.5.3j compiles every fixed uniform PPT inverter into an exact uniform reversible NOT/CNOT/Toffoli circuit with clean ancillas, polynomial overhead and identical fair-coin output law. This does not realize those gates, coins or polynomial $C_P$ on a PU carrier. A quantified PPT-to-PU simulation certificate combined with the marked Physical Hardness Postulate yields $\mathsf{P}\ne\mathsf{NP}$ only conditionally. The OWF, direct-separation, and PPT-to-PU simulation certificates all remain $\varnothing_{\mathrm{cert}}$.

### Temporal Waves and CC Substrate (Section O.6)

**Summary of Definition O.1 (Temporal Wave)**
A propagating solution for a registered local cycle-rate or phase field under a specified evolution equation, initial/boundary data, and causal-response law. The definition alone identifies no electromagnetic or gravitational carrier.

**Summary of Remark O.4 and Theorem O.6a (Temporal-Wave Response Boundary)**
The gravitational-wave reading belongs to the separately accepted linearized Einstein/spin-2 branch. Theorem O.6a constructs a distinct exact CC alternative on the bounded scalar class: $\Box_c\phi=J$ maps injectively to the normalized qubit probability $p_+=(1+\kappa\phi)/2$, the replacement-channel difference satisfies Definition 30, and the inverse, field equation, cone and scalar-source/qubit-control ownership commute exactly. It does not identify the scalar with a TT gravitational wave or supply a simultaneous arrow--CC--gravity carrier. The latter coexistence predicate remains owned independently by `TV-O-10`.

### Γ-Convergence and Metric Emergence (Section O.7)

**Section O.7.1 (Spatial Sector).** On the joint Theorem-D.6e Mosco/Cheeger-identification and Theorem-44a positivity/regularity branch, the limiting quadratic form determines a Riemannian spatial metric; Theorem D.6 alone does not imply this conclusion:

Let $u_n:V(G_n)\to\mathbb R$ be a discrete test field. A representative spatial functional is
$$
F_n(u_n)=\sum_{(x,y)\in E(G_n)} w_{xy}\,\Phi_{link}\!\left(\frac{u_n(y)-u_n(x)}{h_n}\right)+\sum_{x\in V(G_n)} h_n^D \mathcal{V}(u_n(x)),
$$
which $\Gamma$-converges to
$$
F(u)=\int_M f(x,\nabla u)\,d^Dx+\int_M \mathcal{V}(u)\,d^Dx,\qquad f(x,\xi)=\xi^T A(x)\xi+o(|\xi|^2),
$$
with $A(x)$ positive definite, defining the spatial metric $g_{ij}(x)$ via $A(x)\propto \sqrt{\det g_{ij}}\,g^{ij}(x)$.

**Section O.7.2 (Time Direction and Lorentzian Signature)**
- **Time orientation:** Hypothesis O.7.2.2 independently supplies a nonvanishing time covector whose orientation is fixed on the Theorem-O.3 common-event-algebra branch by a positive pathwise entropy-production certificate. A registered reset, positive conditional reset entropy, or detailed balance does not supply that orientation.
- **Finite propagation:** Proposition F.1 gives a discrete Lieb--Robinson bound only under its finite-range, bounded-strength, and bounded-incidence hypotheses. Corollary F.1 yields continuum microcausality only with its geometric convergence, cone normalization, embedding, dynamics-convergence, support-growth, and positive spacelike-margin hypotheses; Definition 27 alone supplies none of these.

On the complete O.7 branch, the positive spatial block $A(x)$ is supplemented by an explicitly assumed local time coordinate carrying the O.3 pathwise orientation, a second-order continuum principal symbol, and an attained nondegenerate operational frontier identified with the characteristic cone. Theorem O.7a then gives, up to the stated conformal normalization,
$$
g^{\mu\nu}\xi_\mu\xi_\nu=-\frac{\xi_0^2}{c(x)^2}+\xi^\top A(x)\xi.
$$
Together with the rank-four continuum realization and nonvanishing temporal covector, whose kernel has rank three by rank-nullity, this has signature $(-,+,+,+)$. Appendix Z supplies only the four-dimensional Euclidean carrier. The O.3 certificate orients an already supplied cone; it does not determine the sign pattern.

**Section O.7 (Conditional Lorentzian Closure)**
The spatial $\Gamma$-limit supplies only the positive spatial sector. A Lorentzian continuum action is available after the independent time-coordinate, second-order-symbol, attained-frontier/characteristic-cone, and dimensional hypotheses above are accepted. $\Gamma$-convergence or irreversibility alone does not produce that action.

**Past-Hypothesis Contrast (Section 14.2.2; Appendix O)**
Appendix O derives a probability-level time orientation only for histories carrying forward and reverse measures on a common event algebra and the independent pathwise entropy-production certificate of Theorem O.3. The registered binary-support and physical-reset ledgers may motivate an irreversibility comparison but do not discharge that certificate; SPAP alone requires no merge. Low-boundary language is theorem-level only as a retained-algebra conditioning statement under $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{ret}}^{(q)}$: the earliest retained response algebra is small, while non-retained microscopic entropy is not determined by this claim.

### Retrocausality Exclusion (Section 14.2.2)

**Interpretive Classification under Artifact Identification.** PU's critique of quantum interpretations uses two layered criteria. The *temporal* criterion of §14.2.2 directly excludes only the retrocausal, two-boundary, and strongly all-at-once quantum ontologies; Copenhagen, Everettian, Bohmian, collapse, QBist, relational, histories, modal, and statistical families are *not* excluded on temporal grounds, and superdeterminist completions are nearby but distinct. Pre/post-selected and weak-measurement formalisms are preserved as operational conditioning rules unless promoted to ontic future-boundary causes (Corollary M.6.14d). The *Artifact Identification Criterion* of §14.2.4 applies separately: interpretive surplus whose specification would require violating $C_{\max} < \ln d_0$, $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{phys}}\ge H_q(P\mid R)\quad(\text{registered reset branch; a positive floor requires }H_q(P\mid R)\ge h_{\min}>0)$, SPAP, or $\delta > 0$ is, under this convention, treated as a feature of the mathematical description rather than of the physically instantiated branch. The Artifact Identification Criterion is an explicit PU admissibility convention, not an empirical no-go theorem: under it, the ontological surplus of Many-Worlds branching, fundamental-continuum Bohmian configuration space, continuously tunable GRW parameters, ontic future boundaries, and conspiratorial setting-hidden-variable coordination is classified as unadmitted surplus — while the operational content of each interpretation, being PU-admissible, is preserved. Measurement-dependence or branch-admissibility readings are treated separately: PU does not identify every denial of independent setting variation with conspiracy; it asks whether the proposed setting variation is a finite-response, branch-preserving counterfactual.

The PU framework distinguishes operational cycle order from retrocausal control.

**Layer 1: Definitional Operational Order**
The Fundamental Predictive Loop (Definition 4) orders its registered operations as
$$
P_{int}\longrightarrow V\longrightarrow D_{cyc}.
$$
This order defines what the manuscript calls a completed predictive cycle. It does not by itself exclude retrocausal correlations, advanced boundary data, or every retrocausal ontology. Theorem 14.1 separately excludes an intervention-stable paradox-enabling control channel whose predictive guarantee remains valid under the receiver's diagonal response policy.

**Layer 2: Conditional Physical Reset Ledger**
Lemma J.1 gives a two-to-one structural merge only in the declared binary-ancilla architecture. If an implementation subsequently executes a registered cyclic reset, Theorem J.1 gives
$$
\frac{\langle Q_{\mathrm{bath}}\rangle}{k_BT}
=H_q(P\mid R)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}},
\qquad
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}\ge0.
$$
A stochastic estimate for temporal reversal requires an independently specified path measure and fluctuation theorem; no value such as $2^{-N}$ follows from Lemma J.1 or Theorem J.1.

**Information-Theoretic Scope:** On the refresh branch, Theorem E.2 bounds the amount of classical information transmitted per ND-RID channel use. It is invariant under relabeling the direction of communication and therefore does not exclude retrocausal channels. Any PU exclusion of retrocausality is conditional on the independently declared causal-order postulates and admissible-history branch.

| Framework | Position on Retrocausality |
|:----------|:---------------------------|
| **Standard QM** | Agnostic (time-symmetric equations) |
| **Ontic retrocausal QM** (TI; ontic readings of TSVF) | Treats future boundary data or advanced waves as causal inputs rather than only post-selected conditioning |
| **PU Framework** | Excluded on its declared causal-order branch; not derived from channel capacity alone |

**Causal-Branch Falsification Criterion:** A confirmed finite intervention-stable paradox-enabling retrocausal control channel satisfying Theorem 14.1's counterfactual premises would falsify the PU causal-order branch. Retrocausal correlations or ontologies outside that operational class require a separate criterion.

### Chronology Protection (Section 14.2.3)

**Summary of Theorem 14.1 (Chronology Protection from SPAP)**
No PPI-admissible finite process can supply intervention-stable paradox-enabling retrocausal control information to its own causal past. Equivalently, the SPAP diagonal obstruction defeats any retrocausal channel whose predictive guarantee about a causally affectable future variable survives the receiver's choice of policy.

*Argument.* A retrocausal control bit with uniform accuracy guarantee
$$
\Pr[\hat\phi=\phi_B]\ge1-\epsilon,\qquad 0\le\epsilon<1,
$$
holding under every receiver policy is intervention-stable. The receiver can implement the diagonal policy
$$
\phi_B=\mathrm{NOT}(\hat\phi),
$$
under which $\hat\phi=\phi_B$ requires $\hat\phi=\mathrm{NOT}(\hat\phi)$, forcing $\Pr[\hat\phi=\phi_B]=0$ and contradicting the guarantee for every $\epsilon<1$. The proof is purely logical: it does not invoke channel capacity, contractivity, or thermodynamic suppression. State-mediated correlations that fail to remain predictive of a free future variable under diagonal use are not intervention-stable control channels and lie outside the theorem's target — they are classified under the finite-window statistical gates of Sections 10 and 14.2.6. The SPAP reset-support capacity deficit, refresh-branch strict capacity bounds, and Theorem O.3 fluctuation suppression supply independent resource gates on attempted carrier-mediated implementations, but the SPAP diagonal obstruction is the chronology-protection theorem.
Macroscopic reversal suppression is available only on the O.3 branch: if $N$ selected cycles share one forward/reverse event algebra and each obeys $\sigma_k\ge h_{\min}>0$, then $P_R/P_F\le e^{-Nh_{\min}}$.

| Feature | General Relativity | Predictive Universe |
|:--------|:-------------------|:--------------------|
| **Field equations** | Locally time-reversal compatible in the relevant regimes | The emergent equations do not by themselves select an arrow |
| **Arrow of time** | Requires state or boundary data | Conditional O.3 path-measure theorem |
| **Closed timelike curves** | Some solutions contain CTCs | Paradox-enabling intervention-stable control channels are excluded by Theorem 14.1; other CTC geometries are not classified by that proof |
| **Chronology protection** | Conjectured [Hawking 1992] | Conditional theorem for the intervention-stable diagonal target class |

**Physical Interpretation:** A GR CTC solution conflicts with Theorem 14.1 only if it realizes a finite PPI-admissible channel carrying a receiver-policy-stable contradictory control guarantee. The diagonal proof does not classify every formal CTC geometry as a physical artifact.

### Dynamical Convergence (Appendix D)

Appendix D provides the variational perspective on PCE dynamics. Alignment is a conditional equilibrium result on the faithful-force-identifiability branch, and regularity is a conditional minimizer result on the strict regular-comparator and monotonicity branch.

**Equation (D.0) (Stochastic Gradient Flow)**
The system's slow adaptation dynamics follow:

$$
dx_t = -\eta(x_t)\nabla V(x_t)dt + \sqrt{2D(x_t)}dW_t
$$
where $V(x)$ is the PCE potential serving as a stochastic Lyapunov function.

**Summary of Lemma D.1 (True PCE Optimality Requires Alignment on the Faithful-Cost-Identifiability Branch)**
On the faithful-cost-identifiability branch, a true stable PCE equilibrium satisfies $C_P(v)=\langle\hat C_v\rangle_{x^*}$ for every MPU $v$. Without that branch, stationarity implies only an aggregate or compensated alignment condition.

### Signed Work-Cost Gap Decomposition (Appendix D.3.1)

Definition D.2 registers the measurable gap
$$
\Delta W_v(\tau)=W_{physical,v}(\tau)-\int_t^{t+\tau}R(\langle\hat C_v(t')\rangle)dt'.
$$

**Summary of Lemma D.2 (Signed Work-Cost Gap Decomposition)**
On the efficiency-saturated branch and for $R\in C^2$,
$$
\frac{\Delta W_v(\tau)}{\tau}
=\frac1\tau\int_t^{t+\tau}R'(\langle\hat C_v(t')\rangle)\delta_v(t')dt'
+\frac1{2\tau}\int_t^{t+\tau}R''(\xi_v(t'))\delta_v(t')^2dt'.
$$
If $|R''|\le L_R$, the absolute remainder is at most $\frac{L_R}{2\tau}\int_t^{t+\tau}\delta_v(t')^2dt'$. The identity is signed and noninjective: $R'$ may vanish and the first integral may cancel. A physical feedback realization therefore requires an independent gain/sign/noncancellation certificate.

**Summary of Corollary D.2 (Mean-Square Alignment Upper Bound)**
Under Proposition D.1's quadratic proxy drift, invariant-subspace, compactness, and diffusion hypotheses,
$$
\limsup_{t\to\infty}\mathbb E[\mathcal M(x(t))]
\le\frac{C_{\mathcal M}}{k_1\eta_{min}}.
$$
The right-hand side contains core-gradient forcing and diffusion and is an upper bound, not an attained positive noise floor. Lemma D.2 alone does not supply the drift used here.

**Summary of Theorem D.1 (Quadratic Representation of Linear Alignment Feedback)**
If the operational adaptation force contains
$$
F_v^{\mathrm{align}}=k_1\bigl(C_P(v)-\langle\hat C_v\rangle_x\bigr),
$$
with the targets independent of the proxy coordinates on the adaptation window, then it is the negative proxy-coordinate gradient of
$$
V_{\mathrm{proxy}}(x)=\frac{k_1}{2}\sum_v\bigl(C_P(v)-\langle\hat C_v\rangle_x\bigr)^2.
$$
Among differentiable proxy-coordinate potentials this representation is unique up to an additive function independent of every proxy coordinate. The theorem represents an assumed linear feedback; it does not prove that alignment feedback is necessary or microscopically present.

**Summary of Theorem D.4 (Properties of Local Minimizers; Local Regularity on the Local-Regularization Branch)**
At every local minimizer,
$$
\nabla_C V_{\text{core}}(x^*)+k_1(\langle\hat C\rangle_{x^*}-C_P)=0,
$$
so the proxy residual is controlled by the local core-gradient scale. Under Theorem D.3's strict regular-comparator and monotonicity hypotheses, every global minimizer is geometrically regular. An arbitrary local minimizer is geometrically regular only on the local-regularization branch, where an arbitrarily close geometry-only perturbation lowers $V_{\text{core}}$ at every irregular point.

**Summary of Theorem D.5 (Ergodic Long-Run Behavior; Low-Noise Concentration in Detailed-Balance Regimes)**
Under the compact-boundaryless $C^2$ potential assumptions (A1) and (A5), bounded SPD mobility (A2), uniformly elliptic diffusion (A3), and irreducible noise (A6), the stochastic gradient flow on $V(x)$ admits a unique invariant measure $\pi$ and is ergodic as stated in Equation (D.12) of Appendix D:
$$
\frac{1}{T}\int_0^T f(x(t))\,dt \xrightarrow[T\to\infty]{a.s.} \int f(x)\,\pi(dx).
$$
In detailed-balance Langevin regimes, $\pi_\theta \propto \exp(-V(x)/\theta)$ concentrates on neighborhoods of the global minimizer set as $\theta \downarrow 0$. Under Theorem D.3's strict regular-comparator and monotonicity hypotheses, every global minimum is geometrically regular; without those hypotheses, Theorem D.5 identifies only the minimizer set. Exact proxy-coordinate alignment at true stable PCE equilibria is supplied separately on the faithful-force-identifiability branch of Lemma D.1 / Corollary D.1.

Regularity is therefore a conditional dynamical outcome on the strict-comparison branch, not a consequence of the bare PCE grammar.

### Exponential Convergence Rates (Appendix D.8)

The complexity adaptation dynamics admit quantitative convergence bounds.

**Summary of Theorem D.7 (Uniqueness and Stability of Optimal Complexity)**
Under the Dominance of Stabilizing Costs (DSC) condition ensuring $\Psi'(C) < 0$, the effective potential $V_{eff}(C)$ is strictly convex with unique global minimum $C^\star$ satisfying $\Psi(C^\star) = 0$.

**Summary of Definition D.6 (Polyak-Łojasiewicz Inequality)**
The potential $V(C)$ satisfies PL with constant $\mu_{PL} > 0$ if:
$$
\frac{1}{2}|\nabla V(C)|^2 \ge \mu_{PL}(V(C) - V(C^\star))
$$

**Summary of Lemma D.7 (PL Constant from Stability)**
On a prescribed neighborhood of $C^\star$,
$$
\underline\lambda:=\inf(-\Psi'(C))>0,
\qquad
\mu_{\mathrm{PL}}=\underline\lambda.
$$

**Summary of Theorem D.8 (Exponential Convergence of Complexity Adaptation)**
On the invariant interval $I$ and under
$$
0<\underline\lambda\le V_{\mathrm{eff}}''\le L_{\mathrm{eff}}<\infty,
$$
the deterministic flow satisfies
$$
|C(t)-C^\star|\le e^{-\underline\lambda\eta_{\mathrm{adapt}}t}|C(0)-C^\star|.
$$
For the projected Itô branch, Equation (D.16) gives an exponentially relaxing upper bound on the expected potential gap. Its constant term is an upper bound, not a proved positive noise floor or a stochastic point-convergence theorem.

**Practical Implications:**

| Condition | Certified conclusion |
|:----------|:---------------------|
| Invariant interval and two-sided curvature bound | Deterministic exponential convergence to $C^\star$ |
| Projected Itô branch with $D_{CC}\le D_{CC}^{\max}$ | Expected-gap upper bound of Equation (D.16) |
| Independent stationary law and lower diffusion control | Required for any positive noise-floor or concentration claim |

Neither DSC alone nor the dimensionless rate formula establishes that convergence is rapid relative to an external timescale; that comparison requires calibrated dynamical rates.

### PCE Marginality and 1/f Spectra (Appendix D.8.5)

**Summary of Definition D.8b (Marginal PCE Relaxation Band)**
An adaptive PCE operating point has an active marginal relaxation band $[\lambda_{\min},\lambda_{\max}]$ when its linearized prediction-error dynamics decompose into stable relaxation modes over a wide finite rate interval with logarithmic rate density $q(\lambda)d\log\lambda$ and modal amplitudes $a(\lambda)$.

**Summary of Theorem D.8a (Spectral Exponent from Relaxation Density)**
If the active mode-weight product satisfies
$$
h(\lambda):=a(\lambda)q(\lambda)\sim A_0\lambda^s,
\qquad -1<s<1,
$$
then the prediction-error spectrum obeys
$$
S(f)\propto f^{-\beta_{\mathrm{spec}}},
\qquad
\beta_{\mathrm{spec}}=1-s.
$$

**Summary of Theorem D.8b (PCE Selection of Pink Spectra)**
On a marginal viability branch with no privileged update scale, PCE minimizes the rate-profile description cost by selecting a scale-neutral active profile $h(\lambda)=h_0$. Therefore
$$
S(f)\propto \frac{1}{f}
$$
on the finite operational band
$$
\lambda_{\min}\ll 2\pi f\ll \lambda_{\max}.
$$

**Interpretation:** Pink noise is the macroscopic spectral signature of PCE-regulated adaptive systems operating near a marginal viability surface. Exact all-frequency $1/f$ behavior is not predicted; finite cutoffs and exponent shifts diagnose the system's active relaxation band and its distance from scale-neutral marginality.

### Spacetime Emergence

**Summary of Theorem 43 (Network Geometric Regularity)**
On the Appendix C/D branch where the physical potential contains the declared irregularity costs, every irregular admissible configuration has a regular equal-proxy comparator of strictly lower cost, and the stochastic selection hypotheses hold:

1. The lowest-potential sector exhibits uniform D-dimensional polynomial volume growth (Def 36)
2. The lowest-potential sector exhibits uniformly bounded synthetic Ricci curvature (Def 37)

3. In low-noise detailed-balance regimes, stationary mass concentrates near this regular sector, enabling the propagation cost metric $d_N$ to support emergent continuum geometry

**Three Essential Conditions (Appendix C):**

| Condition | Requirement | Violation Consequence |
|-----------|-------------|----------------------|
| **(LV) Local Viability** | $PP(v) \in (\alpha, \beta)$ for each MPU | Network instability |
| **(GC) Global Coherence** | Predictive information propagates macroscopically | Loss of large-scale prediction |
| **(RE) Resource Efficiency** | Configuration minimizes $V(x)$ | Unsustainable costs |

Under the strict-comparator premises, an irregular configuration cannot be a global minimizer; POP/PCE alone does not establish regularity. Theorems C.1–C.6 supply conditional penalty and doubling/Poincaré results. Lemma C.6d and Theorem C.6e supply fixed-trace $D_4$ shell isotropy and exclude shell-tensor rank collapse only. Fixed-radius geometric noncollapse, curvature transfer, Mosco closure, and manifold rigidity are independent entries of Theorem 43.5 and Appendix D; AQFT and local-horizon bridges remain separate.

### Microscopic Ricci Curvature Derivation (Appendix C.3)

On the registered regular-response branch, the strict edgewise bound $W_1(\mathcal P\delta_v,\mathcal P\delta_u)\le\vartheta_R<1$ gives the conditional Ollivier--Ricci lower bound $\kappa_R\ge1-\vartheta_R>0$; bare PU principles do not supply that premise.

**Representative Local Response Kernel:**
A convenient admissible realization weights local MPU transitions by predictive complexity:
$$
P_{vu} = \frac{P^{(0)}_{vu} \exp(-\lambda_R I'(C_P(u)))}{Z_v}
$$
where $I'(C_P)$ is the derivative of the PCE effective cost-rate function. The exact exponential form is a representative kernel, not a theorem-level necessity; the regularity proof uses only positivity of neighbor weights, bounded baseline comparability, finite response sensitivity to local complexity changes, and strict edgewise smallness of the induced Wasserstein response.

**Ollivier-Ricci Curvature:**
$$
\text{Ric}_{\text{OR}}(v \to u) = 1 - \frac{W_1(\mathcal{P}\delta_v, \mathcal{P}\delta_u)}{d(v,u)}
$$

**Positive Ricci Lower Bound in the Regular-Response Regime (Appendix C.3.3)**
If the induced one-step kernel satisfies a strict edgewise smallness bound
$$
W_1(\mathcal{P}\delta_v,\mathcal{P}\delta_u) \le \vartheta_R < 1
$$
for adjacent vertices, then
$$
\kappa_R := 1 - \vartheta_R > 0.
$$
For the representative kernel above, one sufficient quantitative condition is
$$
\eta_R = C_{geom} \cdot \lambda_R \cdot e^{\lambda_R B} \cdot M \cdot L_{C_P} < 1,
$$
but the regularity proof uses only the abstract strict-response bound. Positive Ricci curvature implies local pathway convergence, simplifying information integration. Its positivity here follows from the assumed strict-response bound; it is not a consequence of bare predictive dynamics.

**Summary of Remark C.3.3a (Ollivier-Ricci to Continuum Scalar Curvature Estimator)**
Under quasi-uniform sampling and an additional weighted-shell/local-isotropy bridge hypothesis—namely asymptotically equal first-shell weights together with

$$
\sum_{y\sim x}\omega_{xy}\hat v_{x\to y}\hat v_{x\to y}^{\mathsf T}=\frac1D I+O(h)
$$
—the Ollivier-Ricci curvature at mesh scale $h$ satisfies $\kappa_h(x,y) = \frac{h^2}{2(D+2)}\mathrm{Ric}_g(v,v) + O(h^3)$. Weighted averaging over neighbors yields a scalar curvature proxy $R_h(x) = R_g(x) + O(h)$ that converges in $L^1_{\mathrm{loc}}$ under refinement. This is a conditional continuum-bridge input used in the locality/consistency hypothesis of Theorem D.6; by itself it does not furnish a Bakry-Émery lower bound or the Section 11.4 synthetic-curvature package used later [Ollivier 2009; van der Hoorn et al. 2020].

**Summary of Theorem C.1 (Path Length Scaling)**

| Registered metric hypothesis | Theorem C.1 conclusion |
|:-----------------------------|:-------------------------|
| Quasi-isometry plus uniformly comparable edge costs | Average and maximum $d_{\mathcal N}=\Theta(L)$ |
| $d_{\mathrm{graph,max}}(L)=\Theta(L^\gamma)$, $\gamma>1$, plus the same edge-cost comparability | $d_{\mathcal N,\max}(L)=\Theta(L^\gamma)$; no average, coherence, or viability conclusion follows |

The conditional GC/RE non-viability conclusions belong to Theorems C.2 and C.5 and require their registered contraction, timing, window, curvature-transfer, or budget premises.

**Viability Functional (Definition C.4):**
$$
\mathcal{V}[\mathcal{C}] = \min\{Q_{\mathrm{LV}}[\mathcal{C}], Q_{\mathrm{GC}}[\mathcal{C}], Q_{\mathrm{RE}}[\mathcal{C}]\}
$$
Configuration $\mathcal{C}$ is viable iff $\mathcal{V}[\mathcal{C}] \ge 1$.

**Summary of Theorem C.5 (Non-Viability of Irregular Geometries)**
Under Theorem C.5's registered contraction, timing, finite-window or extensive-budget, curvature-transfer, and threshold hypotheses, sufficiently large irregularity forces $\mathcal V[\mathcal C]<1$. The conclusion does not follow from path scaling alone.

**Summary of Theorem C.6 (Doubling and Poincaré from PCE)**
Under uniform PCE saturation with $|w_e/C^*_{\text{max}} - 1| \le \varepsilon_C$:

- **Doubling**: $\mu(B(x,2r)) \le D_\star \mu(B(x,r))$ with $D_\star = 2^D(1+4\varepsilon_C)\frac{\Delta_{\text{max}}}{\Delta_{\text{min}}}$
- **(1,2)-Poincaré**: $\fint_{B(x,r)}|f - f_{B}|d\mu \le C_{\mathrm{PI}}(r) \cdot r \left(\fint_{B(x,4r)}|\nabla f|^2 d\mu\right)^{1/2}$

These metric-measure properties are outputs of Theorem C.6 under its stated hypotheses. Lemma C.6d and Theorem C.6e add first-shell isotropy and exclusion of shell-tensor rank collapse. Fixed-radius geometric noncollapse is a separate Theorem-43.5 certificate; Theorem D.6e separately supplies curvature-transfer, finite-core/recovery, and Euclidean-rigidity defects.

**Summary of Theorem 43.5 (Operational Continuum Branch Package)** [§11.3]
On the minimal $M=24$, $D=4$ branch, $D_4$ shell moment closure supplies fixed-trace isotropy. Theorem 43.5 then independently requires fixed-radius geometric noncollapse, curvature transfer, finite-core/recovery, Mosco convergence, and quantitative rigidity. Only on their joint zero-defect/detailed-balance branch do the stated noncollapsed $\mathrm{RCD}^*(K,4)$ limits, Euclidean tangents, and regular manifold subbranch follow.

**Summary of Theorem 44 (Gromov-Hausdorff Limit)** [§11.4]
On the operational-continuum branch of Theorem 43.5, consider a sequence of pointed, rescaled MPU network metric spaces $\{(X_n, o_n) = (\mathcal{V}_n, \delta_{eff, n}^{-1} d_{\mathcal{N}_n}, o_n)\}$ where mesh size $\delta_{eff, n} \to 0$. The bounded-action family is precompact in pointed measured Gromov–Hausdorff topology. Hence a subsequence converges to a limit pointed metric-measure space $(M,d_\infty,\mu_\infty,o_\infty)$. The limit is a doubling PI space with quadratic Cheeger energy, hence infinitesimally Hilbertian. On the selected non-collapsed branch there exists a full $\mu_\infty$-measure regular set $M_{reg}\subseteq M$ on which tangent cones are Euclidean $\mathbb{R}^D$; on the PU branch selected by Theorem Z.11 this means $\mathbb{R}^4$. The Euclidean tangent is unique $\mu_\infty$-a.e.

*Proof.* Theorem C.6e supplies uniform first-shell isotropy and exclusion of shell-tensor rank collapse. Theorem 43.5 separately registers fixed-radius geometric noncollapse, while Lemma D.6a's two-sided normalized-volume and bounded-geometry hypotheses give pointed measured Gromov--Hausdorff precompactness. Hence a subsequence exists only on their joint branch.

Theorem D.6e selects the asymptotically defect-free sequence and gives Mosco convergence to a quadratic Cheeger energy. Quadraticity makes the limit infinitesimally Hilbertian. When the registered curvature-transfer defect tends to zero, Theorem C.6c identifies the noncollapsed limit as an $\mathrm{RCD}^*(K,4)$ space; its doubling and Poincaré properties pass to the limit on this branch. Finally, when the quantitative-rigidity defect also tends to zero, Theorem 44a gives a full-measure regular set with unique Euclidean $\mathbb R^4$ tangent and the stated $C^{1,\alpha}$ regular subbranch. These conclusions are exactly the assertions of Theorem 44. ∎

**Summary of Theorem 45 (Metric Tensor $g_{\mu\nu}$)** [§11.5]
Conditional on Theorem 44, the regular set $M_{reg}\subseteq M$ admits an a.e. defined, symmetric, non-degenerate rank-2 tensor field $g_{\mu\nu}(x)$ compatible with the limit distance:
$$
ds^2 = g_{\mu\nu}(x) dx^\mu dx^\nu
$$
The quadratic limit energy makes the first-order differential structure infinitesimally Hilbertian, and the Euclidean-rigidity/regularity hypothesis on the selected branch yields the stated $C^{1,\alpha}$ regularity in appropriate coordinates. This gives the positive-definite Riemannian tensor on the regular operational-distance branch. A pseudo-Riemannian or Lorentzian metric requires the separate Appendix O time, principal-symbol, and cone certificates.

**Summary of Corollary 45b (Fisher-Propagation Compatibility)**
The predictive Fisher tensor $h=\frac14F^Q$ pulls back to the regular continuum chart through the retained protocol-response map. On the certificate branch where the same finite-response family supplies both Fisher distinguishability and the Cheeger propagation quadratic form, the positive operational-distance tensor satisfies $h=g^{sp}$. The Lorentzian metric enters only after the causal-clock and cone-orientation construction of Section 11.6.

**Section 11.5.2 (Continuum Relabeling Symmetry and Diffeomorphism Invariance)**
The emergent manifold branch admits coordinate charts without making a chart label observable. Discrete relabeling neutrality alone does not imply full smooth-diffeomorphism invariance. Theorem 45a derives continuum diffeomorphism covariance only on the conjunction of a local finite-order action, geometric transformation laws for all retained fields, and Hypothesis 11.5.3.3's identification of bookkeeping relabelings with orientation-preserving diffeomorphisms.

**Map summary — Theorem 46 (Finite Operational Causal-Speed Bound)** [§11.6]
On a branch with a separately registered positive edge-update duration $\tau_{\min}$, nonzero scale $\delta$, successive edge-by-edge serialization in the propagation-cost metric, and bounded edge weights $w_{xy}\le w_{\max}$, every $n$-edge path satisfies
$$
t\ge n\tau_{\min},
\qquad
d_{\mathcal N}\le n\delta w_{\max},
$$
and therefore
$$
\frac{d_{\mathcal N}}{t}\le\frac{\delta w_{\max}}{\tau_{\min}}.
$$
Theorem 29 identifies an internal characteristic timescale but does not establish the per-edge duration premise. The bound need not be attained. An attained frontier is separate branch data; $c=\delta/\tau_{\min}$ additionally requires normalized uniform weights and one-link attainment. Lorentzian signature requires the complete Corollary 46a/Appendix O package.

**Summary of Corollary 46a (Lorentzian Signature and Local Lorentz Kinematics from Theorem 46 and Appendix O)** [§11.6]
Under the four hypotheses O.7.2.1–O.7.2.4, including a rank-four positive continuum tensor, a nonvanishing time covector with rank-three kernel, the uniform operational causal-speed upper bound of Theorem 46, and the separately assumed attained frontier, cone coincidence, nondegeneracy, and second-order closure, Theorems O.7a and O.7b force a Lorentzian $(-,+,+,+)$ principal symbol and derive the tangent-frame group $SO^+(1,3)$. Corollary O.7b.2 records the matching no-Riemannian-replacement gate: a positive-definite principal symbol has no nonzero characteristic cone. Full matter-dynamical Lorentz covariance separately requires common-cone and covariant interaction/representation certificates. Global strict-spin bundle closure remains conditional on $w_2=0$ or a strict-spin $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{tan}}$; $\mathrm{Spin}^c$ or twisted branches require replacement bundle data.

**Summary of Theorem 47 (Predictive Holonomy and Riemann Curvature)** [§11.7]
On the regular product-bundle branch of Theorems 48 and G.4b, the supplied predictive connection $D_\mu^{\mathrm{pred}}=\partial_\mu+\Omega_\mu\otimes1+1\otimes A_\mu^{\mathrm{int}}$ has curvature
$$
\mathcal F_{\mu\nu}^{\mathrm{pred}}=R_{\mu\nu}(\Omega)\otimes1+1\otimes F_{\mu\nu}(A^{\mathrm{int}}),
$$
and its infinitesimal loop holonomy is governed by this curvature. The spin projection is the Riemann curvature and the internal projection is gauge curvature. The theorem does not construct the connection from ND-RID activity, prove either projection nonzero, or identify $T_{\mu\nu}^{(\mathrm{MPU})}$ as its source; those require the separate continuum, connection, and Section 12 source-response bridges.

**Summary of Theorem 48 (Fibre-Bundle Representation)** [§11.8]
Assuming the emergent Lorentzian manifold is oriented, time-oriented, and spin, and the internal rank-$d_0$ Hilbert bundle is Hermitian, the combined spin and unitary frame data define a principal fibre bundle $P(M,G)$ over the emergent spacetime manifold $M$, with structure group:
$$
G = \text{Spin}(1,3) \times U(d_0)
$$

| Component | Structure | Physical Meaning |
|:----------|:----------|:-----------------|
| **Fibre** | $(\mathcal{F}_x, \mathcal{P}_x)$ | Spin frame + internal unitary frame |
| **Connection** | $A_\mu = \omega_\mu \oplus A_\mu^{\text{int}}$ | Spin connection + internal gauge connection |
| **Curvature** | $F_{\mu\nu} = R_{\mu\nu} + F_{\mu\nu}^{\text{int}}$ | Riemann curvature + gauge field strength |

The covariant derivative ensuring consistent parallel transport is:
$$
D_{\mu}\Phi = \partial_{\mu}\Phi + \rho^{\text{Lor}}_{*}(\omega_{\mu})\Phi + \rho^{\text{int}}_{*}(A^{\text{int}}_{\mu})\Phi
$$

### Causality Emergence

**Summary of Proposition F.1 (Discrete Lieb--Robinson Bound)**
Suppose the strongly continuous Heisenberg generator decomposes as $\mathcal L_n^*=\sum_Z\mathcal L_{n,Z}^*$ with uniform finite range, bounded local operator norm, bounded graph degree, and bounded incidence of generator terms. Then its finite interaction norm gives constants $C_\mu,v_\mu<\infty$ such that
$$
\|[\alpha_t^{(n)}(A),B]\|
\le C_\mu|X|\|A\|\|B\|
e^{-\mu(d_{\mathcal N_n}(X,Y)-v_\mu|t|)}.
$$
The constants are determined by the registered interaction norm and graph convolution bound. Definition 27 supplies none of these locality premises, and no formula depending only on $(J,r_0,z_{\max},D,\tau)$ follows without incidence and walk-counting conventions.

**Summary of Corollary F.1a (Lightcone Normalization on the Strict Single-Clock Branch):** On the strict single-clock finite-range branch, non-overlapping local gates of range $r_0$ per layer supply the lightcone normalization with speed $v_*=(D r_0)/\tau$; outside this branch, lightcone identification remains an explicit AQFT bridge hypothesis or the output of an accepted $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{cone}}$ for the retained sectors entering the net.

**Summary of Corollary F.1 (Conditional Einstein Causality Limit)**
On Theorem F.0's geometric-convergence, cone-normalization, embedding, dynamics-convergence, support-growth, and uniform Lieb--Robinson branches, let compactly supported continuum observables have a positive spacelike margin from the limiting cone. The discrete bound then drives their embedded commutator norm to zero, giving
$$
[\mathfrak A(\mathcal O_1),\mathfrak A(\mathcal O_2)]=\{0\}
$$
for the retained spacelike-separated regions. This is a conditional continuum-transfer theorem, not a consequence of ND-RID dynamics alone.

**Summary of Theorem 12.1 (Local Clausius Relation ⇒ EFE)** [§12.3]
Let $(M,g)$ be the emergent Lorentzian manifold with local Rindler horizons. Assume the saturated area-law density, conserved energy flux, linearized Raychaudhuri equation with vanishing initial expansion and shear, and the all-null Clausius relation on shrinking transverse patches $P_\varepsilon$ and affine lengths $h_\varepsilon$, with every entropy and Clausius remainder $o(A_\varepsilon h_\varepsilon^2)$ uniformly on the retained null family. Then, in natural units,
$$
R_{\mu\nu} - \tfrac{1}{2}Rg_{\mu\nu} + \Lambda g_{\mu\nu} = 8\pi G_{\mathrm{op}}\, T_{\mu\nu}^{(MPU)},
$$
with $\Lambda$ an integration constant. The SI-restored form is Theorem 50.

*Dependency outline:* Clausius consistency gives
$$
\left(R_{\alpha\beta} - \frac{2\pi}{\eta} T_{\alpha\beta}^{(MPU)}\right)k^\alpha k^\beta = 0
$$
for every null vector. A symmetric tensor that annihilates all null quadratic forms is proportional to $g_{\alpha\beta}$; contracted Bianchi together with $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}=0$ then yields the Einstein tensor plus cosmological constant.

**Summary of Theorem 12.3 (Universality at Fixed Scale)** [§12.5]
For the predictive action $S_{PU}(g, \Phi)$ with diffeomorphism invariance and no background structures, the Noether identity implies $\nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu} = 0$ with the stress-energy convention
$$
T^{\mu\nu}:=\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S_{MPU}}{\delta g_{\mu\nu}},
\qquad
T_{\mu\nu}:=-\frac{2}{\sqrt{-g}}\frac{\delta S_{MPU}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}
$$
For a sharply localized pressureless test body with negligible self-gravity and metric-only coupling through the boundary-sufficient metric, conservation implies the metric geodesic equation. Theorem 12.3 does not assert this for all matter or for branches carrying species-dependent or nonmetric retained forces.

**Summary of Corollary 12.3.1 (Universality of $G$ at a Coarse-Graining Scale on the Sourced Weak-Field Branch)**
If the same area-law coefficient $1/(4G(\mu))$ is used in the Clausius-derived Einstein equation and that sourced equation is taken to its weak-field Newtonian limit, the Poisson coefficient is the same $G(\mu)$. Geodesic motion proves metric universality on its test-body branch but fixes no value of $G$.

**Summary of Theorem E.1 (Conditional Physical Reset Ledger)**
Let $P$ be the pre-reset record and let $R$ contain every record retained unchanged through the reset. Under the degenerate-register, isothermal-bath, cyclic-control, and Hamiltonian-return hypotheses of Theorem E.1,
$$
\frac{\langle Q_{\mathrm{bath}}\rangle}{k_BT}
=H_q(P\mid R)+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}},
\qquad
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}\ge0.
$$
The memory entropy decreases by $k_BH_q(P\mid R)$, while the total entropy production of the reset implementation is $k_B\varepsilon_{\mathrm{diss}}$. Measurement, feedback, or information-acquisition terms require separate implementation and no-double-counting ledgers.

**Summary of Lemma E.1 (Strict Contractivity of Average 'Evolve' Channel on the Refresh Branch)**
If the average ND-RID channel contains a nonzero input-independent full-state refresh component, $\mathcal{E}_N=(1-p)\Psi+pT_\sigma$ with $p>0$, then it is strictly contractive in trace distance with factor $f_{\text{RID}}=1-p<1$. If $\sigma\succ0$, the channel is primitive (unique full-rank fixed point).

**Summary of Theorem E.2 (Refresh-Branch Channel Capacity Bound)**
The strictly contractive refresh-branch channel has bounded classical capacity:
$$
C(\mathcal{E}_N) < \ln d_0.
$$

**Summary of Proposition E.2a (Reset-Support Capacity Deficit)**
A registered completed reset of an $r$-dimensional factor inside $\mathcal H_{d_0}$ confines the output to support dimension at most $d_0/r$, hence
$$
C(\mathcal E_N)\le\ln d_0-\ln r.
$$
For a binary registered reset, $r=2$, so $C(\mathcal E_N)\le\ln d_0-\ln2$. SPAP alone does not register this channel.

**Summary of Theorem E.3 (Boundary Channel Density)**
Geometric regularity gives the deterministic upper bound on boundary-crossing links. On the density-certificate branch, the number of effective independent channels crossing boundary area $\mathcal A$ scales as:
$$
N_{\mathrm{eff\,links}} = \sigma_{\mathrm{eff\,link}} \cdot \mathcal{A}+o(\mathcal A)
$$
where $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff\,link}}=\chi/(\eta\delta^2)$.

**Summary of Theorem E.6 (Conditional Boundary Area Bound and Saturated Area Law)**
On the density-certificate branch, the reliable boundary response entropy satisfies
$$
S_{\mathrm{rel}}(\mathcal A)
\le
k_B\left(\frac{\chi C(\mathcal E_N)}{\eta\delta^2}\right)\mathcal A+o(\mathcal A).
$$
Equality requires the capacity-achieving, entropy-saturating, additive-ledger branch of Theorem E.6. Writing the positive saturated coefficient as $1/(4L_{P,\mathrm{op}}^2)=c^3/(4G_{\mathrm{op}}\hbar)$ is an operational definition; identifying $G_{\mathrm{op}}$ with measured Newton $G$ is a separate calibration. Under geometric regularity without the density certificate, Lemma E.5.1 supplies only the coarser upper bound.

**Comparison of Two Boundary-Scaling Routes (Appendix E.6)**
The appendix compares two routes with different hypotheses and conclusions:

1. **Many-body correlation branch:** Theorem E.4a gives a mutual-information boundary bound for finite-range Gibbs states. A von Neumann entanglement-entropy area law is theorem-level only in the stated one-dimensional gapped setting; in higher dimensions the manuscript retains Theorem E.4' as an explicit ansatz. None of these statements fixes the Bekenstein-Hawking coefficient without the independent horizon saturation and normalization branch.
2. **Operational channel counting**: the completed registered-reset support deficit bounds capacity structurally, and refresh/minorization branches add the strict contractive bound $f_{\text{RID}} < 1$; geometric scaling of boundary channels at density $\sigma_{\mathrm{eff\,link}} = \chi/(\eta\delta^2)$ yields the same operational coefficient on the density-certificate branch.

These routes have different hypotheses and conclusions: the many-body route bounds specified correlation measures, while the operational channel route gives a boundary-capacity bound and reaches equality only under Theorem E.6's saturation hypotheses. They do not constitute two hypothesis-free derivations of the Bekenstein-Hawking equality.

**Equation E.9 (Operational Gravitational Coupling)**
$$
G_{\mathrm{op}}
=\frac{\eta\delta^2c^3}{4\hbar\chi C(\mathcal E_N)}.
$$
This is the positive saturated-coefficient definition on Theorem E.6's stated branch; $G_{\mathrm{op}}=G$ is an external calibration.

**Equation E.16 (Conditional Planck--MPU Calibration)**
Assume residual-capacity saturation,
$$
C_{\max}^*=\ln d_0-\varepsilon_0,
$$
the minimal values $d_0=8$ and $\varepsilon_0=\ln2$, the ideal density choices $\chi=\eta=1$, and the operational normalization $\delta^2/L_P^2=4\chi C_{\max}^*/\eta$. Then
$$
C_{\max}^*=2\ln2,
\qquad
\frac{\delta}{L_P}=\sqrt{8\ln2}=2.354820045\ldots.
$$
This ratio is a value on the stated calibration branch, not a branch-independent consequence of PCE.

For a regular bounded region, the local boundary budget

C_{\mathrm{total}}=N_{\mathrm{channels}}C_{\max}

### Predictive Throughput and Cosmic Censorship (Section 14.2.4)

**Map summary — Definition 14.2.4.1 (Predictive Throughput)**
For $n$ registered boundary rounds with classical message $M_n$, exterior record $\mathcal R_n$, and actual elapsed time $T_n\ge n\tau_{\min}$,
$$
L(S):=\sup_{\{\mathcal P_n\}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{I(M_n;\mathcal R_n)}{T_n}.
$$

**Map summary — Theorem 14.2.4.1 (Conditional ND-RID Throughput Bound)**
The displayed rate bound additionally assumes the registered channel-use clock; Theorem 29 alone does not supply it.
$$
L(S) \leq \frac{N_{\mathrm{eff\,links}}(S) \cdot C_{\max}}{\tau_{min}} = \frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{eff\,link}} \, A(S) \cdot C_{\max}}{\tau_{min}} + o(A)
$$

Definition 14.2.4.1a and Corollary 14.2.4.1b specialize the one-round budget to a physically learned guard batch only when, for the pre-round exterior record $R_{0,m}$ and newly received output $Y_m$,
$$
mI_{\mathrm{guard}}
\le
I(G_m;Y_m\mid R_{0,m})
\le
N_{\mathrm{eff\,links}}(S)C_{\max}.
$$
Consequently,
$$
m\le
\left\lfloor
\frac{N_{\mathrm{eff\,links}}(S)C_{\max}}
{I_{\mathrm{guard}}}
\right\rfloor.
$$
Distinct labels, preshared correlations, and marginal bounds do not supply the conditional joint lower bound, and Proposition N.4 is not used as a round-transfer theorem.

**Summary of Lemma 14.2.4.2a (Transport-Contraction Sensitivity Bound)**
For CPTP transport $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$ composed of $n(\gamma)$ cycles each contracting trace distance by at most a registered factor $f_{RID}<1$, output statistics of any POVM obey
$$
D_{\mathrm{TV}}(p,q)\le D_{tr}(\mathcal{E}_\gamma(\rho_1), \mathcal{E}_\gamma(\rho_2)) \leq f_{RID}^{n(\gamma)} D_{tr}(\rho_1, \rho_2).
$$
This is a transport-distinguishability statement; it supplies no curvature, horizon, or censorship conclusion.

**Summary of Lemma 14.2.4.2b (Normal-Coordinate Certificate Test)**
Under Theorem 14.2.4.2's hypotheses, $B_\delta\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$ is sufficient for the stated normal-coordinate accuracy; its failure forfeits only this sufficient certificate, not the manifold description itself.

**Summary of Theorem 14.2.4.2 (Fixed-Resolution Manifold-Certificate Boundary)**
At fixed resolution $\delta$ and tolerance $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$, the framed bound $B_\delta:=C_1\lVert R\rVert_{\mathrm{fr},\delta}\delta^2+C_2\delta^3\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$ certifies normal-coordinate accuracy $\sup_{U_\delta}|g_{AB}-\eta_{AB}|\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$; if $B_\delta>\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$, only that sufficient certificate is lost. No horizon-formation, bounded-curvature, extendibility, completeness, or censorship conclusion follows.

**Summary of Theorem E.9.1 (Boundary Area Budget on the Density-Certificate Branch)**

For a causal prediction boundary $\Sigma$ satisfying the density and additive-budget hypotheses,
$$
S_\Sigma^{\mathrm{op}}
\le
\frac{\chi C_{\max}}{\eta\delta^2}\mathcal A+o(\mathcal A).
$$
Equality with $\mathcal A/(4L_P^2)+o(\mathcal A/L_P^2)$ requires the theorem's saturation, packing, and spacing entries. Thermodynamic horizon entropy and measured $G$ require the additional information--entropy and calibration bridges.

**Map summary — Theorem E.10.2 (Serialized Propagation-Speed Bound and Conditional Attainment)**
On the registered nearest-neighbor serialization branch with edge length at most $\delta$ and edge time at least $\tau_{\min}$,
$$
v_{\mathrm{ser}}\le\frac{\delta}{\tau_{\min}}.
$$
Equality and identification with $c$ require the separate one-link-attainment and scale-identification hypotheses.

**Map summary — Corollary E.10.2 (Conditional Locality Bound in the Serialized ND-RID Regime)**
The speed bound is kinematic and conditional on registered serialization, metric, spacing, and edge-clock data. A reset-cost ledger may constrain implementation cost but does not establish locality or a Lieb--Robinson estimate.

**Summary of Theorem 49 (Horizon-Entropy Area Law)**
On the geometric-regularity, density-certificate, residual-budget, capacity-achieving, entropy-saturating, additive-ledger branch,
$$
S_{\text{boundary}}(\mathcal A)
=
\frac{\mathcal A}{4G_{\mathrm{op}}}+o(\mathcal A)
$$
in natural units, while in SI units
$$
S_{\text{boundary}}(\mathcal A)
=

Without the saturation entries the theorem supplies only the corresponding upper bound, and identifying $G_{\mathrm{op}}$ with measured $G$ is separate.

**Summary of Theorem 50 (Conditional Einstein Field Equations)**
The Einstein-response conclusion requires the complete Theorem-12.1 package: controlled shrinking-patch remainders, saturated constant horizon-entropy density, the linearized Raychaudhuri relation, the Clausius identity for every retained null direction, and a conserved registered stress source, together with Appendix E's operational coupling and units calibration. On that branch,
$$
R_{\mu\nu}-\tfrac12Rg_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}
=\frac{8\pi G_{\mathrm{op}}}{c^4}T_{\mu\nu}^{(\mathrm{MPU})}.

### Stress-Energy Tensor Construction (Appendix B)

The macroscopic stress-energy tensor $T_{\mu\nu}^{(MPU)}$ sourcing emergent gravity is constructed through a systematic pathway from microscopic MPU operators:

**Construction Pathway:**

| Step | Structure | Definition |
|:----:|:----------|:-----------|
| 1 | Operational complexity $\hat{C}_v$ | Hermitian operator with spectrum $\lambda(d) = K_0 + \Delta C(d)$ (Def B.1) |
| 2 | Cost operators $\hat{R}, \hat{R}_I$ | Functional calculus: $\hat{R}(C_v) = R(\hat{C}_v)$ (Thm B.1) |
| 3 | Energy density $\hat{\rho}_v$ | $\hat{\rho}_v = \frac{1}{V_0}\left(\hat{H}_v + (\hat{R} + \hat{R}_I)\tau_0 + \hat{E}_{int}\right)$ (Def B.3) |
| 4 | Flow operators $\hat{\pi}_{v,j}, \hat{p}_{v,jk}$ | Defined via local conservation laws (Def B.5) |
| 5 | Canonical tensor $\hat{T}^{\mu\nu}_{(can)}$ | Assembled from energy density and flows (Def B.6) |
| 6 | Symmetric tensor $\hat{\Theta}^{(MPU)}_{\mu\nu}$ | Belinfante-Rosenfeld symmetrization (Thm B.4) |
| 7 | Macroscopic tensor $T^{(MPU)}_{\mu\nu}(x)$ | Coarse-grained source $\omega(\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu})$ and, on the regular variational branch, $\frac{2}{\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}}\frac{\delta S_{(\mathrm{MPU})}}{\delta g_{\mu\nu}}$ for covariant metric variations, equivalently $-\frac{2}{\sqrt{\lvert g\rvert}}\frac{\delta S_{(\mathrm{MPU})}}{\delta g^{\mu\nu}}$ for inverse-metric variations (Def B.8; Thm B.8c) |

**Summary of Definition B.3 (Microscopic Energy Density)**
The energy density operator includes contributions from internal Hamiltonian, excess operational energy above $R(C_{op})$, reflexive/irreversible overhead, and interactions:
$$
\hat{\rho}_v = \frac{1}{V_{\mathrm{MPU}}}\left( \hat{H}_v + (\hat{R}(C_v)-R(C_{op})\hat{\mathbb I}_v)\tau_0 + \hat{R}_I(C_v)\tau_0 + \frac{1}{2}\sum_{v' \sim v}\hat{V}_{vv'}\right)
$$

The interaction operator $\hat{V}_{vv'}$ includes both conservative potential energy and dissipative contributions. The Lindblad form describes reduced two-MPU dynamics; $\hat{V}_{dissip-contrib}^{(vv')}$ is the local self-adjoint edge term (possibly on an enlarged Hilbert space) that accounts for the system--auxiliary energy channel realizing $\mathcal L_D$, with activity-conditioned entropy ledger $\dot S_{tot}\ge k_B\epsilon r_{int}$ and $0\le r_{int}\le1/\tau_{int}$; saturation is a separate branch premise.

**Summary of Theorem B.6 (Correspondence with Standard Forms)**
The macroscopic tensor reproduces standard physical forms:

| State | Form | Physical Meaning |
|:------|:-----|:-----------------|
| Vacuum $\omega_{vac}$ | $T_{\mu\nu}^{(MPU)}\rvert_{\mathrm{vac}} = 0$ | Vacuum normalization; $\Lambda$ enters Eq (76) geometrically |
| Thermal $\omega_{th}$ | $T_{\mu\nu}=(e+p)u_\mu u_\nu+p g_{\mu\nu}$ for energy density $e$ and $u^\mu u_\mu=-1$; equivalently $e=\rho_m c^2$ for mass density $\rho_m$ | Perfect fluid on the local-equilibrium branch |

These are conditional standard-form checks: the vacuum row assumes a Poincaré-invariant vacuum and the stated vacuum subtraction, while the thermal row assumes local isotropic equilibrium. The metric-variational identification and continuum conservation are the separate conclusions of Theorems B.8b--B.8d, not consequences of Theorem B.6 alone.

### Curvature–Decoherence Bridge (Section 11.7.2)

**CPTP Transport Structure:**
$$
\mathcal{E}_\gamma = \overleftarrow{\mathcal{P}} \exp\left( \int_0^1 ds \, \dot{\gamma}^\mu(s) \, \mathcal{L}_\mu \right)
$$
where each $\mathcal{L}_\mu$ is a GKSL generator decomposing into unitary (holonomy) and dissipative (ND-RID) components.

**Reference-Anchored Curvature Phase and Conditional Chronometric Rate:**
Take one branch on the reference worldline, $x_0=0$, and the other at $x_1=L_q$. In Fermi coordinates the reference-anchored quadratic potential difference is
$$
|\Delta\Phi|
=\frac12\left|R_{0m0n}L_q^mL_q^n\right|+O(|L_q|^3).
$$
The coherent relative phase therefore satisfies
$$
|\dot\Theta_{ij}|
=\frac{|\Delta E_{ij}|}{2\hbar}
\left|R_{0m0n}L_q^mL_q^n\right|+O(|L_q|^3).
$$
For a pair centered symmetrically about the reference worldline, the even quadratic term cancels between the two endpoints; the displayed formula must not be used for that geometry.

A decoherence or chronometric jump rate equals this phase rate only on the saturated chronometric ND--RID/GKSL branch with $\Gamma_{\mathrm{ch}}=|\dot\Theta|$, or after an independent source-averaging/open-system mechanism derives the same rate. Thus
$$
\Gamma_{\mathrm{ch}}^{(ij)}
=\frac{|\Delta E_{ij}|}{2\hbar}
\left|R_{0m0n}L_q^mL_q^n\right|+O(|L_q|^3)
$$
is a conditional rate, whereas the preceding phase relation is the coherent weak-curvature statement.

**Conditional discriminator against self-gravity collapse models:** On the registered reference-anchored geometry and saturated chronometric/open-system branch, equal mass distributions with distinct internal energy splittings give a rate proportional to $|\Delta E_{ij}|$. Comparison with a Penrose--Diósi null requires that model's own mass-density prediction and the common experimental nuisance ledger; it is not a branch-independent PU prediction.

**Summary of Lemma 12.1 (Uniqueness of Einstein Tensor)**

Within the four-dimensional, local, metric-only, diffeomorphism-invariant action class whose Euler--Lagrange equations contain at most second metric derivatives, Lovelock's theorem fixes the field tensor to a linear combination of $G_{\mu\nu}$ and $g_{\mu\nu}$. This is an action-class uniqueness result; horizon entropy alone does not prove its hypotheses or fix the two coefficients.

### Scale-Dependent Statistical and Thermodynamic Structures

**Summary of Theorem 12.2 (Scale-Dependent Statistical and Thermodynamic Structures)**
On independently certified branches, PU supplies the following scale-dependent structures; the microscopic modular row is not by itself physical equilibration:

Thermodynamic equilibration under ND-RID yields characteristic structures at different scales:

| Scale | Equilibration Process | Physical Result |
|:------|:----------------------|:----------------|
| Microscopic (MPU) | Modular KMS representation; physical equilibrium only with an independent selector | Born weights have trace form on Theorem G.1.7's certified domain; time-stationarity requires an independent invariant-state/dynamics certificate |

**Summary of Theorem E.9.3 (Horizon Temperature Structure)**
Horizon temperatures share universal form:
$$

### Emergent QFT Structure (Appendix F)

The Algebraic Quantum Field Theory (AQFT) framework provides the rigorous mathematical setting for the manuscript's operational-continuum discussion, conditional on the explicit bridge hypotheses collected in Theorem F.0 and the geometric status boundary stated in Theorem 43.5, Section 11.4, and Appendix C.7.

**Summary of Definition F.1 (Net of Local Observables)**
To each spacetime region $\mathcal{O} \in \mathfrak{O}(M)$, associate a local C*-algebra $\mathfrak{A}_n(\mathcal{O})$ acting on the tensor product Hilbert space of MPUs within that region:
$$
\mathfrak{A}_n(\mathcal{O}) \subset \mathcal{B}\left(\bigotimes_{v \in R_n(\mathcal{O})} \mathcal{H}_v\right)
$$

The net satisfies isotony: $\mathcal{O}_1 \subset \mathcal{O}_2 \Rightarrow \mathfrak{A}_n(\mathcal{O}_1) \subset \mathfrak{A}_n(\mathcal{O}_2)$.

**Map entry: Proposition F.1 (Discrete Lieb-Robinson Bound)**
For operators $A \in \mathfrak{A}_n(X)$, $B \in \mathfrak{A}_n(Y)$ at graph distance $d_{\mathcal{N}_n}(X,Y)$:
$$
\|[\alpha_t^{(n)}(A), B]\| \le C\|A\|\|B\||X| e^{-\mu(d - v_{LR}^{(n)} t)}
$$
with finite Lieb-Robinson velocity $v_{LR}^{(n)}$ determined by the local boundedness and finite-range hypotheses of Appendix F.

**Map entry: Corollary F.1 (Einstein Causality Emergence)**

$$
[\mathfrak{A}(\mathcal{O}_1), \mathfrak{A}(\mathcal{O}_2)] = 0 \quad \text{for } \mathcal{O}_1 \perp \mathcal{O}_2
$$

**Summary of Theorem F.2 (Spin-Statistics Connection)**
Under the Appendix F continuum AQFT bridge together with the additional Wightman realization hypotheses stated in Theorem F.2, half-integer spin fields anticommute and integer spin fields commute at spacelike separation. On branches satisfying Theorem F.5a, the Wightman realization package may be replaced by the modular descent package: Haag duality, split property, DHR transportability, PCE/KMS modular boost action, modular reflection, and vanishing modular cocycle obstruction. For a one-particle internal space of dimension $d_{\mathrm{int}}$,
$$
\dim\operatorname{Sym}^N\mathcal H
=\binom{d_{\mathrm{int}}+N-1}{N},
\qquad
\dim\bigwedge^N\mathcal H
=\binom{d_{\mathrm{int}}}{N}
$$
on their respective domains. For $d_{\mathrm{int}},N\ge2$, and for the fermionic sector with $N\le d_{\mathrm{int}}$, these dimensions are smaller than $d_{\mathrm{int}}^N$. If the multiplicity participates in any retained localization, fusion, gauge, transport, or protocol response, the hypotheses fail and no exclusion of response-active parastatistics follows.
