$$
on shell as a consequence of diffeomorphism invariance.

On branches satisfying Theorem F.5a, the antiunitary CPT action is supplied by modular wedge reflection and sector conjugation after the modular cocycle obstruction vanishes.

**Summary of Theorem U.8 (Translation Zero Modes)**
By translation invariance, the continuum second variation $D^2 S_{\text{cont}}(\phi^*)$ annihilates:

\left\{-\partial_\mu \phi^*\right\}_{\mu=1}^4

**Summary of Theorem U.8a (Virial Stationarity under Dilations)**
At the PCE-Attractor, the virial identity holds:
$$
\frac{d}{d\rho} S_{\text{cont}}[\phi^*_\rho]\bigg|_{\rho=1} = 0
$$
where $\phi^*_\rho(x) = \phi^*(\rho x)$. This is a first-variation identity, not by itself a proof of a Hessian zero mode.

**Summary of Theorem U.8c (Pure-Dilatation Kernel Obstruction)**
For the Definition U.4 continuum action, the pure-coordinate dilatation tangent $s=x^\mu\partial_\mu\phi^*$ has strictly negative Hessian quadratic form and is not in the Hessian kernel:

\qquad
s\notin\ker D^2S_{\text{cont}}(\phi^*).
$$
This statement does not assert that $s$ is a Hessian eigenvector.

This restricted computation is not a carrier-marking or action theorem.

**Summary of Theorem U.13b (Four-Mode Nullity of the Sampled-Angular Hessian under the Stated False-Vacuum Spectral Hypothesis)**

## Part XVI-C: Effective Action Formalism (Appendix X)

### From Predictive Statistics to Generating Functionals

**Equation X.1 (Cumulant Generating Functional)**
For sufficient statistics $\mathcal{O}_a[\varphi]$ with sources $J^a(x)$:
$$
W[J] := \ln Z[J] = \ln\!\int\!\mathcal{D}\varphi\;p_\theta[\varphi]\;
\exp\!\Big(\!\int\! d^dx\, J^a(x)\,\mathcal O_a{[\varphi]}(x)\Big)
$$

**Equation X.2 (1PI Effective Action)**
The Legendre transform of $W[J]$:
$$
\Gamma[\Phi] := \sup_{J}\Big\{\!\int\! d^dx\,J^a\Phi_a - W[J]\Big\}, \qquad \frac{\delta \Gamma}{\delta \Phi_a(x)}=J^a(x)
$$
where $\Phi_a(x) := \delta W/\delta J^a(x) = \langle \mathcal{O}_a(x)\rangle_J$.

**Summary of Proposition X.1 (Information Geometry and Curvature)**
The connected two-point kernel and 1PI Hessian are functional inverses:
$$
\mathcal{G}_{ab}(x,y) = \frac{\delta^2 W}{\delta J^a(x)\delta J^b(y)}, \qquad \Gamma^{(2)}_{ab} = \mathcal{G}^{-1}_{ab}
$$
Under LAN conditions, $\mathcal{G}_{ab}$ coincides with the Fisher information metric.

### Functional RG and Wilsonian Coarse-Graining

**Equation X.4 (Wetterich Flow Equation)**
With IR regulator $R_k$ suppressing modes with $q \lesssim k$:
$$
\partial_k \Gamma_k[\Phi] = \frac{1}{2}\,\mathrm{STr}\!\Big[\big(\Gamma^{(2)}_k[\Phi]+R_k\big)^{-1}\,\partial_k R_k\Big]
$$
where $\mathrm{STr}$ is the supertrace.

**Summary of Corollary X.2 (Fixed Points and RG Relevance)**
Directions with negative eigenvalues of the $\Gamma_k$ stability matrix are RG-relevant; positive eigenvalues are irrelevant.

$$

The first-order Thomson-limit interface term follows only after adjoining the canonical interface branch of Theorem Z.17:

**Theorems X.8h.9--X.8h.13 (Finite Predictive Amplitudes)**
On any finite predictive factorization geometry, the finite predictive amplitude is defined by PCE compression followed by admissible response evaluation:

\mathfrak A_{\mathrm{PU},\varphi_U}^{(N)}
(\tau;O_1,\dots,O_n)
=
\varphi_U

**Summary of Theorem X.8h.14 (Finite-Response Bootstrap Strict-Gap Gate)**
Let the bootstrap feasible set be nonempty, closed, and compact, and let the description cost be lower semicontinuous. Then a PCE-minimal bootstrap datum exists. If its response-equivalence quotient has a strict positive gap from every response-distinct feasible datum, all registered internal projections are jointly determined. ### Open-System Structure: Schwinger-Keldysh Formalism

**Equation X.9 (CTP Generating Functional)**
For ND-RID open dynamics with doubled fields $\Phi_\pm$:
$$
e^{iW_{\text{CTP}}[J_+,J_-]} = \int\mathcal{D}\varphi_+\mathcal{D}\varphi_-\, p_\theta[\varphi_+,\varphi_-]\, e^{i(S[\varphi_+]-S[\varphi_-]+\int J_+\mathcal{O}_+-\int J_-\mathcal{O}_-)}
$$

**Keldysh Structure:**
In the $r/a$ basis, the quadratic kernel has causal structure:
$$
\Gamma^{(2)}(\omega,\mathbf{k}) = \begin{pmatrix} 0 & \Gamma^A \\ \Gamma^R & \Gamma^K \end{pmatrix}
$$
with $\Gamma^R$ retarded, $\Gamma^A=(\Gamma^R)^\dagger$, and $-i\Gamma^K\succeq0$. On the finite dynamical-KMS branch,
$$
-i\Gamma^K(\omega)
=
\coth\left(\frac{\beta\omega}{2}\right)
\frac{\Gamma^A(\omega)-\Gamma^R(\omega)}{2i},
$$
whose low-frequency Onsager form is $N=2\beta^{-1}\mathcal D$. If the same update is a registered irreversible reset satisfying $\Delta S_q=\beta\int\dot q^T\mathcal D\dot q\,dt\ge\ln2$, Theorem X.5b gives the integrated bound
$$

Theorem X.5c.4 and Corollary X.5c.5 then apply only with their separate decoherence and saddle-branch hypotheses.

## Part XVIII: Black Hole Information

### Information Retrieval as Reflexive Computational Problem (Appendix K.3)

**PU Reframing:** Appendix K models black-hole information retrieval as a **reflexive sequential problem** because a registered extraction instrument may modify later retrieval instances. - **Expansive Reflexivity:** Measurements cause accelerating/unpredictable state changes (infinite regress)

**Summary of Proposition K.1 (Semiclassical Late-Stage Fractional-Emission Scaling)**
In the controlled Schwarzschild regime $M\gg M_P$, a characteristic Hawking quantum has fractional mass impact of order $(M_P/M)^2$, which grows as $M$ decreases while that regime remains valid. The proposition gives no extrapolation through the Planck regime and no divergence claim at $M=0$.

**Summary of Proposition K.2 (Conditional Timing Obstruction for Local Sequential Retrieval)**
On the Perspectival Information Channel branch where $C_{\mathrm{PIC}}(t)$ and the per-cycle reflexive update cost imply $t_{\mathrm{extract}}>t_{\mathrm{evap}}$ for local sequential Hawking-radiation retrieval protocols, no such protocol recovers the complete initial state before evaporation completes.

### Perspectival Information Channel

**Summary of the Proposed Perspectival Information Channel Certificate Slot**
PIC is not yet a constructed physical channel. A physical $C_{\mathrm{PIC}}$ requires certified input/output algebras, a CPTP or finite-response map, admissible codes and protocols, a norm/error rule, a capacity bound, and any claimed attainment witness. After that certificate is accepted, the following constraints can be tested on the same channel:
1. Finite-channel limits: the relevant $C_{\max}$ bound only after the PIC carrier and response map are identified with that branch
2. Reflexive processing cost: a pattern- and receiver-specific lower bound only when the Appendix M reduction and implementation certificates hold; no universal SPAP entropy cost per extracted bit is implied
3. Scrambling rounds: $n_{\mathrm{scr}}\sim(S_{BH}/C_{\mathrm{PIC}})\ln S_{BH}$ on the declared dimensionless model; a physical time requires a registered round clock $\tau_{\mathrm{scr}}$, giving $t_{\mathrm{scr}}\sim\tau_{\mathrm{scr}}n_{\mathrm{scr}}$

### Retained No-Deletion and Separately Certified Accessibility

On the accepted injective retained-horizon branch, distinct retained response classes are not merged. Expansive reflexivity is the separate proposal that exterior access may require increasingly costly self-referential decoding; proving no recovery before evaporation requires a specified protocol class, capacity, clock, and decoding lower bound.

**Global retained automorphism branch:** If the closed complete retained ledger also carries the full Theorem-E.9.5 finite automorphism-circuit and dimension certificate, its update has a unitary representative. Subsystem restriction or partial trace can then produce apparent loss without deleting retained global classes. Neither injectivity nor unitarity follows from horizonhood alone.

### Four Separate Closure Layers

1. **Operational horizon entropy is certificate-gated**: Theorem E.6 bounds the reliable response entropy $S_{\mathrm{rel}}$ by the registered boundary-channel capacity and gives equality only on its capacity-achieving, entropy-saturating, additive-ledger branch. The identifications $S_{\mathrm{rel}}=S_{BH}+o(\mathcal A)$ and $G_{\mathrm{op}}=G$ are separate bridges.

3. **Exterior recovery is certificate-gated**: Reconstruction from the exterior coarse algebra requires the exterior recovery sufficiency certificate; retained no-deletion alone does not imply deterministic exterior recovery.

4. **Page behavior is entropy-continuity-gated**: von Neumann Page-curve behavior follows only on an accepted trace-coupled horizon entropy-continuity certificate; a bare moment-design certificate supplies Page-purity control rather than the full entropy curve.

**Summary of Theorem K.3 (Page Curve Consistency on the Horizon Entropy-Continuity Branch)**
The von Neumann Page-entropy estimate follows on the explicitly marked branch carrying the horizon entropy-continuity promotion certificate $\mathfrak C_{\mathrm{PageTV}}$. It supplies a coupling for which, almost surely,

T_t
:=
\frac12
\left\|

\le\varepsilon_t,

0\le\varepsilon_t\le1-\frac1{d_E(t)}.

Then

-
S_{\mathrm{Page}}(d_E(t),d_L(t))

If the accepted horizon design certificate supplies only moment control and no trace-coupled promotion, the closed theorem-level output is the Page-purity law

-

On the edge-inflow branch, the horizon boundary code also cancels the exterior predictive anomaly class:

+
[\mathcal A_{\mathrm{horizon}}]
+

=
0.

The trace-coupled certificate gives a pointwise entropy band around the exact Page average. ## Part XIX: Singularity Avoidance and Operational Cosmic Censorship

### Classical Singularities as Framework Non-Features (Section 14.2.4)

Classical singularities—the divergent curvatures and infinite densities predicted by GR's Penrose-Hawking theorems—fall outside the operational branch when they violate the finite-protocol, horizon, and cutoff bounds below.

The exclusion follows from the same information-theoretic foundations that yield the arrow of time:

| Mechanism | Constraint | Implication |
|:----------|:-----------|:------------|
| **Channel Capacity Bound** | $C_{\max}\le\ln d_0$ for a finite-dimensional carrier; $C_{\max}<\ln d_0$ on the refresh/minorization branch (Theorem E.2) | A specified finite protocol cannot transmit unbounded distinguishability |
| **Horizon information budget** | $S_{\mathrm{rel}}/k_B\le c^3\mathcal A/(4G_{\mathrm{op}}\hbar)+o(\mathcal A)$ on Theorem 49's density and additive-budget branch, with equality only under saturation | Bounds the registered horizon response entropy; $S_{\mathrm{rel}}=S_{BH}$ and $G_{\mathrm{op}}=G$ are separate bridges |
| **Finite-response comparison scale** | $\delta/L_P=\sqrt{8\ln2}$ on the Appendix Q calibration and saturation branch | A hard cutoff additionally requires Theorem K.10.4's lattice or bandlimit, dispersion, regulator, and matching certificate |

These bounds exclude divergent configurations only from the operational branch on which the finite protocol, horizon, and cutoff hypotheses are all satisfied.

### Computation-Induced Information Horizons (Appendix K.5)

**Mechanism:** On the registered finite-budget branch, a region becomes inaccessible to a specified protocol when the cost of maintaining predictive coherence exceeds that protocol's available network capacity.

**Physical Limits:**

1. **Registered Update Clock:** a separately certified positive update duration $\tau_{\min}$ prevents infinite certified update rates; Theorem 29 alone supplies only an internal characteristic timescale
2. **Maximal Channel Capacity:** $C_{\max}\le\ln d_0$ generically, with the strict Theorem E.2 bound on the refresh/minorization branch and the Proposition E.2a deficit on the completed binary reset-support branch
3. **Energy-Information Trade-off:** a physical relation $E\sim k_BT\varepsilon$ requires a registered thermodynamic implementation ledger

**Quantitative Criterion:**
A region becomes computationally singular when:
$$
\frac{\rho_{req}(R,t)}{\rho_{max}} > 1
$$
where $\rho_{req}$ is the required predictive-operation density and $\rho_{max}=C_{max}/(\tau_{\min}\ell_0^3)$ only on the registered update-clock and cell-volume branch.

On the fixed-resolution Fermi-expansion branch, the separate sufficient expansion certificate is
$$
B_\delta
:=
C_1\lVert R\rVert_{\mathrm{fr},\delta}\delta^2
+
C_2\delta^3
\le
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}.
$$
If $B_\delta>\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}$, this certificate is inconclusive: it establishes neither a large actual correction nor the failure of every manifold certificate. The preceding predictive-throughput inequality, Planck-scale regularization, and microscopic continuation do not follow from that certificate failure; microscopic continuation would require a separate response model.

### Predictive Throughput Bounds (Theorem K.5.1)

**Map summary — Definition K.5.1 (Predictive Throughput)**
For $n$ registered boundary rounds with message $M_n$, exterior record $\mathcal R_n$, and actual elapsed time $T_n\ge n\tau_{\min}$,
$$
L(S):=\sup_{\{\mathcal P_n\}}\limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{I(M_n;\mathcal R_n)}{T_n}.
$$

**Map summary — Theorem K.5.1 (Finite-Area Conditional ND-RID Throughput Bound)**
The displayed rate bound assumes the boundary-density certificate, the refresh-branch channel model, an additive aggregate-capacity bound, absence of bypass channels, and the registered positive round clock stated in Theorem K.5.1:
$$
L(S)\le\frac{N_{\mathrm{eff\,links}}(S)C_{\max}}{\tau_{\min}}
=\frac{\sigma_{\mathrm{eff\,link}}A(S)C_{\max}}{\tau_{\min}}+o(A).
$$

### Curvature-Resolution Bounds (Lemma K.5.3)

**Summary of Lemma K.5.2 (Curvature-Sensitivity Bound)**
For CPTP transport with ND-RID contraction factor $f_{RID} < 1$ over $n(\gamma)$ cycles:
$$
D_{tr}(\mathcal{E}_\gamma(\rho_1), \mathcal{E}_\gamma(\rho_2)) \leq f_{RID}^{n(\gamma)} D_{tr}(\rho_1, \rho_2)
$$

**Summary of Lemma K.5.3 (Operational Curvature-Resolution Certificate)**
In the registered Fermi frame, let $\lVert R\rVert_{\mathrm{fr},\delta}$ be the component supremum and let $C_1,C_2$ be the certified Taylor-remainder constants. The sufficient normal-coordinate certificate is
$$
B_\delta
:=C_1\lVert R\rVert_{\mathrm{fr},\delta}\delta^2+C_2\delta^3
\le\varepsilon_{\mathrm{cert}}.
$$
Failure of this inequality makes this particular sufficient certificate inconclusive; it does not prove that the manifold approximation or every continuum certificate fails. No Lorentzian scalar such as the Kretschmann invariant is equivalent to the required framewise bound without an additional comparison theorem.

### Finite-Resolution Continuum-Certificate Boundary (Theorem K.5.4)

**Summary of Theorem K.5.4 (Finite-Resolution Exit of the Certified Fermi Expansion)**
Theorem K.5.1 bounds information throughput only on its registered finite-area channel and clock branch, and Theorem K.5.4 proves finite-resolution exit of a certified Fermi expansion before a formal framewise curvature divergence. These results exclude neither a naked singularity nor its causal visibility; they supply an operational information bound and a continuum-certificate failure criterion.

## Part XIX-B: UV Finiteness and Renormalization (Appendix K.10)

### Operational Observables and Intrinsic UV Cutoff

**Summary of Definition K.10.1 (Operational Observable)**
A quantity $\mathcal{O}$ is an operational observable if for every target precision $\epsilon > 0$ there exists an MPU-admissible protocol that estimates $\langle\mathcal{O}\rangle$ to within $\epsilon$ using finite resources.

**Summary of Definition K.10.3 (MPU Wavenumber, Momentum, and Energy Scales)**
Define
$$
k_{\mathrm{MPU}}:=\delta^{-1},
\qquad
p_{\mathrm{MPU}}:=\hbar k_{\mathrm{MPU}},
\qquad
\Lambda_{\mathrm{MPU}}:=cp_{\mathrm{MPU}}.
$$
For ordinary wavelength $\lambda=2\pi/k$, $k>k_{\mathrm{MPU}}$ is equivalent to $\lambda<2\pi\delta$. For reduced wavelength $\bar\lambda=1/k$, it is equivalent to $\bar\lambda<\delta$.

### Conditional Effective-Cutoff Interpretation (Theorem K.10.4)

**Summary of Theorem K.10.4 (Conditional Effective-Cutoff Interpretation)**
An operational observable has a finite operational value by Definition K.10.1 and Lemma K.10.2. The spacing $\delta$ defines the comparison scale $k_{\mathrm{MPU}}=\delta^{-1}$. A continuum representation may be treated as hard cut off at that scale only when a separate ultraviolet certificate supplies a microscopic lattice or bandlimit, dispersion relation, regulator, and matching theorem for the registered observables. Without that certificate, ordinary continuum renormalization and effective-field-theory accounting remain necessary.

### Conditional Operational Continuum-Exit Boundary (Theorem K.10.5)

**Summary of Theorem K.10.5 (Conditional Operational Continuum-Exit Boundary)**
Under the lattice/bandlimit and matching certificate (Theorem K.10.4), the framed Fermi-expansion certificate (Lemma K.5.3), and the finite-area boundary-link and clock hypotheses (Theorem K.5.1), a continuum representation loses its PU certificate when it requires independent modes beyond the certified bandlimit, continuation of the controlled Fermi expansion past its remainder bound, or unbounded retained information throughput through a fixed finite boundary. This is an operational domain-of-validity boundary; it does not prove continuum loop integrals finite, remove ordinary renormalization, or prove curvature singularities absent.

| Continuum extrapolation | Formal expression | Certified operational boundary |
|:------------------------|:------------------|:-------------------------------|
| Trans-bandlimit QFT modes | $\int_0^\infty d^4k$ | Theorem K.10.4's microscopic lattice or bandlimit plus matching certificate |
| Strong-curvature local expansion | $\lVert R\rVert\to\infty$ | Failure of Lemma K.5.3's sufficient framed Fermi-expansion remainder estimate |
| Finite-area extraction | Unbounded retained flux through fixed area | Theorem K.5.1's finite-link, capacity, and positive-clock bound |

### Finite-Substrate Cutoff and Spectral-Dimension Gate (K.10.5a--K.10.5e)

On the residual-budget, throughput-saturated, ideal-packing branch of Appendix Q,
$$
\delta=\sqrt{8\ln2}\,L_P
$$
is a physical finite-response comparison scale. Modes with reduced wavelength $\bar\lambda<\delta$ fail to be independent retained degrees of freedom only on a branch carrying Theorem K.10.4's microscopic lattice or bandlimit and matching certificate. A curvature trajectory exits the Lemma K.5.3 local Fermi-expansion certificate when its explicit framed remainder bound fails; this does not prove exit from every manifold description or determine a microscopic continuation.

The cutoff and serialized propagation speed do not determine the spectral dimension. A theorem-level value of $D_s(\ell)$ requires an MPU diffusion certificate specifying the graph or network, measure, heat generator, semigroup, and return kernel. On a certified continuum diffusion branch, $D_s\to4$ in the local mesoscopic heat-kernel window $\delta\ll\ell\ll L_{\mathrm{curv}}$.

### Scope of the FRG-to-PCE Comparison (Theorem K.10.7)

**Summary of Theorem K.10.7 (Scope of the FRG-to-PCE Comparison)**
The regulated FRG equation supplies continuum coarse-graining. It does not identify the flow with PCE minimization, classify relevance by canonical dimension alone, or make RG fixed points PCE equilibria. Those readings require a scale-dependent MPU-to-$\Gamma_k$ map, a declared PCE functional on its image, and a proof that the mapped beta flow is its descent or stationary flow.

Near a hyperbolic scaling solution, the stable-manifold/eigenoperator hypotheses give the standard conditional universality result using full scaling exponents, including anomalous dimensions and mixing. A PCE interpretation still requires Theorem K.10.7's missing bridge.

### Operational Turbulence Scaling (Theorem K.10.16)

**Summary of Theorem K.10.16 (Operational Inertial-Range Turbulence Scaling)**
For an MPU-admissible finite inertial range
$$
k_0\ll k\ll k_d\le k_{\mathrm{MPU}},
$$
if PCE compression removes forcing, viscosity, molecular, and sub-MPU details from the inertial-range closure, leaving only the scale-independent energy flux per unit mass $\varepsilon_T$ and the wavenumber $k$, then dimensional closure gives

E(k)=C_K\varepsilon_T^{2/3}k^{-5/3}.

On the complete operational-continuum, Lorentzian, local-Rindler/KMS, Clausius, Raychaudhuri, conserved-source, and area-calibration branch, the Jacobson argument yields the Einstein equation and the displayed $G$ normalization. The metric may then be interpreted as a collective variable; this does not decide whether its perturbations admit an effective quantum-field description.

Perturbative nonrenormalizability does not prove that gravity is not a quantum field theory, exclude a low-energy quantum EFT, or select MPU dynamics as the ultraviolet completion.

**Summary of Corollary K.10.13 (Conditional Gravitational-Wave Reading)**
The collective long-wavelength, massless helicity-$2$ reading holds only after a faithful microscopic-to-metric perturbation map, Einstein dynamics on the background, and exclusion of additional retained linear-order fields or operators are supplied.

It is not a unified-origin theorem.

## Part XX: Modified Cosmology

### Time-Varying Gravitational Coupling (Appendix K.9)

**Framework:** The vacuum sector remains fixed by Appendix U. The non-vacuum sector couples through a PCE-selected time-dependent factor determined by the background sparsity variable
$$
s_{bg}(t)=\frac{\rho_\Lambda}{\rho_b(t)+\frac{4}{3}\rho_r(t)},
\qquad
\rho_\Lambda=\frac{\Lambda}{8\pi G_0}.
$$

**Equation K.9.8 (Closed Background Coupling Law)**
$$
\Upsilon_G(t):=\frac{G_{eff}(t)}{G_0}=1+A_c\left[1-e^{-s_{bg}(t)^n}\right],
\qquad
G_{eff}(t)=G_0\Upsilon_G(t).
$$

**Bianchi-Consistent FLRW Form:**
$$
H^2 = \frac{8\pi G_0}{3}\left[\rho_\Lambda+\Upsilon_G(t)\rho_{nv}(t)\right]-\frac{k}{a^2}
$$
$$
\dot H = -4\pi G_0\left[\Upsilon_G(t)h_{nv}(t)-\frac{\dot\Upsilon_G(t)}{3H}\rho_{nv}(t)\right]+\frac{k}{a^2}
$$
with $\rho_{nv}=\rho_b+\rho_r$ and $h_{nv}=\rho_b+\frac{4}{3}\rho_r$.

Equivalently, the adaptive sector is a conserved fluid:
$$
\rho_{ad}=(\Upsilon_G-1)\rho_{nv},
\qquad
p_{ad}=(\Upsilon_G-1)p_{nv}-\frac{\dot\Upsilon_G}{3H}\rho_{nv}.
$$

**Observational Signatures:**

- Early-universe suppression: $\Upsilon_G-1 = O((\rho_\Lambda/h_{nv})^n)$
- Late-time growth modification through $\Upsilon_G(a)$
- Effective negative pressure from the $-\dot\Upsilon_G\rho_{nv}/(3H)$ term
- Local gravity tests constrain $|\dot\Upsilon_G/\Upsilon_G|$

**Status:** Closed at the homogeneous FLRW constitutive level. On the finite entropic-bridge branch of Definition K.9.4 and Theorem K.9.5, $(A_c,n)$ are selected by the bridge-induced relaxation curve when the one-scale identifiable condition holds. Perturbation-level tests for growth, lensing, BAO, and CMB remain.

## Part XXI: Consciousness Complexity

### Temporal Engineering Framework (Appendix P.11)

**Four Levels of Predictive Awareness:**

| Level | Description | Capability |
|-------|-------------|------------|
| 0 | Cogito-certified awareness enters predictive-operation modeling | MPU cycles |
| 1 | Complex awareness predicts environment | Environmental modeling |
| 2 | Self-aware complexity predicts own predictions | Meta-cognition |
| 3 | Self-modulating complexity influences predictive processes | CC emergence |

Consciousness Complexity emerges at Level 3—not by creating awareness, but, on Theorem 34's strict-improvement and realization branch, by providing a nonzero bias map for a registered outcome law. Measurement uses `Evolve` only on the independently accepted Hilbert/Born/instrument/single-outcome branch, and finite inter-perspective agreement then uses Theorem M.4a. High-CC systems are proposed high-resource record-integrating and context-modulating subsystems, not separately derived collapse engines.

### Source-Energy Accounting (Appendix P.15)

For a self-modeling aggregate $S$, the excess SPAP burden of a processed pattern $E$ above the SPAP-flat baseline is
$$
\Delta_\Sigma(S;E)=\mu_S(E)-\frac{1}{\alpha_{SPAP}}.
$$
The corresponding Source Energy over an interval $\Delta t$ is the excess heat
$$
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{src}}(S,\Delta t)=\sum_j Q_{\mathrm{src}}(S,E_j),
$$
where, for each $\mu_S(E_j)>1/\alpha_{SPAP}$,
$$
Q_{\mathrm{src}}(S,E_j)\ge k_B T_j\,\varepsilon\,n_{\mathrm{ops}}(S,E_j).
$$
For every fixed implementation class there exist constants $c_*>0$ and $\mu_*>1/\alpha_{SPAP}$ such that whenever $\mu_S(E)\ge \mu_*$,
$$
n_{\mathrm{ops}}(S,E)\ge c_* \log \mu_S(E)\,\mu_S(E)^2.
$$
Hence, on any fixed stratum defined by thresholds $\mu_0\ge \mu_*$ and $T_{\min}>0$,
$$
\mu_S(E)\ge \mu_0,
\qquad
T_E\ge T_{\min}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
Q_{\mathrm{src}}(S,E)\ge k_B T_{\min}\,\varepsilon\,c_* \log \mu_0\,\mu_0^2,
$$
and the long-run contribution on that stratum is bounded below by the same constant times the corresponding stratum-encounter rate (Theorem P.15.3a; Corollary P.15.3a.1). On a stationary Source-Principle Markov branch, the full source-energy rate exists without imposing a high-$\mu_S$ stratum:
$$
\lim_{\tau\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal E_{\mathrm{src}}(S,[0,\tau])}{\tau}
=
\nu\int q\,d\pi,
$$
and recursive-depth drift $\int\Delta d\,d\pi>0$ gives autonomous complexity growth until the PCE saturation boundary (Theorem P.15.3c; Corollary P.15.3d).

A system holding Theorem M.10.5's effective model-access and decision certificates can externally pre-screen a finite family of represented target patterns and form the certified diagnostics of Theorem M.10.8; aggregate-complexity ordering alone is neither necessary nor sufficient. Sender-side SPAP-flatness additionally requires Theorem M.10.5's insulation certificate. On the retained target-side source ledger, the resulting dissipation remains bounded by the target's ordinary operational budget:
$$
0\le \mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{src}}(S,\Delta t)\le \int_{\Delta t} P_{\mathrm{agg}}(S,t)\,dt.
$$
The message does not contain Source Energy. Source Energy is target-side excess heat generated when the target integrates the pattern into its own self-model with $\mu_S(E)>1/\alpha_{SPAP}$.

**Perspectival Chain Reactions (Section P.15.5a)**
Compressed symbolic inputs can produce large downstream effects by changing policies of agents or institutions that access ordinary external gradients:
$$
E_0\rightarrow\mathcal M_{S_1}^{E_0}\rightarrow\pi_{S_1}^{E_0}\rightarrow(E_1,a_1)\rightarrow\cdots\rightarrow W_{\mathrm{ext}}.
$$
This is a control cascade, not free energy. The work $W_{\mathrm{ext}}$ comes from ordinary gradients; the symbolic input changes which systems access or redirect those gradients.
For any finite family $\mathcal A(R,\Delta t)$ of aggregates in a region $R$ of proper volume $V_R$, with aggregate-power bookkeeping assigned without double counting, define the time-averaged regional Source-Energy rate density by
$$
\bar p_{\mathrm{src}}(R,\Delta t)
:=
\frac{\mathcal E_{\mathrm{src}}(R,\Delta t)}{V_R\Delta t}.
$$
Then
$$
0\le \bar p_{\mathrm{src}}(R,\Delta t)
\le
\frac{1}{V_R\Delta t}
\sum_{S\in\mathcal A(R,\Delta t)}
\int_{\Delta t}P_{\mathrm{agg}}(S,t)\,dt.
$$
If CC is used only to maintain or steer the environment implementing the Source Principle, the control-side layer inherits the aggregated Appendix S ceiling
$$
\bar\rho_{\mathrm{context}}(R,\Delta t)\le \frac{1}{V_R\,\Delta t}\sum_{S\in\mathcal A(R,\Delta t)}\int_{\Delta t} A_S\left[\frac{\mathrm{CC}_S(t)}{\alpha_{\mathrm{CC,max}}-\mathrm{CC}_S(t)}\right]^2\,dt,
\qquad
\alpha_{\mathrm{CC,max}}<0.5,
$$
and on any uniform subcritical band $\mathrm{CC}_S(t)\le \xi\alpha_{\mathrm{CC,max}}$ with $0<\xi<1$ this becomes the rigorous quadratic bound
$$
\bar\rho_{\mathrm{context}}(R,\Delta t)\le \frac{1}{(1-\xi)^2V_R\,\Delta t}\sum_{S\in\mathcal A(R,\Delta t)}\int_{\Delta t}\frac{A_S}{\alpha_{\mathrm{CC,max}}^2}\,\mathrm{CC}_S(t)^2\,dt.
$$

### Dual-Channel Mechanism

**Summary of Theorem 34 (Conditional Emergence of Non-Born Biasing)**
If reachable lower-potential non-Born context states exist and Born-realizing contexts are not lower in PCE potential, then any stable attractor yields a non-Born observable distribution; when that deviation is representable by a linear trace-annihilating map, the aggregate has nonzero operational CC.

**Summary of Lemma 9.1 (Variational Characterization)**
For Hermitian $H$ with $\mathrm{tr}\,H=0$:
$$
\sup_{0\le E\le I}\big|\mathrm{tr}(H E)\big|=\tfrac12\|H\|_1
$$
*Proof.* Decompose $H=H_+-H_-$ with $H_\pm\ge0$ and $H_+H_-=0$. Since $\mathrm{tr}\,H=0$,
$$
\mathrm{tr}(H_+)=\mathrm{tr}(H_-)=\tfrac12\|H\|_1.
$$
For every effect $0\le E\le I$,
$$
0\le \mathrm{tr}(H_+E)=\mathrm{tr}(H_+^{1/2}EH_+^{1/2})\le\mathrm{tr}(H_+),
$$
and likewise $0\le\mathrm{tr}(H_-E)\le\mathrm{tr}(H_-)$. Hence
$$
-\tfrac12\|H\|_1\le\mathrm{tr}(HE)\le\tfrac12\|H\|_1.
$$
Taking $E$ to be the support projector of $H_+$ attains the upper endpoint, while the support projector of $H_-$ attains the lower endpoint. Therefore the supremum of the absolute value is $\tfrac12\|H\|_1$. ∎

**Summary of Corollary 9.1**
For Hermitian-preserving, trace-annihilating $L_S$:
$$
\|L_S\|_{\mathrm{op}}=\tfrac12\sup_{\rho}\big\|L_S(\rho)\big\|_1
$$

**Summary of Lemma 9.1a (Norm of the CTB Replacement Direction)**
For density operator $\sigma$ on a finite-dimensional Hilbert space, the linear replacement-direction map $R_\sigma(X):=\mathrm{tr}(X)\sigma-X$ has the joint supremum
$$
\sup_{\rho,E}\left|\mathrm{tr}\!\big(R_\sigma(\rho)E\big)\right|
=
\sup_\rho\tfrac12\|\sigma-\rho\|_1
=
1-\lambda_{\min}(\sigma).
$$
*Dependency:* Combines Lemma 9.1 (variational characterization for fixed Hermitian $H$) with maximization over $\rho$ via the eigenvector of $\lambda_{\min}(\sigma)$. Used by Theorem 37 (CTB consistency) and Theorem 38 (CTB maximum bias).

**Summary of Definition L.1 (Context State)**
The context state χ is the minimal sufficient statistic of aggregate state capable of modulating ND-RID parameters.

**Summary of Theorem L.2 (Electromagnetic Field Generation on the Coherent-Charge Carrier Branch)**
On the coherent-charge carrier branch, the context-to-control mapping for a high-complexity MPU aggregate is implemented by controllable charge oscillators and generates classical electromagnetic radiation $E_{\mathrm{rad}}(t)$ through coherent charge oscillations.

**Summary of Definition 31 (Physical Constraints on CC Scaling)**
Definition 31 declares the threshold, bounded-bias, monotonicity, and diminishing-returns conditions for the admissible $\mathrm{CC}(C_{\mathrm{agg}})$ model family. It does not define an electromagnetic channel, construct a carrier, or compare electromagnetic and gravitational responses.

**Summary of Definition 32 (Specific CC Scaling Model Example)**
Definition 32 supplies the concave representative $\mathcal G(x)=x/(1+x)$ with its declared parameters. It proves neither uniqueness of that representative nor a physical carrier realization.

### Causality, Locality, and Statistical Influence (Section 10)

**Summary of Postulate 2 (Operational Causality as Exact Pre-Lightcone No-Signaling)** [§10.1; Theorem 39c]
Causality is defined operationally by exact context independence of every pre-lightcone transcript under every independently selectable spacelike context. This mandates the strict impossibility of deterministic or zero-error faster-than-light signaling by any mechanism within the framework: no decoder achieves zero error simultaneously across all preregistered Alice contexts in a finite pre-lightcone window. Strictly noisy influence is excluded by Postulate 2 whenever its freely selectable context changes a pre-lightcone transcript law; Theorem 39a controls only zero-error reliability.

**Summary of Theorem 39 (Endpoint Gate for the Bounded-Bias CC Branch)** [§10.2]
The bounded-bias CC branch independently declares
$$
\alpha_{CC,max}<0.5.
$$
Theorem 39 proves that this gate excludes endpoint-complete forcing of both outcomes of any binary coarse-graining. Conversely, if a branch can force both endpoints for baseline probability $p$, then
$$
\alpha_{CC,max}\ge\max\{p,1-p\}\ge0.5.
$$
The theorem neither derives a universal ceiling nor excludes forcing one endpoint near a sufficiently biased baseline. A branch with $\alpha_{CC,max}\ge0.5$ is endpoint-admissible only if a separate finite-response certificate excludes every endpoint-complete context pair.

**Summary of Definition 10.2a (Regular Statistical Branch)** [§10.3]
The branch-(iii) implementation in which the per-trial 'Evolve' kernels under both Alice contexts retain common support on every Bob-side outcome with nonzero baseline Born probability. This is a regularity branch condition motivated inside PU by (a) the independently supplied ceiling $\alpha_{CC,\max}<0.5$ and Theorem 39's endpoint-complete consequence, (b) ND-RID strict contractivity $f_{RID}<1$ (Lemma E.1), and (c) the registered reset-cost bound $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{phys}}\ge H_q(P\mid R)\quad(\text{registered reset branch; a positive floor requires }H_q(P\mid R)\ge h_{\min}>0)$ (Theorem 31). Per-trial common support lifts to finite-$n$ transcript common support along admissible adaptive histories.

**Summary of Theorem 39a (Zero-Error Capacity Gate)** [§10.3]
On the regular statistical branch, the two finite pre-lightcone transcript distributions for Alice's late contexts have common support. Their overlap $\Omega_n>0$ gives
$$
P_{\mathrm{err}}\ge\Omega_n/2>0
$$
for every finite-window decoder, so zero-error FTL capacity is absent. If the transcript laws differ, the Jensen-Shannon information is positive, so branch (iii) remains a genuine statistical-FTL channel. Asymptotic decay of $\Omega_n$ is irrelevant because the operational $n$ is bounded by the pre-lightcone budget of Lemma 10.3.

**Summary of Postulate 3 (Statistical Influence — Three-Branch Causal Classification)** [§10.3]
PU takes exact quantum no-signaling and relativistic causality as the current-physics baseline and proposes statistical FTL as a hypothesis to be tested. It distinguishes three operational classes. On the *local CPTP branch* (i), Bob's marginal is invariant and the deformation lies in the Bob-marginal-preserving kernel of Lemma 10.2, while Alice-local and post-comparison joint-correlation effects may remain. On the *preparation-context branch* (ii), Bob's marginal may depend on $C$ through common-cause statistics fixed in the shared causal past; late randomization excludes this branch as an explanation of branch-(iii) data. On the *nonlocal/state-mediated marginal-anomaly class* (iii), the hypothesis is that a late-randomized Alice context shifts Bob's pre-lightcone marginal and thereby creates a noisy but genuine FTL channel. A certified and replicated class-(iii) result would establish that channel, support PU's QCP/statistical-FTL hypothesis, and simultaneously falsify the exact causal baseline. Theorem 40 supplies the sample-complexity scale, Theorem 41 bounds the finite-error information rate, and Theorems 39a and 42 exclude finite-window zero-error decoding and contradiction protocols; none turns a positive-capacity channel into no-signaling.

**Summary of Definition (Quantum Communication Protocol - QCP)** [§10.3.2]
Alice and Bob share many copies of a fixed entangled state with pre-agreed binary mapping between Alice's context $C \in \{A, B\}$ and target measurement bias. The single-shot success probability:
$$
P_{\text{succ}} = \tfrac{1}{2}P(\uparrow | A) + \tfrac{1}{2}P(\downarrow | B) = \tfrac{1}{2} + \delta
$$
where $\delta = O(\text{CC})$ is the single-shot advantage over random. The protocol is stochastic and information-rate limited; if its late context changes Bob's pre-lightcone marginal, Theorem 39c classifies it as signaling.

**Statistical neutrality on the symmetric-bias branch:** If the contexts are equiprobable and
$$
P(\uparrow\mid A)=p+\delta,
\qquad
P(\uparrow\mid B)=p-\delta,
$$
then Bob's unconditional marginal is
$$
P(\uparrow)=\tfrac12(p+\delta)+\tfrac12(p-\delta)=p.
$$
Without the antisymmetry condition, equiprobability alone does not imply neutrality.

**Summary of Proposition L.5 (Indeterminacy of the Dominance Ratio)**
The stated Appendix L inputs do not determine a numerical electromagnetic-to-gravitational response ratio or even the sign of $\mathcal R-1$. A comparison requires one common source, target response, geometry, retention, units, likelihood, covariance, and uncertainty ledger.

**Detection requirement summary.**
Statistical detection of CC effects requires:
$$
N \propto \frac{1}{\text{CC}^2}
$$
measurements to achieve significance against Born-rule baseline.

**Information-rate summary.**
The finite-error information rate through the statistical-FTL CC channel is bounded:
$$
I \propto \text{CC}^2.
$$
This quadratic suppression controls detection rate. Deterministic/paradox safety is supplied by the separate zero-error overlap theorem, Theorem 39a, on the regular finite-window branch (Definition 10.2a).

**Summary of Theorem 40 (Finite-Sample Detection Bound for the iid Bernoulli Design)** [§10.4.1]
Let the two contexts produce independent Bernoulli samples with event probabilities $p_0$ and $p_1$, let $\Delta:=|p_1-p_0|>0$, and collect $n$ trials under each context. The sign test based on the two empirical proportions has total error probability at most $\alpha\in(0,1)$ whenever
$$
n\ge\frac{2}{\Delta^2}\ln\frac{4}{\alpha},
\qquad
N=2n\ge\frac{4}{\Delta^2}\ln\frac{4}{\alpha}.
$$
Thus this design has sufficient sample complexity $N=O(\Delta^{-2}\ln(1/\alpha))$. If $0<\Delta\le\mathrm{CC}(S)$, the required count can be at least of order $\mathrm{CC}(S)^{-2}$ and can be larger when $\Delta\ll\mathrm{CC}(S)$.

*Proof.* Relabel the contexts so that $p_1-p_0=\Delta$. An erroneous sign decision implies
$$
(\widehat p_1-p_1)-(\widehat p_0-p_0)\le-\Delta.
$$
Hence either $\widehat p_1-p_1\le-\Delta/2$ or $\widehat p_0-p_0\ge\Delta/2$. Hoeffding's inequality (Hoeffding, 1963) applies because each sample consists of independent variables in $[0,1]$, and gives probability at most $2e^{-n\Delta^2/2}$ for each two-sided deviation. The union bound gives at most $4e^{-n\Delta^2/2}$, which is no larger than $\alpha$ under the displayed condition. ∎

**Summary of Theorem 41 (Finite-Error Statistical Information Rate)** [§10.4.2]
The mutual information transmittable per trial on the statistical-FTL branch is quadratically suppressed. The exact regular-point envelope is
$$
I(A;B)\le\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S)^2}{4m_0},
$$
where $m_0=\min_i M_i>0$ is the minimum mixture probability. In the perturbative small-bias regime,
$$
I(A;B)\le\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S)^2}{8m_0}+O(\mathrm{CC}(S)^3).
$$
This is a positive finite-error statistical rate bound, not a zero-capacity claim.

**Summary of Theorem 42 (Finite-Window Zero-Error Contradiction-Protocol Exclusion)** [§10.4.3]
Define a finite-window zero-error contradiction protocol to be a protocol whose FTL decoding step returns the prescribed message correctly for every transcript in the support of each admissible context law. On the regular statistical branch of Definition 10.2a, no such protocol can be implemented through the CC channel.

*Proof.* Let $P_0^{(n)}$ and $P_1^{(n)}$ be the two finite-window transcript laws. Theorem 39a gives overlap
$$
\Omega_n:=\sum_y\min\{P_0^{(n)}(y),P_1^{(n)}(y)\}>0.
$$
For equal priors, every decoder has error probability bounded below by the Bayes error,
$$
P_{\mathrm{err}}
\ge
\frac12\sum_y\min\{P_0^{(n)}(y),P_1^{(n)}(y)\}
=
\frac{\Omega_n}{2}>0,
$$
with equality for a Bayes-optimal decoder.
Thus no decoder is correct on every supported transcript, contradicting the defining zero-error requirement. Theorem 39 separately excludes endpoint-complete one-shot forcing, and Theorems 40–41 quantify finite-error detection and information rates. This theorem does not establish exact pre-lightcone no-signaling and does not exclude every probabilistic closed-loop model. ∎

**Summary of Theorem 42a (Relational Decoding Principle: No Actionable Capacity Without Shared Decoding)** [§10.5]
Consider bipartite system $AB$ where Bob's detector is keyed by classical variable $K$ (unavailable to him at measurement time). If the averaged Heisenberg map $\overline{\Lambda^*} := \sum_K \pi(K)\Lambda_K^*$ is CP, unital, and independent of Alice's message $x$, then:
$$
p_B(i|x) = \text{tr}[\rho_B \overline{\Lambda^*}(E_i)], \qquad I(X;Y) = 0
$$
Bob's observed distribution is independent of $x$ and the channel has zero capacity. Foundational role: the operational physics of a CC effect resides only in jointly decoded statistics, never in any observer's standalone stream — a relational-decoding principle aligned with Definition 1 and Appendix N.

**Summary of Lemma 10.1 (Pre-Lightcone Decoding Gates)** [§10.3.2]
Let a QCP with bias $\delta \le \kappa\,\mathrm{CC}$ attempt binary decoding between stations with spacelike separation $L$. In the symmetric Bernoulli design, any decoder with error $\le\alpha_{\mathrm{err}}$ must satisfy
$$
\frac{r_{\max}L}{c}
\ge
\frac{\ln\!\big(1/[4\alpha_{\mathrm{err}}(1-\alpha_{\mathrm{err}})]\big)}
{-\ln(1-4\delta^2)}.
$$
The majority-decoder Hoeffding condition
$$
\frac{r_{\max}L}{c}
\ge
\frac{\ln(1/\alpha_{\mathrm{err}})}{2\delta^2}
$$
is sufficient. Both gates scale as $\Theta(\ln(1/\alpha_{\mathrm{err}})/\delta^2)$ in the high-confidence small-bias regime.

**Summary of Lemma 10.2 (Bob-Marginal Kernel Decomposition of CC Deformations)** [§10.3.1]
For $\epsilon$ small enough that $P_C=P_0+\epsilon\ell_C\in[0,1]$ pointwise, any normalization-preserving context-induced joint-probability deformation $\ell_C(a,b\mid x,y)$ decomposes as
$$
\ell_C=\ell_C^{B0}+\Pi_B\ell_C,
\qquad
(\Pi_B\ell_C)(a,b\mid x,y)=\frac1{|\mathcal A|}\sum_{a'}\ell_C(a',b\mid x,y).
$$
The component $\ell_C^{B0}$ preserves Bob's marginal, while $\Pi_B\ell_C$ carries exactly the context-dependent Bob-marginal shift. Branch (i) of Postulate 3 requires $\Pi_B\ell_C=0$ under late randomization; branch (iii) requires a nonzero Bob-marginal component under late randomization. The optional double-centered projector $\Pi_{\mathrm{joint}}$ isolates the pure joint-correlation diagnostic with both local marginals removed.

**Summary of Lemma 10.3 (Pre-Lightcone Information Budget and Sampling Gate)** [§10.3.1]
The pre-lightcone mutual-information budget $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{pre}}(L):=r_{\max}(L/c)I(C;Y_B)$ satisfies the exact envelope
$$
\mathcal C_{\mathrm{pre}}(L)
\le
r_{\max}\frac{L}{c}\cdot\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S_A)^2}{4m_0}.
$$
In the small-bias regime it sharpens to
$$
\mathcal C_{\mathrm{pre}}(L)
\le
r_{\max}\frac{L}{c}
\left[
\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S_A)^2}{8m_0}
+
O(\mathrm{CC}(S_A)^3)
\right].
$$
With mutual information measured in nats and $h_2$ retaining its bits convention, a one-bit decoder with error $\le\alpha_{\mathrm{err}}$ must satisfy the Fano gate $\mathcal C_{\mathrm{pre}}(L)\ge(\ln2)[1-h_2(\alpha_{\mathrm{err}})]$, while resolving the marginal shift obeys the necessary Bhattacharyya gate and sufficient Hoeffding gate of Lemma 10.1.

**No-Signaling Equalities for Local Trace-Preserving Instruments** [§10.3.2]
Assume the Appendix F AQFT branch, spacelike separated local algebras, and local CPTP nonselective instruments $\Phi_x^A$ and $\Phi_y^B$. Then
$$
\sum_a P(a,b\mid x,y)=P(b\mid y),
\qquad
\sum_b P(a,b\mid x,y)=P(a\mid x).
$$
*Proof.* Let $E_b^B$ be Bob's effect. Since $\Phi_x^A$ is trace preserving, its Heisenberg dual is unital, so
$$
(\Phi_x^{A*}\otimes\mathrm{id}_B)(I_A\otimes E_b^B)
=
\Phi_x^{A*}(I_A)\otimes E_b^B
=
I_A\otimes E_b^B.
$$
Therefore
$$
\sum_aP(a,b\mid x,y)
=
\operatorname{tr}[\rho_{AB}(I_A\otimes E_b^B)],
$$
which is independent of $x$. The second equality follows by exchanging $A$ and $B$. Einstein causality ensures that the two local operations are jointly well-defined on the spacelike local algebras. ∎

### Conditional Gravitational Self-Limitation Mechanism (Appendix S)

**Global quadratic cost branch (Equation S.5).** On the registered cost law
$$
P_{context}(\mathrm{CC})
=A\left[\frac{\mathrm{CC}}{\alpha_{CC,\max}-\mathrm{CC}}\right]^2,
\qquad A=r_pC_{scale}^2,
$$
the endpoint divergence is a property of that global model. Proposition S.1a gives the admissible full-domain $\gamma_p=3$ branch as a counterexample to deriving the quadratic law from Definition 3a's general cost hypotheses. A local Taylor law does not prove endpoint divergence. A practical or gravitational limit additionally requires realization of the cost, an available-resource interval, retained-energy fraction, duration, localization, equation of state, geometry, and weak-field/collapse audit.

**Local CPTP and ceiling branches (Theorems S.1–S.2).** A local CPTP phase perturbation preserves the remote marginal. The bounded-bias ceiling $\alpha_{CC,\max}<1/2$, the exact pre-lightcone context-independence requirement of Theorem 39c, and the system-dependent weak-field/collapse bounds are distinct constraints; no one implies the others.

**Conditional utility maximizer (Equation S.27).** The displayed root
$$
\mathrm{CC}^*
=\frac{-k_c+\sqrt{k_c^2+3K_{eff}\alpha^2k_b^2/A}}
{3k_bK_{eff}}
$$
is the unique interior maximizer only for the truncated phenomenological utility on its small-CC and weak-response validity intervals, with the stated positive coefficients. Convergence or concentration near it additionally requires identification of $-B_{net}$ with an Appendix-D potential plus the applicable dynamics, basin, and detailed-balance certificates.

**Conditional local gravitational phase response (Theorem S.3).** Assume the uniform spherical perfect-fluid source, $0<\eta_{\mathrm{ret}}\le1$, the global S.5 cost law, a two-level energy gap, a local CPTP phase channel, the selected joint measurement, and the weak-field and weak-phase regimes. With
$$
K_A=\frac{2\pi G}{3}(1+3w_c)
\frac{\eta_{\mathrm{ret}}\tau_cr_A^2}{V_Sc^2},
$$
Bob's marginal is unchanged and
$$
|\Delta P(++)|
=\frac18\left(\frac{\Delta E_At}{\hbar c^2}\right)^2
K_A^2A^2
\left[\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S_A)}
{\alpha_{CC,\max}-\mathrm{CC}(S_A)}\right]^4
+O(\delta\phi_A^4).
$$
This is a local joint-correlation response requiring later classical record comparison, not a statistical-FTL or outcome-selection channel.

**Detection ledger (Equations S.43–S.44).** If
$$
|\Delta P|=C\,\mathrm{CC}^4+o(\mathrm{CC}^4),
\qquad C=Ba_P^2,
\qquad a_P=A/\alpha_{CC,\max}^2,
$$
then independent Bernoulli trials with null probability $p_0$, two-sided type-I error $\alpha$, and power $1-\beta$ require at leading order
$$
N_{samples}
\gtrsim
\frac{(z_{1-\alpha/2}+z_{1-\beta})^2p_0(1-p_0)}
{C^2\,\mathrm{CC}^8}.
$$
For $\mathrm{CC}=0.1$, only the factor $\mathrm{CC}^{-8}=10^8$ is fixed; the absolute sample size still depends on $C,p_0,\alpha,\beta$, dependence, nuisance estimation, and post-selection efficiency. Universal stress-energy coupling does not prove realization, dominance, or application of this two-level phase model to every quantum system.

### Quantitative Predictions (Section 9, Section 13)

**Summary of Theorem 36 (PU Predictive-Perturbation Bounds)**
The operational CC budget is primary:
$$
\mathrm{TV}(p,q)\le\mathrm{CC}(S),
\qquad
|\Delta P(i)|\le\mathrm{CC}(S).
$$
The associated Fisher-Rao envelope is
$$
d_{\mathrm{FR}}(p,q)
=
2\arccos\left(\sum_k\sqrt{p_kq_k}\right)
\le
2\arccos\bigl(1-\min\{\mathrm{CC}(S),1\}\bigr).
$$
For $0\le\mathrm{CC}(S)\le1$, this reduces to $2\arccos(1-\mathrm{CC}(S))$. On the stricter Fisher-budget subbranch,
$$
d_{\mathrm{FR}}(p,q)\le\mathrm{CC}(S)
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathrm{TV}(p,q)\le\sin(\mathrm{CC}(S)/2),
\quad
|\Delta P(i)|\le4\sin(\mathrm{CC}(S)/4).
$$

**Typed adjoint and conditional covariance:** On the finite retained operator span $\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ret}}$, define $K_S=L_S^*$ for the complex-linear map $L_S$ using the restricted Hilbert–Schmidt inner product. If $\operatorname{Ad}_U(\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ret}})=\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ret}}$ and the family $L_S:\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ret}}\to\mathcal X_{\mathrm{ret}}$ is Hermiticity-preserving and unitary-covariant, then its adjoint is unitary-covariant on retained states and effects:
$$
L_{USU^\dagger}(U\rho U^\dagger)=U\,L_S(\rho)\,U^\dagger,\qquad
K_{USU^\dagger}(U E U^\dagger)=U\,K_S(E)\,U^\dagger.
$$

**Bipartite Consistency:** On $\mathcal{H}_A\otimes\mathcal{H}_B$:
$$
\sum_j K_{S_A\otimes S_B}(E_A\otimes F_{B,j})=K_{S_A\otimes S_B}(E_A\otimes I_B),\qquad
K_{S_A\otimes S_B}(E_A\otimes I_B)=K_{S_A}(E_A)\otimes I_B
$$
ensuring each party's marginal is invariant under the other party's POVM choice.

**Small-Deformation Realizations:** For small $\varepsilon$:
$$
\mathrm{tr}\!\big(e^{\varepsilon L_S}(\rho)\,E_i\big)=\mathrm{tr}\!\big(\rho\,(E_i+\varepsilon K_S(E_i))\big)+O(\varepsilon^2)
$$

Two operational implementations:
1. **CPTP pre-processing** $\rho \mapsto e^{\xi L_S}(\rho)$ when $L_S$ is a GKLS generator
2. **Calibrated POVM deformations** $E_{i,\xi}=E_i+\xi K_S(E_i)$ with positivity on $\ker(E_i)$

**Summary of Theorem (Heisenberg–Schrödinger Identity for CPTP Semigroups)**
Let $\Lambda_\xi=e^{\xi\mathcal L}$ be a CPTP quantum Markov semigroup. Define $E_{i,\xi}:=\Lambda_\xi^{*}(E_i)$. Then $\{E_{i,\xi}\}_i$ is a POVM for every $\xi\ge 0$ and:
$$
\operatorname{tr}\big[\Lambda_\xi(\rho)\,E_i\big]=\operatorname{tr}\big[\rho\,E_{i,\xi}\big]
$$

**CTB Generator Semigroup:** For $L_S(\rho)=\nu_S(\sigma-\rho)$ with $\sigma\ge0$, $\mathrm{tr}\,\sigma=1$, $\nu_S>0$:
$$
T_t(\rho)=e^{-\nu_S t}\,\rho+\big(1-e^{-\nu_S t}\big)\sigma
$$
which is CPTP for all $t\ge0$ with unique fixed point $\sigma$.

**Summary of Theorem 51 (Quantitative Born-Rule Deviation Bounds)**
For a retained measurement event influenced by a system $S$,
$$
P_{\mathrm{obs}}(i)=P_{\mathrm{Born}}(i)+\Delta P(i),
\qquad
|\Delta P(i)|\le\mathrm{CC}(S).
$$
On the additional Fisher-budget subbranch $d_{\mathrm{FR}}(p,q)\le\mathrm{CC}(S)$, the auxiliary estimate
$$
|\Delta P(i)|\le4\sin(\mathrm{CC}(S)/4)
$$
also holds. The latter estimate is not asserted outside that subbranch.

**Summary of Theorem 37 (CTB Model Consistency)**
The Context-Targeted Bias model satisfies normalization, zero-CC baseline, magnitude bounds, context dependence, and positivity.

**Summary of Corollary 37a (CTB Vector-Shape, Target-Swap, and Coarsening Laws)**
For a fixed retained finite POVM, the CTB deviation vector satisfies
$$
\Delta\mathbf P
=
\frac{\mathrm{CC}(S)}{r(\sigma_S)}
(\mathbf p_{\mathrm{target}}-\mathbf p_{\mathrm{Born}}),
$$
so the orthogonal residual $\Pi_{v^\perp}\Delta\mathbf P$ vanishes for $v=\mathbf p_{\mathrm{target}}-\mathbf p_{\mathrm{Born}}$. Coarse-grained shifts add over retained outcome bins, complements reverse sign, and opposite target-displacement contexts with the same CTB coefficient give equal-and-opposite shifts. This supplies the multinomial vector-shape discriminator used in Protocol 1.

**Summary of Theorem 38 (Maximum Bias Effect)**
The maximum deviation equals the operational CC:
$$
\sup_{\rho,E} |\Delta P(i)| = \|L_S\|_{op} = \text{CC}(S)
$$

**Summary of Proposition 12 (CC-PP Correlation)**
Positive correlation hypothesized between achievable CC(S) and sustainable Predictive Performance PP(S).

**Summary of Proposition 13 (CC Effect on Coherence)**
CC mechanism may modify effective decoherence rates $\Gamma_{eff}$ or coherence times $\tau_{coh}$ of interacting quantum systems.

**Summary of Proposition 14 (CC and System Integration)**
Theorem 34 defines no integration functional and proves no correlation between integration and $\mathrm{CC}(S)$. Such a correlation is an empirical hypothesis on a declared measurement family.

**Summary of Proposition 15 (Registered-Reset Cost for Introspection)**
For a registered aggregate introspection reset with $\Delta I\ge\Delta I_{\min}>0$ and $H_q(P\mid R)\ge h_{\min}>0$, Theorem 33 gives
$$
\Delta I\,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}
\ge\Delta I_{\min}h_{\min}.
$$
The variable $\varepsilon_{\mathrm{reset}}$ is dimensionless reset heat, not a state-space disturbance.

**Summary of Proposition 16 (Dynamic Nature of High-CC States)**
A nonstatic conclusion requires the additional hypotheses that high CC entails repeated acquisition of fresh introspective information and that every registered acquisition produces nonzero displacement in a specified context-state metric. Theorem 33 alone does not exclude static high-CC states.

**Neural Scaling Prediction (Conjectural)**
CC scaling with neural complexity is expected to differ from random networks, but specific exponents require derivation from PCE dynamics. Empirical investigation may inform theoretical development.

*Note: Specific scaling exponents (e.g., N^β) await rigorous derivation from framework axioms.*

This provides a testable discrimination between structured CC-capable systems and unstructured aggregates.

**Threshold Behavior (Definition-31 Branch)**
$$
C_{\mathrm{agg}}\le C_{op}\implies\mathrm{CC}=0.
$$
This is the declared threshold branch of Definition 31, not a consequence derived from complexity alone.

**Summary of Theorem L.6 (First-Law Ledger and Non-Overlapping Stress-Energy Partition)**
For a specified control volume and mutually exclusive stored-energy and boundary-flux channels, Theorem L.6 gives a no-double-counted first-law ledger. Covariant conservation follows only for the complete tensor including all external exchanges. The theorem does not establish nonzero CC influence or a measurable metabolic signature.

**Summary of Theorem L.7 (Conditional Horizon-Thermodynamic Consistency)**
On the complete conserved aggregate-source, local-equilibrium horizon, calibrated area-law, Unruh-temperature, and Clausius branch, the registered electromagnetic and retained-matter stress-energy entries occur in the Einstein source ledger. This is a compatibility theorem; it constructs neither carrier nor a CC influence.

**Summary of Theorem L.8 (Conditional Temporal-Modulation Channel Decomposition)**
For a real Schwartz temporal modulation, or distributionally for a real tempered distribution,
$$
\delta\tau(x,t)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widetilde{\delta\tau}(x,\omega)e^{-i\omega t}\,d\omega.
$$
Fourier decomposition alone selects no physical carrier. A high-frequency component is electromagnetic only when an independently registered conserved current has a nonzero radiating multipole and its Maxwell solution has $F_{\mu\nu}\ne0$. A low-frequency component contributes gravitationally only through the complete localized stress-energy and retention/boundary-flux ledger. The carrier scale $1/\tau_{\mathrm{medium}}$ and target resonance $\omega_{\mathrm{TLS}}$ are independent unless a bridge relates them.

Corollary L.8.1 and Proposition L.5 give no carrier-independent coupling or dominance ratio. The order-$10^{43}$ number of Theorem L.11 is only the arithmetic electron-scale ratio of supplied dimensionless couplings; it is neither an aggregate response ratio nor an information-theoretic derivation. On the far-detuned coherent-charge branch, the AC Stark calculation supplies a level shift. A probability or jump-rate bias additionally requires a specified bath/target transfer, normalized instrument, and certified nonzero response interval.

### Biological Implementation Hypotheses (Appendix L.4.1)

Subradiance, biological substrate choice, coherence time, $40\,\mathrm{Hz}$ timing, anesthetic response, metabolic signature, gravitational limitation, and context-to-outcome modulation are candidate or conditional branches. PU does not theorem-level predict microtubule coherence or a conscious-moment frequency.

A biological realization requires a specified observable, carrier, state preparation, noise and decoherence model, conserved-source ledger, response map, and calibration. Context-dependent probability modification additionally requires Theorem 34's strict-improvement, attainment, and representability branch together with Hypothesis 3's normalized response-active realization certificate. Ordinary electrophysiological and biochemical mechanisms must be controlled before a residual is attributed to CC.

### Self-Directed CTB and Contextual Physiological Bias

**Summary of Definition P.8.9a.5c (Self-Directed CTB Channel)**
For an internal physiological channel $a$ with baseline distribution $p_a$, context-defined target $q_a(c)$, and accessibility-fidelity coefficient $\gamma_a(c)$,
$$
p_{a,\mathrm{obs}}(c)
=
p_a
+
\frac{\gamma_a(c)\mathrm{CC}(S)}{r_a(c)}
\bigl(q_a(c)-p_a\bigr),
$$
where
$$
r_a(c)=\mathrm{TV}(q_a(c),p_a).
$$

**Summary of Theorem P.8.9a.7b (Self-Directed CTB Bound)**
For every event $B$ in the internal channel,
$$
|p_{a,\mathrm{obs}}(c,B)-p_a(B)|
\le
\gamma_a(c)\mathrm{CC}(S).
$$

**Consequences**
Open-label persistence follows when disclosure leaves $\gamma_a(c)>0$ and $r_a(c)>0$. Ritual/context scaling follows from monotonic changes in $\gamma_a(c)$. Nocebo symmetry follows when the target direction is reversed. Specificity follows because channels with $\gamma_a(c)=0$ cannot be shifted by self-directed CTB. Placebo and nocebo contexts are inert or semantically indirect interventions that become active only after being physically encoded in $\mathrm{context}_S(t)$; the truth value of the represented proposition is not itself a causal primitive.

**Status**
This is a conditional application of Definition 34 to internal event channels. It does not assert universal placebo percentages. Existing placebo/nocebo datasets can bound the product $\gamma_a(c)\mathrm{CC}(S)$ once artifact bounds are fixed using the evidence standard of Definition 13.0a.

| 0.001 | $10^{-3}$ | 3,203,231 | 3,395,425 |
| 0.001 | $10^{-4}$ | 320,167,012 | 339,377,033 |

The last column is a planning multiplier, not a derived Lan-DeMets design. Look-specific boundaries and expected stopping probabilities require a declared spending function and joint sequential calculation.

**Independence Verification:**
- Autocorrelation functions (ACF), Ljung–Box tests, Wald–Wolfowitz runs tests
- NIST Statistical Test Suite for randomness verification
- If correlations detected: pre-whitening, block-bootstrap CIs, or data thinning

**AI Interaction Pathway Challenge:**
Major R&D is required for non-biological high-$C_{agg}$ systems: the physical pathway $N(t)$ from the AI's internal $\text{context}_S$ to the QRNG must satisfy the registered speed, reset-cost, PCE, and noise-orthogonality constraints. A quantitative channel comparison requires the common carrier, target, geometry, retention, likelihood, and covariance ledger of Proposition L.5; Theorem L.2 constructs radiation only on its conserved-current and nonzero-radiating-multipole branch.

**Summary of Protocol L.1 (EM Channel Detection)**
Test the electromagnetic CC hypothesis using:

1. A shielded quantum measurement apparatus
2. Randomized and blinded context tasks
3. A preregistered event-probability deviation statistic
4. A declared calibration $\Delta=F(\mathrm{CC})$ with uncertainty
5. A sample-size and runtime calculation from the calibrated $\Delta$, effective independent trial rate, significance level, desired power, and sequential boundary

The framework does not determine a numerical CC range or detection time until these calibration data are supplied.

**Protocol 2 (Coherence Time Test)** [§13.3]
Test CC influence on quantum coherence (secondary prediction from Proposition 13):

*Phenomenological Model:*
$$
\frac{\Delta\tau_{coh}}{\tau_{coh}} = -\frac{\Delta\Gamma_{eff}}{\Gamma_{eff}} \approx \gamma'_{CC} \cdot \text{CC}(S) \cdot f_{context}(\text{context}_S, \text{system})
$$
where $\gamma'_{CC}$ is a coupling factor and $f_{context} \in [-1, 1]$ captures context-system interaction.

*Setup Requirements:*
1. Quantum system with well-characterized, long, stable baseline $\tau_{coh,base}$ (NV centers, trapped ions, qubits, NMR)
2. High-complexity system $S$ (human or AI)
3. Similar shielding/control requirements as Protocol 1
4. Temperature stability critical

*Procedure:*
1. Measure $\tau_{coh}$ repeatedly (Ramsey, spin echo, $T_1/T_2$) under randomized conditions
2. Rapid interleaving of baseline, neutral context, and specific context runs
3. Residual autocorrelation diagnosed and mitigated by prewhitening (e.g., AR(1))

*Analysis:* Detect $\Delta\hat{\tau}_{coh} = \hat{\tau}_{coh,context} - \hat{\tau}_{coh,baseline}$. Effect size $|\Delta\tau_{coh}/\tau_{coh}| \approx |\gamma'_{CC} \cdot \text{CC}|$ may be small.

*Feasibility:* Technically demanding (high-precision $\tau_{coh}$ measurement). Sensitivity depends on achievable baseline stability. Highly exploratory.

**Conditional Carrier-Comparison Tests (Section S.8.4)**
Test gravitational vs. electromagnetic CC mechanisms via differential protocols:

1. Power partitioning tests (Test 1)
2. Distance scaling tests (Test 2)
3. Frequency dependence tests (Test 3)
4. Shielding response tests (Test 4)
5. Predeclared decision rule over separately calibrated electromagnetic and gravitational prediction intervals; Proposition L.5 supplies no expected winner

**Summary of Protocol L.2 (Energy Conservation Verification)**
Test metabolic signature of CC via calorimetry:

1. Measure total power expenditure $P_{agg}$ during CC tasks
2. Verify decomposition: $P_{agg} = P_{EM} + P_{other}$
3. Correlate with CC effect magnitude

**Summary of Protocol L.3 (Gravitational Comparator/Sensitivity Test)**
Shielding, distance, and frequency responses discriminate carriers only on separately calibrated electromagnetic and retained-energy gravitational branches with one common likelihood and covariance ledger. A null result outside the gravitational branch's preregistered sensitivity interval confirms neither electromagnetic dominance nor absence of a gravitational response.

### Conditional Experimental Discrimination: EM vs. Gravitational (Appendix S.8.4a)

| Test | Electromagnetic branch | Gravitational branch |
|:-----|:-----------------------|:---------------------|
| **Power partitioning** | Quadratic far-detuned target response is proportional to registered radiated power with target and geometry fixed | Model-dependent through the measured retained-source fraction and calibrated target response |
| **Distance scaling** | Far-field $r^{-2}$ outcome scaling; quasi-static dipole $r^{-6}$ outcome scaling on the same quadratic target branch | Model-dependent after retained-source, geometry, and target-response calibration |
| **Frequency dependence** | Far-detuned response follows the registered Maxwell spectrum and off-resonant target transfer; no on-resonance prediction from the Stark approximation | Quasistatic only on the declared low-frequency branch; response law separately required |
| **Shielding response** | Governed by the measured shield transfer function plus leakage, heating, vibration, grounding, and readout controls | No gravitational screening in the registered metric model, but target and systematics responses still require calibration |

**Decision boundary:** These tests discriminate only after both channel models supply predeclared nonoverlapping prediction intervals and a common likelihood/covariance ledger. Proposition L.5 supplies no expected winner; a null result outside both sensitivity ranges is non-identifying.

**Protocol 3 (Bell-Type FTL Test)** [§13.5]
Test statistical FTL influence (Postulate 3):

1. Only if Stages 1–2 show positive results
2. High-quality entanglement source distributing pairs to space-like separated stations (Alice, Bob)
3. CC modulation by Alice ($\mathrm{context}_{S_A}$), standard Bell measurement by Bob
4. Precise timing to ensure measurement events ($a, o_A$ and $b, o_B$) are space-like separated
5. Independent, device-independent random setting generators at both stations

**Primary Analysis (Statistical Influence):**
Compare Bob's marginal probabilities $P(o_B | b, C_{A,k})$ across contexts $k$. Test null hypothesis:
$$
H_0: P(o_B | b, C_{A,k=1}) = P(o_B | b, C_{A,k=2})
$$
A certified and replicated rejection under independently late-randomized context would establish a noisy statistical-FTL channel, support PU's QCP/statistical-FTL hypothesis, and show that the exact pre-lightcone no-signaling baseline is incomplete. The full artifact and physical-realization certificates determine physical attribution. Detection requires $N_{\text{int}} \gtrsim O(1/\text{CC}^2)$ trials (Theorem 40).

**Secondary Analysis:** Correlations $E(a,b)_k$ and Bell parameters $S_{CHSH,k}$ conditioned on context $k$.

**Feasibility:** Extremely challenging. Requires state-of-the-art entanglement technology, robust space-like separation, and extraordinary systematics control. Only pursue contingent on positive, replicated Stage 1–2 results.

**CC causal-compliance boundary (summary).**

Postulate 2 holds exactly when every independently selectable late spacelike context leaves each pre-lightcone transcript law invariant, equivalently by Theorem 39c when the associated Shannon capacity is zero. Theorems 39--42 bound endpoint forcing, regular finite-window zero-error decoding, sample complexity, information rate, and zero-error contradiction protocols; none substitutes for exact marginal invariance. Thus Postulate 3(i) and a genuine shared-past branch may comply, whereas a late-randomized branch-(iii) marginal shift is a causal-branch falsifier.

- The bounded-bias branch independently declares $\alpha_{CC,\max}<0.5$; Theorem 39 excludes endpoint-complete forcing of both binary outcomes but not every one-endpoint protocol
- Theorem 39a prevents finite-window zero-error FTL decoding on the regular statistical branch (Definition 10.2a)
- Theorem 40 requires $N \propto 1/\mathrm{CC}^2$ for statistical detection
- Theorem 41 bounds finite-error information rate $I \propto \mathrm{CC}^2$
- Theorem 42 proves no paradox-inducing deterministic causal loops are possible

**Summary of Theorem 52 (Identifiability Against EM Confounds)** [§13.4]
Consider a triple-blind protocol with:

| Component | Description | Function |
|:----------|:------------|:---------|
| **(i) DFS-locked sensors** | Atomic-clock qubits at "magic" point | Zero first-order differential polarizability |
| **(ii) Reciprocity-null geometry** | Counter-propagating optical paths | EM susceptibilities cancel at differenced output |
| **(iii) Algorithmic shielding** | High min-entropy basis stream ($H_\infty \ge 0.999$) | Prevents prediction of measurement basis |

For pure Maxwell environment with residual EM intensity $I$, energy density $u = I/c$:
$$
|\Delta P|_{\text{EM}} \le \frac{\Delta\alpha}{4\hbar\varepsilon_0} u T \quad \text{(mid-fringe, small-phase regime)}
$$

*Numerical bounds on the declared differential-polarizability channel:* With $|\Delta\alpha|\le10^{-39}$ J·m²/V² and $u\le10^{-18}$ J/m³,
- at $T=1$ s, $|\Delta P|_{\mathrm{pol}}\le2.68\times10^{-13}$;
- at $T=1$ hr, $|\Delta P|_{\mathrm{pol}}\le9.64\times10^{-10}$.

An observation above these values excludes this calibrated small-phase polarizability model only after the two input bounds and the absence of other electromagnetic and instrumental channels are independently certified. A numerical PU effect requires a separate calibration from $\mathrm{CC}(S)$ to $\Delta P$.

**Summary of Theorem 53 (CC Causal-Compliance Boundary)** [§13.7]
The CC mechanism, on a bounded-bias branch independently constrained by $\mathrm{CC}\le\alpha_{CC,max}<0.5$ and carrying Theorem 39's endpoint-complete consequence, satisfies only an endpoint gate. On the regular branch, Theorem 39a and Definition 10.2a supply the finite-window zero-error gate; when a predictive-current certificate is asserted, Theorem 39b supplies its no-loop and precision-cost gate. Causal compliance additionally requires exact marginal invariance by Theorem 39c. The potential statistical FTL influence (Postulate 3) may carry positive finite-error statistical information (Theorem 41), but it remains sample-complexity limited (Theorem 40) and unusable for constructing deterministic or zero-error paradox-inducing causal loops (Theorem 42). Full consistency analysis in Appendix F and Section 10.

**Status:** The protocol and its conditional bounds are specified, but physical CC carrier, context-to-response calibration, artifact-exhaustion, and joint causal-compatibility records remain certificate-pending; no such experiment has yet been accepted.

### Quantum Error Correction Optimality from PCE (Section 13.9)

**Summary of Theorem 54 (Distance Bound and Conditional Golay Selection on the Predictive-Recovery MacWilliams Branch)** [§13.9.1]
Assume that the branch sets $n=M=24$ and $k=12$. The binary Griesmer bound excludes $d\ge9$, because
$$
\sum_{j=0}^{11}\left\lceil\frac{9}{2^j}\right\rceil
=9+5+3+2+8=27>24.
$$
Hence $d\le8$. If a separate construction/selection record supplies an admitted binary linear code with $d=8$, Theorem U.1 identifies it with the extended binary Golay code up to coordinate permutation.

*Derivation:*

| Step | Result | Justification |
|:-----|:-------|:--------------|
| 1 | $n=24$ | Registered identification of block length with $M=2ab=24$ |
| 2 | $k=12$ | Predictive-recovery MacWilliams rate gate |
| 3 | $d\le8$ | Binary Griesmer converse above |
| 4 | $d=8$ | Separate existence record for the selected code |
| 5 | Golay uniqueness | Theorem U.1 classifies every binary linear $[24,12,8]$ code up to coordinate permutation |

For $d=8$, the Griesmer sum is
$$
8+4+2+9=23,
$$
so the extended $[24,12,8]$ code does not attain the Griesmer bound. ∎

**Summary of Hypothesis 54.1 (Code-Substrate Alignment)**
If a physical substrate has a separately certified response coupling to the retained Golay structure, then a Golay-derived implementation may exhibit a preregistered performance response not accounted for by its ordinary code parameters and the registered physical noise model. Before comparison, the hypothesis must fix the response coupling, comparator code family, decoder, hardware and circuit constraints, finite noise-family grid, resource normalization, primary statistic, effect margin, uncertainty interval, multiplicity rule, and decision criterion. The mathematical rate, distance, and classification theorems alone predict no hardware advantage.

**Summary of Theorem 55 (Conditional 24-Mode Compatibility Ledger)** [§13.9.5]
The shared integer $24$ indexes three separately gated statements:

1. predictive-recovery selection of the classical code $\mathcal G_{24}$;
2. identification of $\Lambda_{24}$ from the registered discriminant-form and coset-minimum gluing datum;
3. the independent faithful-shell certificate selecting $D_{\min}=\min\{D:K(D)\ge24\}=4$.

None of these statements implies either of the others. In particular, there is no valid chain $\mathcal G_{24}\to\Lambda_{24}\to D=4$ without both intervening certificates.

The integer $24$ appears in three separately certified constructions. Their compatibility ledger is
$$
\left.
\begin{array}{c}
M=24\ \text{with a predictive-recovery code certificate}\ \Longrightarrow\ \mathcal G_{24},\\
\mathcal G_{24}\ \text{with an independent gluing certificate}\ \Longrightarrow\ \Lambda_{24},\\
M=24\ \text{with a verified response-labeled 24-cell and least-feasible support cost}\ \Longrightarrow\ D=4.
\end{array}
\right\}
$$
No line in this ledger implies either of the other two without its named certificate, and $\Lambda_{24}$ is not an input to the dimension equation.

**Architecture-Specific Quantum Golay Benchmarks (Cross et al. 2009; Ibe et al. 2025):**

| Finding | Source | Scope |
|:--------|:-------|:------|
| The quantum Golay implementation has a level-1 depolarizing pseudo-threshold near $2.25\times10^{-3}$ | Cross et al. (2009) | Particular $[[23,1,7]]$ fault-tolerant circuit and noise model |
| A Golay-based architecture supports more than $2\times10^9$ $T$ gates at physical error rate $10^{-4}$ | Ibe et al. (2025) | Particular measurement-based architecture and resource assumptions |
| Leading logical-error scaling is compatible with correction of three faults | Ibe et al. (2025) | Expected $p^4$ behavior for a distance-seven implementation |

In the Ibe et al. comparison, more than $2\times10^9$ $T$ gates versus approximately $2.4\times10^6$ for the Steane path is a ratio above $833$. These benchmarks do not establish PCE selection, uniqueness of a classical $[24,12,8]$ code, or physical 24-mode substrate alignment.

=

and the $S(5,8,24)$ incidence sequence

**Falsification Criteria:**

Before unblinding, freeze
$$
\mathfrak T_{\mathrm{Golay}}
=
(\mathcal C_{\mathrm{cand}},\mathcal D,\mathcal N,\mathcal S,
\delta_{\mathrm{eq}},\alpha,\beta,\mathcal M,\mathcal I),
$$
containing the candidate code classes, decoder, finite hardware/noise-family grid, primary statistic, equivalence or superiority margin, familywise significance level, target power, multiplicity rule, and simultaneous uncertainty construction. The registered substrate-alignment claim is rejected on the covered record if its preregistered global decision rule establishes any of the following:

1. equivalence or inferiority of the Golay-derived $[[23,1,7]]$ implementation to every registered distance-matched comparator;

6. a stable structured weight-$8$ excess not supported on the $759$ Golay octads;
7. failure of the octad-conditioned Steiner incidence sequence $253:77:21:5:1$;
8. failure of the $1771$-class, six-tetrad law under controlled weight-$4$ decoder-boundary probes;
9. a stable $M_{24}$-invariant native residual shell at weight $1\le w\le7$; or
10. on a registered octad-stiffness implementation, failure of the unique uniform zero mode or splitting of the centered eigenvalue beyond the certified anisotropy and calibration error.

No universal numerical factor such as $1.1$ or $1.2$ follows from code classification; any such threshold must be supplied prospectively by the independent substrate-response certificate.
